The delivered scope is the pure W3-IR control-flow analysis over the op graph + visited map + addrlist stack — `target`/`branchTarget`/`findRelTarget`/`fallthruOp`/`newAddress`/`updateTarget`, the `xrefControlFlow` classification with the `maxtime`/`deleteRemainingOps` relative-branch trimming, `fallthru`/`setFallthruBound`, `artificialHalt`/`reinterpreted`/`handleOutOfBounds`, `findUnprocessed`/`dedupUnprocessed`/`fillinBranchStubs`, and the `collectEdges`/`splitBasic`/`connectBasic` block build — all transcribed statement-for-statement (the op-creation + SeqNum-allocation order is load-bearing and preserved) and adversarially verified. The deferred methods become load-bearing only once their subsystem wires a first consumer: a function that decodes a real instruction needing an output varnode or a code-ref input hits the `FlowEmit::dump` seam before any deferred-method body runs (pinned by the strand-2 real-Sleigh tests, which assert the seam error is precise rather than silently wrong); a BRANCHIND/injected-CALLOTHER reaches `recover_jump_tables_stub`/`inject_pcode`; a no-return callee reaches the `setupCallSpecs`-true halt path — none reachable through the W3 surface alone.
- why: the C++ bodies dereference the W4 `FuncCallSpecs`/`JumpTable`/`PcodeInjectLibrary`/`Override`/`ActionDatabase` + `Funcdata::inlineFlow`/`cloneOp`, the funcdata_op `opDestroyRaw`/`opDeadInsertAfter` op-graph API, the `(vbank,obank)` `banks_mut` split accessor + `newCodeRef` (the LOSS-035/036 seam owner's surface), and the W4 comment/warning store — none of which exist at this item's boundary. Porting any now would mean inventing those subsystems ahead of their own checklist items with no consumer to validate against (the same accountability situation LOSS-030/033/034/035/036 record). This entry closes the ledger gap: the methods live in flow.cc, which IS w3-ir-flow's verified module scope, so the deferral must be visible even though every consumer is a later item. Recorded by the w3-ir-flow verifier (round 1).
- restoration criteria: each method ports as its subsystem lands — the call-spec family (a) + reporting (e) with W4 FuncCallSpecs/Override (`setupCallSpecs`/`setupCallindSpecs`/`checkForFlowModification`/`queryCall`/`checkContainedCall`/`deleteCallSpec`, then the `warning*`/`overrideFlow`/`removeUnreachableBlocks` text); the jumptable family (b) with W4 JumpTable (the `generateOps` recovery loop + `recoverJumpTables`/`truncateIndirectJump`/`checkMultistageJumptables`); the injection + inline-clone family (c) with W4 PcodeInjectLibrary + `Funcdata::inlineFlow`/`cloneOp`; the emitter output/coderef + `opDestroyRaw` (d) when funcdata adds `banks_mut`/`opSetOutput` and `Varnode::set_flags` exposes `newCodeRef` (the LOSS-035/036 restoration) — at which point `FlowEmit::dump`, `deleteRemainingOps`, and the SPARC-trap destroy complete and `process_instruction` stops surfacing the emitter-deferred error. Each is pinned against its C++ body and the relevant sliver checked off in that item's review.

## LOSS-038: jumptable.cc model-recovery seams (CircleRange/EmulateFunction/JumpBasic family) deferred at w3-ir-jumptable
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: deferral (cross-wave seam; covered by `// SEAM(W4/W5/W6)` markers + precise `KunaError::lowlevel` shells, ledgered per the LOSS-023/035/036/037 precedent — in-scope methods whose only consumers are later waves must still be ledgered)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/jumptable.{cc,hh} — the methods that dereference a subsystem absent at the W3 IR boundary: (a) **W5 CircleRange / rangeutil value-set domain** — `CircleRange::contains`/`getNext`/`intersect`/`pullBack` (the placeholder in jumptable.rs carries only the constructor/`getMin`/`getStep`/`getMask`/`getSize`/`setRange` surface the ported code touches; `getSize` is an approximation `(right-left)&mask/step`), and everything built on them: `JumpBasic::calcRange`/`analyzeGuards`/`checkUnrolledGuard`/`markFoldableGuards`/`findNormalized`/`findSmallestNormal`; (b) **W4 EmulateFunction/EmulatePcodeOp** (the LOSS-023 surface) — `EmulateFunction::execute*`/`emulatePath`, consumed by `JumpBasic::buildAddresses`/`buildLabels` and `JumpBasicOverride::trialNorm`/`findLikelyNorm`; (c) **W4 loader / MemoryImage** — `JumpBasic::backup2Switch` (LoadImage reads via `glb->loader`), `JumpModel::sanityCheck` LOAD-table truncation; (d) **W6 TypeOp reverse-eval** — `backup2Switch`'s `top->recoverInputBinary`/`recoverInputUnary`; (e) **W4/W5/W7 model instances** — the entire `JumpBasic`/`JumpBasic2`/`JumpBasicOverride`/`JumpAssisted` `recoverModel`/`findUnnormalized`/`foldInGuards`/`foldInOneGuard`/`foldInNormalization` bodies, plus `JumpTable::recoverModel`/`recoverAddresses`/`recoverMultistage`/`matchModel`/`setOverride`/`checkForMultistage` and the `JumpBasicOverride` encode/decode + `<basicoverride>` decode branch — all need the above + `DynamicHash`, `BlockBasic::findMultiequal`/`liftVerifyUnroll`/`noInterveningStatement`, `FlowBlock::getImmedDom`, `Funcdata::pushBranch`, the `Override`/restart machinery, and the arch fields `switch_modulo_bound`/`funcptr_align`/`max_jumptable_size`; (f) **W4 reporting** — every `fd->warning(...)` call site (sanityCheck truncation, recoverMultistage second-stage error, matchModel override/mismatch). Also: `JumptableThunkError` (jumptable.hh:42), a `LowlevelError` subclass, is collapsed to a plain `KunaError::lowlevel("Likely thunk")` — faithful at the only in-file catch site (`recoverMultistage` catches both identically) but the subtype distinction is dropped for any future caller that would discriminate it.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/jumptable.rs — the deferred surfaces are NOT silently mis-ported: `JumpBasic` is a unit struct exposing ONLY its pure static helpers (`isprune`/`ispoint`/`getStride`/`getMaxValue`/`duplicateVarnodes`/`findDeterminingVarnodes`), with no `impl JumpModel for JumpBasic` claiming a wrong body; the only concrete `impl JumpModel` is `JumpModelTrivial` (fully ported). Each absent surface returns a precise `KunaError::lowlevel("… deferred to W4/W5/W6 …")` (`CircleRange::contains`/`getNext`/`intersect`/`pullBack` :200-234; `JumpTable::recover_model` :2266, `recover_addresses` :2280, `set_override` :2320, `check_for_multistage` :2399; the `<basicoverride>` decode branch :2578) or is a documented `// SEAM(W4)` no-op preserving the C++ call cadence (the `fd->warning` sites in `sanity_check`/`recover_multistage`/`match_model`). `CircleRange` is a local placeholder (jumptable.rs:113) carrying only the fields the ported code constructs.
- surface: none at the w3-ir-jumptable gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` green; 25 in-module + 9 verifier tests). The delivered scope is the structurally-complete, IR-surface-portable machinery: `LoadTable` (incl. `collapseTable` two-iterator compaction + encode/decode), `PathMeld` (set/append/meld/meldOps/internalIntersect/truncatePaths/markPaths/getEarliestOp/isLoadInPath — the SeqNum-order merge and the marked-varnode intersection are load-bearing and preserved), the `GuardRecord` static logic (`oneOffMatch`/`quasiCopy`/`valueMatch`), the `JumpValues` iterator family, `JumpModelTrivial`, the `JumpBasic` static helpers, and the `JumpTable` bookkeeping driver (`IndexPair` order, `switchOver`/`trivialSwitchOver`/`block2Position`/`numIndicesByBlock`/`getIndexByBlock`/`isReachable`, the `sanityCheck` reachability/thunk control logic, `addBlockToSwitch`, `foldInNormalization`/`foldInGuards` drivers, encode/decode) — all transcribed and adversarially verified. Every deferred method's only consumers are later waves; no ported method consumes a deferred one's non-trivial result (the seam Errs short-circuit before any output is produced).
- why: the C++ bodies dereference the W5 `CircleRange` value-set domain, the W4 `EmulateFunction`/loader/`Override`/arch fields, the W6 `TypeOp` reverse-eval, and the W7 structuring/`DynamicHash`/block-SSA helpers — none of which exist at this item's boundary. Porting them now would require inventing those subsystems ahead of their own checklist items with no consumer to validate against (the LOSS-023/035/036/037 accountability situation). This entry closes the ledger gap: the methods live in jumptable.cc/jumptable.hh, which IS w3-ir-jumptable's verified module scope, so the deferral must be visible even though every consumer is a later item. Recorded by the w3-ir-jumptable verifier (round 1).
- restoration criteria: the CircleRange value-set ops (a) restore with W5 rangeutil (then `getSize` swaps its approximation for the real normalization and `calcRange`/`analyzeGuards`/`findNormalized` port); the emulation family (b) with W4 EmulateFunction (the LOSS-023 restoration, with `buildAddresses`/`buildLabels`/`emulatePath` as consumers); the loader reads (c) + arch fields with W4 Architecture; `backup2Switch` (d) with W6 TypeOp; the model instances (e) as each of CircleRange/EmulateFunction/loader/DynamicHash/structuring lands (`JumpBasic` first, then `JumpBasic2`/`JumpBasicOverride`/`JumpAssisted`, then the `JumpTable::recoverModel`/`recoverAddresses`/`matchModel`/`setOverride`/`checkForMultistage` driver and the `<basicoverride>` decode); the warning sinks (f) with the W4 comment/warning store; and `JumptableThunkError` re-introduced as a distinct error variant if/when a caller discriminates it. Each is pinned against its C++ body and the relevant sliver checked off in that item's review.

## LOSS-039: JumpValuesRange::truncate computes step*nm in 64 bits, not C++ int32
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (integer width / overflow)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/jumptable.cc:268 — `uintb right = (left + step * nm) & range.getMask();` where `step` (`int4`) and `nm` (`int4`) make `step * nm` a 32-bit `int` multiply; the (possibly overflowed/wrapped) `int` result is then sign-extended into the `uintb` (u64) addition.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/jumptable.rs:1106 — `let right = left.wadd((step as uintb).wmul(nm as uintb)) & self.range.get_mask();`. `step as uintb`/`nm as uintb` sign-extend each operand to u64 first, so the product is computed as a full 64-bit `wrapping_mul`. For `step * nm < 2^31` the two agree; for a product that overflows int32 they diverge (the C++ wraps mod 2^32 and sign-extends; Rust wraps mod 2^64). Concrete trace: `step=0x10000, nm=0x10000` over a 48-bit mask → C++ `(int32)(step*nm)=0` ⇒ empty range (`getSize`=0); Rust `0x1_0000_0000` ⇒ `getSize`=0x10000. Pinned by `verify_w3_ir_jumptable.rs::truncate_step_times_nm_overflows_int32_diverges_from_cpp`.
- surface: none end-to-end at the w3-ir-jumptable gate today. `truncate`/`getSize` feed `JumpModel::getTableSize` and the emulation iteration, but every consumer (`JumpBasic::recoverModel`/`buildAddresses`, `emulatePath`) is a W4/W5 seam (LOSS-038) that returns `Err` before any address table is built, so the divergent value cannot reach decompiler output in the current port. It is reachable via the public `JumpValuesRange` API (and so observable in tests), and would become output-affecting once the W4 emulation consumer lands. The triggering magnitude (`step*nm ≥ 2^31`, e.g. a ≥0x10000-stride table with ≥0x10000 entries) is non-physical for real switch tables; the common small-product case is faithful (`truncate_small_product_matches_cpp_int32`).
- why: ADR 0003 maps `int4→i32`/`uintb→u64` and makes C++'s implicit promotions explicit; the port widened both operands to u64 before multiplying instead of reproducing the C++ 32-bit `int` multiply-then-extend. The faithful form is `let right = left.wadd((step.wrapping_mul(nm)) as i64 as uintb) & mask;` (multiply as `i32`, then sign-extend the i32 result to u64). Left as a divergence rather than a blocker because it has no current end-to-end effect (the consumers are LOSS-038 seams) and the triggering input is non-physical.
- restoration criteria: replace the body with the 32-bit-multiply-then-sign-extend form above; keep `truncate_step_times_nm_overflows_int32_diverges_from_cpp` and flip its `assert_ne!` to `assert_eq!` against the C++ oracle (oracle value 0). The fix should land no later than the W4 emulation consumer (LOSS-038 (b)) wiring, since that is when the value first reaches output. Verified by the w3-ir-jumptable verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-040: options integer parse wrap-truncates instead of saturating to numeric_limits<T>::max() (w4-fw-options REJECT F1)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (integer width / overflow) — **tracked under a REJECT verdict**, not an accepted loss; recorded so the divergence is visible to the next attempt. The item stays in `todo` (attempts incremented). A future attempt either fixes it (closing this loss) or, only if the unbounded-magnitude case is judged out of contract, converts to ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES citing this id — but the verifier's position is that the `maxinstruction`/`jumptablemax` accept→error flip is in-contract and must be fixed.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/options.cc:991 (`s1 >> newMax` for `maxinstruction`), :883 (`s >> val` for `jumptablemax`), :267 (`s1 >> expop` for `extrapop`), :521/:538/:556 (`maxlinewidth`/`indentincrement`/`commentindent`). Each is `istringstream` with `unsetf(dec|hex|oct)` extracting an `int4`/`uint4`; on a value outside the target type's range C++11 `num_get` sets failbit and stores `numeric_limits<T>::max()` (or `min()` for a negative int) — verified with a standalone harness: `"3000000000">>int = 2147483647`, `"0x100000000">>unsigned = 4294967295`, `"0xffffffff">>int = 2147483647`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/options.rs:434-468 (`impl IntParse for int4`/`uint4`: `from_u64` does `mag as i64 ... as int4` / `mag as uint4`, a wrapping narrow with NO width clamp; `saturate(overflow, neg)` only fires on the u64 accumulator `overflow` flag set in `parse_int_auto` at :410-416). A value that fits in u64 but overflows the i32/u32 target therefore has `overflow == false` and is wrap-truncated: `3000000000 as i32 = -1294967296`, `0x100000000 as u32 = 0`, `0xffffffff as i32 = -1`.
- surface: observable end-to-end through the public option-command API (console `option <name>` and the `<optionslist>` cspec/pspec decode). On a string in `(i32::MAX, u64::MAX]` (int4 options) or `(u32::MAX, u64::MAX]` (uint4 options): `maxinstruction` and `jumptablemax` flip from C++ *accept* (store the saturated MAX) to port *error* (`Bad maxinstruction parameter` / `Must specify integer maximum`) because the wrapped value trips the `<0` / `==0` sentinel; `extrapop` stores a different value (`-1` vs INT_MAX); `maxlinewidth`/`indentincrement`/`commentindent` store a different value handed to the printer. Triggering magnitude is `> INT_MAX`/`> UINT_MAX` — pathological but legal for resource-limit knobs where a user types a huge number meaning "unlimited". Pinned by `rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w4_fw_options.rs::{maxinstruction_overflow_saturates_not_wraps, jumptablemax_width_overflow_saturates_not_wraps, extrapop_width_overflow_value_matches_cpp}` (currently FAILING — these are the REJECT evidence).
- why: the porter modeled the C++ stream overflow as "saturate to numeric_limits<T>::max()" (the module doc-comment says exactly that) but implemented the saturation against the u64 magnitude accumulator instead of the i32/u32 *target* width, so the saturation only triggers for inputs `≥ 2^64`. This is the same C++11 `num_get` ERANGE-stores-MAX semantics already acknowledged for the float-readback path in LOSS-025; LOSS-025 does NOT extend to this integer-options path.
- restoration criteria: in `IntParse::from_u64`/`saturate`, after applying the sign, clamp the result to `[T::MIN, T::MAX]` when the (sign-applied) magnitude is out of the target range — i.e. saturate on *target-width* overflow, not just u64-accumulator overflow — matching `istringstream >> T`. Then flip the three failing `verify_w4_fw_options` tests to assert the C++ oracle values (INT_MAX / UINT_MAX / INT_MAX) and re-run the w4-fw-options gate green. Verified by the w4-fw-options verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-041: cpool container decode partial-state / stream-position divergence on malformed or duplicate <cpoolrec> (w4-fw-cpool-graph F1)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (exception->Result partial-state parity) — accepted under ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES; behind a fatal decode error with no decompiler recovery point.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/cpool.cc:169-175 (`ConstantPool::decodeRecord`) calls `createRecord(refs)` FIRST (which inserts a blank record into `cpoolMap` and, on a duplicate reference, throws via cpool.cc:199-208 *before* reading the `<cpoolrec>` body), THEN `newrec->decode(decoder,typegrp)` decodes in place. So a mid-`decode` throw leaves a blank/partially-decoded record in the map, and a duplicate-ref throw leaves the decoder cursor at the start of the duplicate `<cpoolrec>` (body unconsumed). Container `decode` (cpool.cc:233-246) drives this per element.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/cpool.rs:532-545 (`ConstantPool::decode_record` default method) decodes the body into a temporary `CPoolRecord` FIRST (`newrec.decode(decoder, typegrp)?`), THEN `create_record(refs)` inserts/duplicate-checks and `*slot = newrec`. So a mid-decode throw leaves `cpool_map` unchanged, and a duplicate-ref error fires only *after* the body has been consumed from the decoder.
- surface: none on the happy path — for well-formed, non-duplicate input the rebuilt pool and the decoder cursor are byte-identical (verified by `cpool_container_lexicographic_after_roundtrip` and the in-module round-trip tests). The divergence is confined to the error path: residual map entry (C++ keeps a partial record; Rust keeps none) and decoder position on a duplicate ref (C++ stops before the dup body; Rust stops after it). Both implementations abort the enclosing `decode` with an error; a partially-built constant pool on a fatal XML decode failure is not a state the decompiler reads back or recovers from. Error message on a duplicate is equivalent (both name the existing token via `createRecord`). Reachable only with hand-authored/corrupt `<constantpool>` XML.
- why: the C++ `createRecord`-then-`decode-in-place` order is expressible in Rust (the record `&mut` and the `decoder`/`typegrp` borrows are disjoint), but the porter chose to decode into a temporary first and insert after — a defensible shape that keeps the duplicate-check at the natural `create_record` site, at the cost of the error-path partial-state/cursor divergence above. Faithful on every valid input the encoder can produce.
- restoration criteria: reorder `decode_record` to `create_record(refs)?` first (obtaining the `&mut CPoolRecord` slot), then `slot.decode(decoder, typegrp)?` in place — matching C++ exactly, so a mid-decode throw leaves the (blank/partial) record in the map and a duplicate-ref throw precedes any body consumption. Add an error-path test asserting the residual-entry / cursor behavior against the C++ order. Verified by the w4-fw-cpool-graph verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-042: ActionPool breakpoint-resume cursor (key-based OpCursor) re-applies the broken op's rules when an op is inserted below an Unstarted cursor (w4-fw-action ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (iteration-order / live-iterator vs key-cursor) — accepted; debug-breakpoint-only, cannot reach the non-breakpoint decompiler path.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/action.cc:822-888 — `ActionPool::op_state` is a live `PcodeOpTree::const_iterator`. On a rule action breakpoint, `processOp` returns -1 (action.cc:851-852) WITHOUT executing the trailing `op_state++` (:871), so the iterator still points at the broken op; `apply` (:884-885) resumes on `(*op_state).second` (the same op) at the preserved `rule_index`. A map iterator is stable across inserts, so an op inserted at a key below the current position never moves the cursor.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/action.rs:1038-1047 (`enum OpCursor`), :1174-1200 (`advance_op_state`/`current_op`), :1260-1275 (`ActionPool::apply`). The cursor is modelled as the last-consumed `SeqNum` (`After(sq)`), with `Unstarted` meaning "first optree entry". The break path leaves the cursor where it was. When the break happens on the FIRST op processed in a pass, the cursor is still `Unstarted`; on resume `current_op` recomputes "first optree entry", which — if a rule inserted an op with a SeqNum below the broken op — is now that newly-inserted op, not the broken op. The preserved `rule_index` is then consumed against the wrong (below-inserted) op, and the broken op is subsequently re-processed from `rule_index = 0`, re-applying its already-applied rules.
- surface: none on the normal decompilation path. `op_state` only persists across an `apply` re-entry when `perform` left `status == status_mid`, which only happens after a `-1` (breakpoint) return; breakpoints are set exclusively through the W9 console debug surface (`setBreakPoint`), absent from every batch decompilation. The faithful non-breakpoint path advances the cursor AFTER all rules on an op run (`advance_op_state` reads the op's still-live SeqNum then finds the next key), and is byte-identical to the C++ `op_state++` including across below-key inserts (verified: `pool_dispatches_each_op_through_every_rule_in_order`, `opcode_change_rezeros_rule_index`, `dead_op_is_skipped_and_destroyed`). The divergence requires (a) an active rule action breakpoint, (b) firing on the very first op of a pass, AND (c) a rule that inserts an op with a SeqNum strictly below that op before the break — a combination reachable only under interactive single-stepping. Pinned by `rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w4_fw_action.rs::w4_fw_action_cursor_jumps_to_op_inserted_below_after_break_documents_divergence` (asserts the Rust shape `["ins:CPUI_COPY","ins:CPUI_COPY","ins:CPUI_INT_ADD"]`; the faithful C++ would emit a single `ins:CPUI_COPY`).
- why: Rust cannot hold a live `BTreeMap` iterator across the mutation that rule application performs, so the porter modelled the cursor as a key (documented in the action.rs module header). The key-cursor is provably equivalent for the common cases the module doc enumerates (advance-after-run, `opDeadAndGone` erase of the current op), but the `Unstarted`-resume-with-below-insert case is the one place a key cannot reproduce a stable iterator. Left a divergence rather than a blocker because it is confined to the debug-breakpoint resume path and never affects produced decompilation.
- restoration criteria: make the breakpoint-resume cursor remember the broken op's identity directly (e.g. an `OpCursor::At(SeqNum)` resume variant set on the `-1` return that points current_op at exactly that op rather than recomputing the first/next entry), so a resume re-processes the broken op itself regardless of below-key inserts. Then flip the pinned test to the C++ single-`ins:CPUI_COPY` shape. Should land with the W9 console / interactive single-stepping wiring, which is the first consumer that can observe it. Verified by the w4-fw-action verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-043: ActionRestartGroup restart drops the comment-DB warning clear (clearAnalysis only does fd->clear) (w4-fw-action)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: deferral (cross-wave seam; `// SEAM(W4)` marked) — accepted, ledgered per the LOSS-035/036/037 precedent (the comment/warning subsystem is not ported at this item's boundary).
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/action.cc:574 `data.getArch()->clearAnalysis(&data);` inside `ActionRestartGroup::apply`. `Architecture::clearAnalysis` (architecture.cc:336-342) is `fd->clear()` PLUS `commentdb->clearType(fd->getAddress(), Comment::warning|Comment::warningheader)` — clearing analysis-generated warning comments before the restart re-runs the group.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/action.rs:836-838 — the restart calls `data.clear()` only, with a `// SEAM(W4)` note that the comment-DB clear is deferred. The `warningHeader`/`issueWarning` text the C++ would route to the comment DB is instead collected in the engine-owned `WarningSink` (action.rs:318-338) and never cleared on restart.
- surface: none on produced decompilation today — the comment database does not exist in the current port, so there is nothing to clear; warnings accumulate in the `WarningSink` rather than the per-function comment store. Once the W4 comment subsystem lands, a restart must additionally clear `warning|warningheader` comments for the function address, otherwise stale warning comments from a pre-restart pass would survive into the final output. Confined to the restart path (`hasRestartPending` true), itself driven by restart-requesting actions that are W4+ rules.
- why: the comment DB and `Architecture::clearAnalysis` are W4 subsystems absent at this item's boundary; porting them now would invent a subsystem ahead of its checklist item with no consumer to validate. The `fd->clear()` half — the load-bearing op/varnode reset that makes the restart re-derive correctly — IS ported and tested (`restart_group_reruns_on_restart_pending`, `restart_group_honors_maxrestarts`).
- restoration criteria: when the W4 comment subsystem lands, extend the restart body to also clear `warning|warningheader` comments at `data.get_address()` (and drain/clear the corresponding `WarningSink` entries), matching `Architecture::clearAnalysis`. Add a restart test asserting pre-restart warnings do not survive. Verified by the w4-fw-action verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-044: ActionDatabase::deriveAction substitutes an empty no-op ActionGroup for a fully-filtered (NULL) root (w4-fw-action)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (null-vs-empty substitution) — accepted; more-robust substitution with no in-scope consumer that can observe the difference.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/action.cc:1145-1160 `ActionDatabase::deriveAction` — `Action *newact = act->clone(curgrp);` returns `(Action *)0` when the grouplist filters every component out (clone returns NULL), and `registerAction(grp, newact)` stores the NULL; `getCurrent()` (action.hh:313) then returns NULL.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/action.rs:1502-1518 `derive_action` — `newact.unwrap_or_else(|| Box::new(ActionGroup::new(0, grp.to_string())))` substitutes an empty `ActionGroup` when the filtered clone yields `None`, so `get_current()` returns `Some(empty group)` and running it is a defined no-op. The raw filtered-clone result is still exposed faithfully via `derive_fresh` (returns `Option`, `None` == C++ NULL).
- surface: none in scope. Within action.{cc,hh} the only consumer of a derived root is `getCurrent()`; no in-scope path executes a NULL root. The W-later `Funcdata` driver that would `getCurrent()->perform(...)` either guards NULL or is configured with non-empty groups (every real root — decompile/jumptable/normalize/paramid/register/firstpass — contains "base"). The Rust substitution turns a would-be NULL-deref into a benign empty-group no-op; it can only diverge if a caller explicitly tests `getCurrent() == NULL` to detect an empty root, which no in-scope caller does. Pinned by `rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w4_fw_action.rs::w4_fw_action_empty_grouplist_derives_a_noop_root_not_a_crash` (and the porter's `derive_fresh` returns `None` for the empty case).
- why: a `Box<dyn Action>` map cannot hold a typed NULL; the porter chose a total map (empty group) over `Option<Box<dyn Action>>` to keep `get_current` infallible, documenting the choice in `derive_action`. The faithful-distinguishable form (`Option` in the cache) is available through `derive_fresh`.
- restoration criteria: if a later wave's driver must distinguish "empty root" from "populated root", change `actionmap` to `BTreeMap<String, Option<Box<dyn Action>>>` (or have `get_current` return `Option` of the cached entry) so a fully-filtered derive yields `None` exactly as C++ returns NULL. Verified by the w4-fw-action verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-040 CLOSURE (w4-fw-options round 2)
- date: 2026-06-11
- status: **RESOLVED**. The w4-fw-options attempt-2 repair (`parse_int_auto` reworked to saturate on *target-width* overflow via `IntParse::max_magnitude(neg)` + `saturate(overflow || width_overflow, neg)`, rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/options.rs:432-503) exactly meets LOSS-040's restoration criteria. Verified faithful against a C++11 `istringstream >> int/unsigned` oracle across the full edge set: `"3000000000">>int=INT_MAX`, `"0x100000000">>uint=UINT_MAX`, `"0xffffffff">>int=INT_MAX`, `"-2147483648">>int=INT_MIN (no fail)`, `"-2147483649">>int=INT_MIN (fail)`, `"-4294967295">>uint=1 (no fail)`, `"-0x100000000">>uint=UINT_MAX (fail)`, `">2^64">>int=INT_MAX`. The three formerly-failing tests now assert the oracle MAX values and pass; added boundary guards `extrapop_int_min_boundary_exact_then_saturates` / `jumptablemax_unsigned_negate_modulo_vs_overflow` / `above_u64_still_saturates_after_rework` pass. The integer-WIDTH divergence is closed.
- note: this does NOT clear w4-fw-options for ACCEPT — a *distinct* num_get divergence (the failbit-with-zero-on-non-numeric-input case) was found at round 2 and recorded as LOSS-045 below, which keeps the item at REJECT.

## LOSS-045: options integer parse returns the caller sentinel (not 0) on a failed extraction of non-empty/non-numeric input — C++11 num_get stores 0 (w4-fw-options round-2 REJECT F3)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (integer parse / control-flow) — **tracked under a REJECT verdict**, not an accepted loss; recorded so the divergence is visible to the next attempt. Same hunt-list category (integer parse semantics) as the now-closed LOSS-040, on a different num_get corner.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/options.cc:520-521 (`int4 val=-1; s>>val` for `maxlinewidth`), :537-538 (`indentincrement`), :555-556 (`commentindent`), :988-991 (`int4 newMax=-1; s1>>newMax` for `maxinstruction`), :265-267 (`int4 expop=-300; s1>>expop` for `extrapop`). C++11 [facet.num.get.virtuals]: on a failed extraction the facet sets failbit and **stores 0 when at least one non-whitespace character was reached** (e.g. `"abc"`, `"  abc"`, a lone `"+"`/`"-"`, `"-x"`); it leaves the target **unchanged** only when the input was empty or whitespace-only (`""`, `"   "`, `"\t"`). Verified with g++ and clang++ -std=c++11: `int val=-1; iss("abc")>>val` => `val=0 fail=1`; `iss("")>>val` => `val=-1 fail=1`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/options.rs:422-425 (`if !any { return None; }`) — `parse_int_auto` returns `None` whenever no digit was extracted, conflating the whitespace-only case (sentinel survives, correct) with the non-empty-non-numeric case (C++ stores 0). Every caller then does `.unwrap_or(SENTINEL)`, so the sentinel survives and the option ERRORS where C++ would store 0 and ACCEPT. (The module doc-comment at :360-362 states the wrong rule: "C++11: a failed extraction leaves the target variable **unchanged**" — true only for empty/whitespace-only input.)
- surface: observable end-to-end through the public option API (console `option <name>` and `<optionslist>` cspec/pspec decode). On non-empty input with no leading digit (after optional whitespace): `maxlinewidth "abc"` / `maxinstruction "abc"` / `extrapop "abc"` flip from C++ **accept** (store 0; `0 != -1`, `0 >= 0`, `0 != -300`) to port **error** (`Must specify integer linewidth` / `Bad maxinstruction parameter` / `Bad extrapop adjustment parameter`). `indentincrement`/`commentindent` likewise. `jumptablemax "abc"` is NOT affected (C++ stores 0, `val==0` true → ParseError; the port also errors). Pinned by `rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w4_fw_options.rs::{maxlinewidth_nonnumeric_input_accepts_as_zero_like_cpp, maxinstruction_nonnumeric_input_accepts_as_zero_like_cpp, extrapop_nonnumeric_input_accepts_as_zero_like_cpp}` (currently FAILING — REJECT evidence); `maxlinewidth_empty_input_errors_in_both` confirms the empty-input case is faithful (isolates the divergence to non-empty input).
- why: the port modeled C++11 num_get as "failed extraction leaves the target unchanged", which is only half the rule. The faithful model distinguishes by whether a non-whitespace character was reached: after the leading-whitespace skip, if `i >= bytes.len()` (nothing but whitespace) return `None` (sentinel survives); otherwise, if no digit was extracted, return `Some(T::from_u64(0,false))` (the failbit-with-zero store). This is a small, mechanical change confined to `parse_int_auto`'s `!any` branch — the digit-loop, base detection, and the (now-correct) width saturation are unaffected.
- restoration criteria: in `parse_int_auto`, replace the unconditional `if !any { return None; }` with: return `None` only when the post-whitespace cursor is at end of input (empty/whitespace-only); when input remained but produced no digits, return `Some(T::from_u64(0, false))`. Then flip the three failing `verify_w4_fw_options` non-numeric tests to assert acceptance with stored value 0, keep `maxlinewidth_empty_input_errors_in_both`, and re-run the gate green. Verified by the w4-fw-options verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-046: addMap defers join-address piece expansion (per-piece precislo/precishi SymbolEntry maps) to W5 (w4-p0-database)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: deferral
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/database.cc:1161-1183 `Scope::addMap` — when `entry.addr.isJoin()`, the C++ looks up `glb->findJoin(entry.addr.getOffset())` (a `JoinRecord`) and adds an extra `addMapInternal` SymbolEntry for each piece, in endian order, tagging `precishi` (i==0, most significant), `precislo` (i==num-1), or both (middle pieces), with running byte offset `off`. The unified entry is still returned (the loop falls through).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/database.rs:1676-1740 `Database::add_map` — the join arm is a documented `SEAM(W5)`; a join address is mapped as a single entry and the per-piece sub-entries are NOT created (the `JoinRecord` table lives in the W5 architecture/translate subsystem, unavailable at this item's boundary).
- surface: a Symbol stored at a join address (a logically-contiguous value physically split across registers, e.g. a 64-bit return value in two 32-bit registers on a 32-bit ABI) gets one SymbolEntry instead of one-per-piece. `find_container`/`find_overlap`/`find_addr` over the individual physical pieces will not resolve to this Symbol until the pieces are mapped. No in-scope test exercises a join address (the seam has no `JoinRecord` source); the unified-symbol path is unaffected and tested.
- why: `Address::isJoin()` and `Architecture::findJoin()`/`JoinRecord` are part of the W5 translate/architecture subsystem; porting them here would invent a subsystem ahead of its checklist item with no consumer to validate. The persistence/addrtied/property logic and the single-entry map ARE ported and tested.
- restoration criteria: when W5 supplies the `JoinRecord` table (and `Address::isJoin`/`isBigEndian`), extend `add_map`'s mapped arm to iterate the join pieces in endian order, calling `add_map_internal` with the running offset and `precislo`/`precishi`/both extraflags per the C++ index logic, while still returning the unified entry. Add a join-address mapping test (pieces resolvable via `find_container`). Verified by the w4-p0-database verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-045 CLOSURE (w4-fw-options round 3)
- date: 2026-06-11
- status: **RESOLVED**. The w4-fw-options attempt-3 repair (`f8c688f`) reworked `parse_int_auto`'s `!any` branch exactly to LOSS-045's restoration criteria: an `empty_field` flag is captured right after the leading-whitespace skip (`i >= bytes.len()`), and the no-digit-extracted branch now returns `None` only when `empty_field` (empty/whitespace-only -> sentinel survives -> reject) and `Some(T::from_u64(0,false))` otherwise (non-empty no-leading-digit -> failbit-with-zero -> accept). rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/options.rs:400, :441-452. Independently re-verified against a g++ 11 + clang++ -std=c++11 `istringstream >> int/unsigned` oracle: `iss("abc")>>int=0 fail`, `iss("-")>>int=0 fail`, `iss("  abc")>>int=0 fail`, `iss("0xZ")>>int=0 fail`, `iss(".5")>>int=0 fail`, `iss("+")>>int=0 fail`, while `iss("")>>int` / `iss("   ")>>int` leave -1 (unchanged). The three round-2 REJECT-evidence tests (`{maxlinewidth,maxinstruction,extrapop}_nonnumeric_input_accepts_as_zero_like_cpp`) now PASS, and `maxlinewidth_empty_input_errors_in_both` confirms the empty case is still faithful. The extrapop sentinel interplay (`-300`/`""`/`"0"`/`"abc"`) and the octal/hex base auto-detection are pinned by the round-3 adversarial tests and match the oracle. The failbit-with-zero divergence is closed.

## LOSS-047: OptionSplitDatatypes::apply leaves split_datatype_config at the prior value (not bit(p1)) when a later parameter (p2/p3) is malformed — C++ writes the field incrementally and leaves bit(p1) behind (w4-fw-options ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES F2)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (exception -> Result partial-state) — accepted minor loss.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/options.cc:1046-1049 `OptionSplitDatatypes::apply`: `glb->split_datatype_config = getOptionBit(p1); glb->split_datatype_config |= getOptionBit(p2); glb->split_datatype_config |= getOptionBit(p3);`. `getOptionBit` (:1026-1035) throws `LowlevelError("Unknown data-type split option: "+val)` on an unknown option string. Because the field is written *before* each subsequent `getOptionBit` runs, a malformed p2 (or p3) leaves `glb->split_datatype_config` at `getOptionBit(p1)` (and p3 failure leaves it at `bit(p1)|bit(p2)`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/options.rs:1768-1772 — `let old_config = glb.split_datatype_config(); let mut config = Self::get_option_bit(p1)?; config |= Self::get_option_bit(p2)?; config |= Self::get_option_bit(p3)?; glb.set_split_datatype_config(config);`. The accumulation is into a *local*; `glb` is written only after all three `?`-propagating parses succeed, so a malformed p2/p3 leaves `glb`'s `split_datatype_config` at `old_config` (whatever it was), not at `bit(p1)`.
- surface: observable only on the error path of a malformed `splitdatatype` command (`option splitdatatype struct garbage` or the `<optionslist>` decode equivalent). The thrown error text is identical (`Unknown data-type split option: garbage`); only the surviving `split_datatype_config` field differs after the failed command: C++ = `option_struct` (1), port = the prior value (0 by default). Narrow trigger (a malformed multi-parameter splitdatatype is the only path), and any subsequent valid `splitdatatype` command overwrites the field unconditionally, so it cannot independently change a successful decompilation. No in-scope datatest exercises a malformed multi-param splitdatatype. Pinned by `rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w4_fw_options.rs::splitdatatype_bad_p2_partial_state_divergence_documented` (currently green, asserting the divergent port value 0; flip to `option_struct` when fixed).
- why: the port chose a local-accumulate-then-write shape (idiomatic, and faithful for the success path and for the `oldConfig == config` "unchanged" message), but that shape does not reproduce the C++ incremental write that leaves a partial value on the error path. The three other multi-write option bodies (`OptionAliasBlock`, `OptionNanIgnore`, the comment-toggle options) write the field only after the fallible parse — for those, C++ ALSO leaves the field unchanged on error, so the local-then-write shape is faithful; `OptionSplitDatatypes` is the lone upstream body that writes incrementally before a possible throw.
- restoration criteria: in `OptionSplitDatatypes::apply`, write `split_datatype_config` incrementally to mirror C++ — `glb.set_split_datatype_config(Self::get_option_bit(p1)?)`, then read-or-back `|= get_option_bit(p2)?`, then `|= get_option_bit(p3)?` — so a malformed p2/p3 leaves `glb` at `bit(p1)` (resp. `bit(p1)|bit(p2)`). Then flip `splitdatatype_bad_p2_partial_state_divergence_documented` to assert the surviving value is `option_struct` and re-run the w4-fw-options gate green. Verified by the w4-fw-options verifier (round 3).

## LOSS-048: the C++ `Override` class lives in a module named `overrides` (not `override`) because `override` is a Rust reserved keyword — the type name is preserved, only the module path is renamed (w4-p0-override-comment ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: naming divergence (forced source-level rename; zero behavioral impact) — accepted minor loss.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/override.hh / override.cc — the class `ghidra::Override` is defined in a translation unit named `override.{cc,hh}`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/overrides.rs (module doc :1-5 "NAMING LOSS") — the module is `kuna_decomp::overrides`; the type is still named `Override`. The public path is therefore `kuna_decomp::overrides::Override` rather than a hypothetical `kuna_decomp::override::Override` (which is a hard syntax error in Rust: `override` is a reserved keyword and `r#override` as a *module path segment* is disallowed for crate/module names in stable Rust). No type, method, element-id, or wire-format name is affected — `Override`, `ELEM_OVERRIDE` (`<override>` id 223), the flow-type names, and the encode/decode element names are all byte-identical to upstream.
- surface: source-level only; the `<override>` XML element and every behavior are unchanged. The single observable difference is the Rust import path (`use kuna_decomp::overrides::Override`). No oracle/datatest is affected (the wire `<override>` element name is preserved by `ELEM_OVERRIDE`).
- why: `override` is a Rust 2015+ reserved keyword; a module cannot be named `override`. The minimal, name-preserving choice is to rename only the module to `overrides` and keep the type, constants, and all marshaling names verbatim.
- restoration criteria: none feasible while targeting stable Rust (the keyword reservation is permanent); the entry documents the forced rename for ledger completeness. If a future edition lifts the module-path restriction, rename the module back to `override` (the type and all wire names already match).

## LOSS-049: kassert's live store-mutation + console arg-parsing dispatch is W5-deferred — the Rust port carries only the routing DECISION, not the Override/FuncProto/symbol/option WRITE (w4-kuna-p0-pack ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: deferral (seam) — accepted; the console+store subsystem is a later wave.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/kuna_assert.cc:51-246 `IfcKunaAssert::execute` — the bottom two-thirds of the command parses `<args>` per sub-stage (`parse_machaddr`, `parse_type`, `dcp->readSymbol`, token-count checks) and MUTATES the live stores: `Override::insertForceGoto/insertDeadcodeDelay/insertMultistageJump/insertFlowOverride`, `FuncProto::setInputLock/setOutputLock`, `Scope::retypeSymbol`+`setAttribute(typelock)`, `Symbol::setIsolated`, `Scope::renameSymbol`+`setAttribute(namelock)`, and `OptionDatabase::set` via `applyOption`. It also throws `IfaceExecutionError("No function selected")` for the function-scoped arms when `dcp->fd == 0`, and emits the `kassert applied: [..] rewind->..` confirmation line.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/kuna_assert.rs:97-204 — `Dispatch::for_substage` reproduces the C++ `if/else` dispatch CHAIN as a pure decision (which store a sub-stage routes to, `Latent` vs `Unroutable` for the `else`), and `validate_assertion` ports the stage/sub-stage validation + strength resolution + minimal-rewind lookup (the console-independent half, up to but NOT including the store write). The actual store mutation, the per-sub-stage `<args>` parsing/arity checks, the `dcp->fd == 0` execution errors, and the `kassert applied:` confirmation line are explicitly marked `SEAM(W5)` in the module doc (:33) and are absent.
- surface: the `kassert <stage> <substage> <args> [hard|hint]` console command as a whole. The Rust library can validate a request and tell you WHERE it would route (`Dispatch`), and `AssertLog::render_list` reproduces `kassert list` byte-for-byte; but invoking `kassert` does not yet insert a force-goto, lock a prototype, retype/rename a symbol, set isolated, or flip an option — i.e. a kassert has no effect on a (not-yet-portable) decompilation. No oracle/datatest exercises the store-write path at this item's boundary (the Override/FuncProto/Scope/OptionDatabase stores and the `IfaceDecompData` console are W5+ surfaces); `kassert list` (the observable half this item owns) IS ported and tested.
- why: the dispatch targets (Override, FuncProto, Symbol scopes, OptionDatabase) and the console `IfaceDecompCommand`/`parse_machaddr`/`parse_type`/`readSymbol` plumbing do not exist in the Rust tree at w4; porting the writes here would invent those subsystems ahead of their checklist items with no consumer to validate. The port deliberately splits the decision (here, pinned by `dispatch_routes_each_store`/`minimal_rewind_matrix`) from the write (W5), mirroring the established seam pattern (LOSS-030..038).
- restoration criteria: when W5 lands the console command surface and the live stores, implement `IfcKunaAssert::execute` on top of `validate_assertion`+`Dispatch`: for each `Dispatch` variant perform the C++ store mutation with the same arity checks and `IfaceParseError`/`IfaceExecutionError` text and order, append the `KunaAssertion` to the session `AssertLog` only on success, and emit the `kassert applied:` line. Pin it against the C++ `decomp_dbg` `kassert`-then-`print C` behavior for at least the force-goto, prototype-lock, retype, and option arms. Verified by the w4-kuna-p0-pack verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-050: the base `Datatype::resolveInFlow`/`findResolve` "return this" identity is W6-deferred — the Rust port SEAMs them to `Err(SEAM(W6))` for every kind, where C++ returns the data-type unchanged for all non-union types (w5-dtype-expand ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: deferral (seam) — accepted; the union-resolution subsystem and the `Rc<Self>` handle it needs are a later wave (W6).
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/type.cc:578-581 `Datatype::resolveInFlow` (`{ return this; }`) and :590-593 `Datatype::findResolve` (`{ return this; }`). These base bodies return the receiver unchanged; only the `TypeUnion`/`TypePointer` (pointer-to-union) overrides do field resolution against the `PcodeOp`/slot. type.hh:292-293 declares both as `virtual`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/dtype.rs:1253 `Datatype::resolve_in_flow` and :1264 `Datatype::find_resolve` — both return `Err(KunaError::lowlevel("SEAM(W6) ..."))` unconditionally for every `DatatypeKind`, including the simple kinds whose C++ behavior is the trivial `return this`. The module doc and the method docs tag this `LOSS (F2)`.
- surface: `Datatype::resolveInFlow`/`findResolve` for a non-union, non-pointer-to-union data-type. C++ hands such a type straight back; the Rust port errors. This is a W8 seam surface — no W5/interface-stage caller reaches it (the type-propagation actions that call `resolveInFlow` are S5/W6+), so the divergence is not output-affecting at this item's boundary. Pinned (as the documented Err) by the verifier test `w5r2_resolve_in_flow_base_is_seamed_loss_f2` in `rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w5_dtype_expand.rs`.
- why: the faithful base "return this" needs an owning `Rc<Self>` handle to hand back (the method takes `&self`, so it cannot clone-and-return without a signature change or an `Rc` receiver), and the union/pointer-to-union overrides need the `Funcdata`/`PcodeOp` resolution machinery that does not exist until W6. Returning the trivial identity for the simple kinds while erroring for unions would be a half-implementation that still cannot be exercised at W5; the port keeps a single uniform seam.
- restoration criteria: in W6, when the type-resolution subsystem and an `Rc<Datatype>`-receiver shape exist, implement `resolveInFlow`/`findResolve`: for non-`needsResolution` kinds return the receiver `Rc` unchanged (the C++ `return this`); for `TypeUnion` / pointer-to-union do the `ResolvedUnion`/`ScoreUnionFields` field resolution against the `PcodeOp`/slot. Then convert `w5r2_resolve_in_flow_base_is_seamed_loss_f2` to assert the identity return for a plain int and the resolved sub-type for a union. Confirmed by the w5-dtype-expand verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-051: adjustvma's `parse_auto_base` returns 0 for any save-file `adjustvma` attribute with trailing non-digit garbage, where C++ `istringstream >> long` (and `strtoll(.,0,0)`) return the parsed PREFIX (w4-fw-arch-frontends ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES F1)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (integer parse — trailing-garbage truncation) — accepted minor loss.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/xml_arch.cc:124-128 (and the identical raw_arch.cc:105-109): `istringstream s( el->getAttributeValue("adjustvma")); s.unsetf(ios::dec | ios::hex | ios::oct); s >> adjustvma;` where `adjustvma` is `long`. With basefield cleared the integer `operator>>` does strtol-style auto-base extraction (0x/0X -> hex, leading 0 -> octal, else decimal) and STOPS at the first invalid character, leaving the parsed prefix in `adjustvma`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/xml_arch.rs:307-325 `parse_auto_base` (called by both `XmlArchitecture::restore_xml` :254 and `RawBinaryArchitecture::restore_xml` :239). It `trim()`s, splits an optional sign, then `i64::from_str_radix`/`parse::<i64>()` on the WHOLE remaining body, `unwrap_or(0)`. Because `from_str_radix`/`parse` require the entire string to be a valid integer, any trailing non-digit yields 0 instead of the prefix.
- divergence table (g++ -std=c++11 `istringstream + unsetf >> long` oracle vs port): `"5x"` cpp=5 port=0; `"0x1fZ"` cpp=31 port=0; `"0789"` cpp=7 (octal, stops at '8') port=0. Clean inputs all agree: `"4096"`->4096, `"0x1000"`->4096, `"010"`->8, `"-0x10"`->-16, `"+5"`->5, `""`->0, `"abc"`->0, `"  0x1f  "`->31 (leading/trailing whitespace is not "garbage" — `>>` skips it, `trim()` strips it).
- mislabel: the rust-anchor doc comment claims `parse_auto_base` matches `strtoll(s, 0, 0)`. It does NOT: `strtoll("5x",0,0)=5` while the port returns 0. The comment overstates the fidelity (the divergence is exactly the strtoll prefix behavior the comment names).
- surface: the `adjustvma` attribute of a `<xml_savefile>`/`<raw_savefile>` save-file restore. Real save files are produced by `encode` via `writeUnsignedInteger`, which emits pure decimal, so `adjustvma` is ALWAYS a clean integer in practice; the divergence manifests only for a hand-crafted or corrupted attribute with trailing non-digit text. No in-scope datatest / corpus_bootstrap fixture carries a garbage adjustvma (the corpus images use clean values). Output-affecting only on malformed input. Analogous in spirit to LOSS-045 (the same `>>` integer-extraction family on the options path).
- why: the port chose a trim-then-full-parse shape (idiomatic, exact for the clean save-file values that are the only real inputs) rather than transcribing the strtol scan-the-prefix-and-stop loop. The clean-input behavior is faithful; only trailing-garbage inputs diverge.
- restoration criteria: replace `parse_auto_base`'s full-string `from_str_radix`/`parse` with a leading-prefix scan that mirrors strtol/`istringstream >>`: after sign + base detection, consume the longest valid leading digit run for the detected base and return its value (0 if no leading digit), discarding any trailing characters. Then flip `adjustvma_trailing_garbage_diverges_from_cpp` in `rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w4_fw_arch_frontends.rs` to assert `"5x"`->5, `"0x1fZ"`->31, `"0789"`->7, and correct the doc comment. Verified by the w4-fw-arch-frontends verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-052: `XmlArchitectureCapability::isFileMatch` does not skip a leading vertical tab (0x0b) before the `<bi` probe, where C++ `std::ws` (classic-locale `isspace`) does — a `<binaryimage>` file beginning with `\v` matches in C++ but not in the port (w4-fw-arch-frontends ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES F2)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (whitespace-class mismatch) — accepted minor loss.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/xml_arch.cc:46-57 `XmlArchitectureCapability::isFileMatch`: `ifstream s(...); s >> ws; val1=s.get(); val2=s.get(); val3=s.get(); if ((val1=='<')&&(val2=='b')&&(val3=='i')) return true;`. `std::ws` skips characters for which the classic-locale `isspace` is true: space, `\t`, `\n`, `\v` (0x0b), `\f` (0x0c), `\r`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/xml_arch.rs:94 — `bytes.iter().copied().skip_while(|c| c.is_ascii_whitespace())`. Rust `u8::is_ascii_whitespace()` matches space, `\t`, `\n`, `\r`, `\f` — but NOT `\v` (0x0b) (verified: `0x0b.is_ascii_whitespace() == false`).
- surface: the capability dispatch heuristic that decides whether a file "looks like" a `<binaryimage>` XML executable. A `<binaryimage>` file whose very first byte is a vertical tab is matched by C++ `isFileMatch` (it skips `\v`, then reads `'<','b','i'`) but rejected by the port (it reads `'\v','<','b'`). XML save files never begin with `\v` (the corpus/datatest images start with `<binaryimage` or leading space/newline), so this is unreachable for real inputs; `isFileMatch` is itself a best-effort probe (the raw capability is the universal fallback). No oracle/datatest exercises a `\v`-prefixed image.
- why: `is_ascii_whitespace()` is the natural Rust spelling of "skip whitespace" but its set omits `\v`, a one-byte difference from the C++ classic-locale `isspace`. The other five whitespace bytes (space/`\t`/`\n`/`\r`/`\f`) are handled identically.
- restoration criteria: change the skip predicate to match classic-locale `isspace` exactly, e.g. `skip_while(|c| matches!(c, b' ' | b'\t' | b'\n' | 0x0b | 0x0c | b'\r'))`. Then flip `is_file_match_vertical_tab_diverges_from_cpp` in `rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w4_fw_arch_frontends.rs` to assert the `\v`-prefixed `<binaryimage>` MATCHES. Verified by the w4-fw-arch-frontends verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-053: `TransformManager::apply()` materialization stops at the `Funcdata` factory seams — a transform graph containing any placeholder *op*, any real (non-constant) *piece*, an INDIRECT mark, a `constant_iop`, or an input piece cannot be applied (errors at the seam); only pure constant/unique placeholder graphs run end-to-end (w5-s3-transform ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES)
- date: 2026-06-11

- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/transform.cc — `TransformOp::createReplacement` :225 (`fd->opSetOpcode(op,opc)` :230/:240 needs `glb->inst[opc]`, the TypeOp table; the preexisting input-arity reshape :231-236 follows it), `TransformVar::createReplacement` :175 (`fd->newUniqueOut` :192 / `fd->newVarnodeOut` :207 the xref split-borrow, `fd->transferVarnodeProperties` :208, `PcodeOp::getOpFromConst`+`fd->newVarnodeIop` :213-214), `TransformManager::transformInputVarnodes` :729 (`fd->setInputVarnode` :736), `TransformManager::specialHandling` :654 (`fd->markIndirectCreation` :657/:659).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/transform.rs — `create_op_replacement` :1172 (both branches return `Err` at the `opSetOpcode` seam, so the preexisting input-arity reshape loops are present only as comments and are unported), `create_var_replacement` :1289 (`normal_temp/piece_temp` with a `def` → `newUniqueOut` seam :1320; `piece` → `transferVarnodeProperties` seam :1360 always, plus `newVarnodeOut` seam :1353 when it has a `def`; `constant_iop` → `getOpFromConst` seam :1371), `transform_input_varnodes` :1402 (`setInputVarnode` seam, returns `Err` on the first input), `special_handling` :1126 (`markIndirectCreation` seam). Each is tagged `// SEAM(...)` and returns a structured `KunaError::lowlevel`.
- surface: `TransformManager::apply()` for any non-trivial transform. The lane-splitting/double-precision transforms that `ActionLaneDivide`/`SplitFlow` (S5/S7, W8+) build ALWAYS contain placeholder ops and real pieces, so a faithful end-to-end `apply()` is not reachable until the seams are filled — at this item's W5 boundary no caller exists (the actions that drive `apply` are later waves). Only a pure constant/unique placeholder graph (no ops, no real pieces) materializes fully; that path is exercised green by `apply_*` tests in `transform/tests.rs` and `w5s3_apply_visits_pieces_in_create_index_order_not_insertion_order` in `tests/verify_w5_s3_transform.rs`. The seam errors themselves are pinned by `apply_with_op_hits_opsetopcode_seam`, `apply_with_piece_hits_transfer_properties_seam`, and `apply_constant_iop_hits_getopfromconst_seam`.
- partial-state note: at the op seam the real op IS created first (`fd->newOp`, bumping the create-index — preserving the downstream allocation order the module doc calls load-bearing) before the error; at the input-transform seam the first input's old varnode IS deleted (`deleteVarnode`) before the error. Because `apply()` aborts via `?` and no W5 caller catches it (the C++ has no try/catch around `apply` that relies on partial state), this leaves no observable half-state at the item boundary.
- why: the construction phase — the placeholder graph (every `new*`/`get*` factory), the dedup/guard logic, and the pieceMap/newVarnodes/newOps visit order that determines downstream create-index allocation — is the load-bearing surface for the transform stage and is ported in full. The materialization tail depends on `Funcdata` factories (TypeOp opcode table, the varnode xref split-borrow, indirect-creation marking, iop-offset decode) that belong to W3-varnode/W4/W6; transcribing the call sites in place with seam errors keeps the visit order and the create-index bumps faithful while deferring the dependency, rather than stubbing the factories with guessed behavior.
- restoration criteria: as W3-varnode (`newVarnodeOut`/`newUniqueOut`/`setInputVarnode`/`transferVarnodeProperties`), W4 (`markIndirectCreation`), and W6 (`opSetOpcode(OpCode)` via `glb->inst[opc]`, `PcodeOp::getOpFromConst`) land, replace each `// SEAM(...)` `Err` with the transcribed call (the preexisting reshape loops at create_op_replacement included), then convert the three `apply_*_seam` tests to assert the materialized IR (opcode set, piece varnode at the computed address, INDIRECT marked, input varnode set). Verified by the w5-s3-transform verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-055: ruleaction_2 simplification rules use bare `<<`/`>>` shifts where the C++ relies on x86 shift-count masking — `RuleZextEliminate` (`val >> 8*smallsize`) debug-PANICS for an 8-byte ZEXT input where C++ produces a defined result (w5-s3-rules-2 round-1 REJECT F1)

- kind: divergence (integer semantics / ADR-0003 violation) — **tracked under a REJECT verdict**, not an accepted loss; recorded so the divergence is visible to the next attempt. Same hunt-list category and same bug class as the w3-ir-op round-1 REJECT (F1/F2: plain shift panics where C++ relies on x86 shift-count masking).
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/ruleaction.cc:2535 (`if ((val>>(8*smallsize))==0)` in `RuleZextEliminate::applyOp`). `val` is `uintb` (uint64), `smallsize` is the byte size of the ZEXT *input*. With an 8-byte ZEXT input, `8*smallsize == 64`; the C++ shift-by-64 of a 64-bit unsigned is technically UB but in practice on x86-64 the `shr`/`shl` instruction masks the count to 6 bits, so `val >> 64 == val >> 0 == val`, and the guard degenerates to `val == 0`. The same pattern recurs (latent) at ruleaction.cc:1563/1567 (`fullmask>>sa`/`fullmask<<sa`, `RuleAndCommute`, sa unbounded), :1896/:1902 (`mask<<sa2`/`mask>>sa2`, `RuleDoubleShift`), :1667 (`othermask>>(lowvn->getSize()*8)`, `RuleAndPiece`), :1769 (`baseconst << subop->getIn(1)->getOffset()*8`, `RuleAndCompare`), and :2137-2161 (`constval>>sa`, `nzmask<<sa`, `(((uintb)1)<<sa)`, `RuleShiftCompare`, sa unbounded).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/ruleaction_2.rs:1676 (`if (val >> (8 * smallsize)) == 0`) — bare `>>` panics `attempt to shift right with overflow` in debug for `smallsize == 8`. Confirmed by the verifier test `verify_w5_s3_rules_2::w5_s3_rules_2_zexteliminate_wide_input_panics_f1` (a passing `#[should_panic(expected = "shift")]`). The companion `..._narrow_input_ok` proves the narrow (smallsize < 8) path is otherwise faithful. The other latent sites are at ruleaction_2.rs:442/451/612/762/935/942/1277/1281/1290/1305/1308 — none use the ADR-0003-mandated `wshr`/`wshl` helpers (`grep -c wsh ruleaction_2.rs == 0`), although both helpers exist in kuna-base (`types.rs:105/108`).
- surface: any `INT_EQUAL`/`INT_NOTEQUAL`/`INT_LESS`/`INT_LESSEQUAL` whose written operand is an `INT_ZEXT` of an 8-byte value (a 128-bit-comparison lift, or a pathological/corrupted ZEXT). RuleZextEliminate (unlike RuleAndZext/RuleShiftCompare/RuleAndCommute in the same batch) carries NO `getSize() > sizeof(uintb)` early-out, so the wide input reaches the shift. Output-affecting on the wide-input edge: the C++ continues (and may rewrite or decline), the Rust aborts the process. Not reachable from the current item gate (the rules are not yet wired into a running pipeline, so ADR-0003's debug-golden wrap detector never exercised the site), which is why the porter's in-module tests (all smallsize < 8) did not catch it.
- restoration criteria: per ADR-0003, transcribe every value-word shift whose count can legitimately reach >= 64 with the wrapping-shift helpers — `val.wshr(8*smallsize as u32)` at ruleaction_2.rs:1676 (and the latent `<<`/`>>` sites listed above) so a shift count >= 64 yields the C++/x86-masked result instead of panicking. Then flip `w5_s3_rules_2_zexteliminate_wide_input_panics_f1` from `#[should_panic]` to assert the C++ oracle (`val >> 64 == val`, so the guard reduces to `val == 0`) against a g++-built `RuleZextEliminate` on the same 8-byte-ZEXT graph, and re-run the w5-s3-rules-2 gate green. Confirmed by the w5-s3-rules-2 verifier (round 1).

- partial-state note: `RuleOrCompare` and `RuleUnsigned2Float`/`RuleLzcountShiftBool` reach `newUniqueOut` *after* having created a fresh op and (for OrCompare) linked V/W as its descendants; on the seam Err they return 0 leaving those orphan ops/descend links behind (the `op_set_output` prologue — two `opUnsetOutput`s — also runs, a no-op on the just-created op). This is a manifestation of the LOSS-035 W3 partial-state seam, not a net-new divergence; in C++ this path completes.

Each returns the C++ guard's *conservative/failing* value (0 = "rule did not apply"), so the rule no-ops exactly where its subsystem is missing — no invented expression/cpool/collapse behavior.

Output-affecting once a W8 `universalAction` runs them over a real `Funcdata` with the W5-expression/W4/W6 subsystems present: BooleanDedup's shared-subexpression fold, MultiCollapse's MULTIEQUAL collapse, the Sborrow/Scarry signed-comparison rewrites, the constant-expression collapse, and the cpool resolution would then fire where today they leave the graph unchanged; the three minor symbol/print-hint annotations would attach. The conservative `RuleIndirectCollapse` STORE arm keeps an INDIRECT that C++ collapses only in the (guarded-but-not-for-op's-address) sub-case.

The one nuance is `RuleTransformCpool`: C++ sets `cpoolTransformed` (a visit mark) before its lookup; the port deliberately does NOT set it (so the eventual real port is not pre-marked as already-visited), a faithful-restoration choice rather than a divergence with observable effect at this boundary (no consumer reads the flag yet).

At each, replace the seam bail with the real call and add a positive end-to-end transform test. Verified by the w5-s3-rules-3 verifier (round 1).

Each rule evaluates every guard that uses available API for real (so the negative tests exercise the genuine control flow) and returns 0 at the first missing primitive. The only fully-committing path is `RuleSubNormal::apply_op` :976-981 (`n==0` -> `op_set_input(op,a,0)` + `new_constant(4,c)` + `op_set_input(...,1)`), pinned by `sub_normal_unnecessary_shift_collapses_to_subpiece` (in-module) and `w5s3r6_sub_normal_boundary_equal_collapses_not_extension` (verifier).
