Output-affecting once a W8 `universalAction` runs them over a real `Funcdata` with the W3/W4/W6 subsystems present: the 13 seamed rules would then perform their PTRADD/PTRSUB undo, the mult/add cleanups, the SUBPIECE-to-shift rewrites, the string-constant setup, the extension push, the CONCAT-structure splitting, and the magic-number division recovery (RuleDivTermAdd/RuleDivTermAdd2/RuleDivOpt) where today they leave the graph unchanged. RuleSubNormal already transforms on the unnecessary-shift (`n==0`) edge.
- partial-state note: `RuleSubNormal`'s extension sub-path (:949) and final-shift path (:993) call `data.new_op(2,...)` and THEN hit `seam_op_set_opcode`/`seam_new_unique_out` and return 0, leaving an orphan dead PcodeOp (no output, unset inputs, on the dead list, referenced by nothing) — exactly the `newUniqueOut`-after-`newOp` partial-state class that LOSS-035 records and `Funcdata::clone_op` already exhibits at this merge base. It is inert (no varnode descend list references it; the op-graph the rule reports "unchanged" via return 0 is unchanged in every reachable respect) and bounded (the rule deterministically no-ops on these inputs, so the engine will not re-trigger it). Pinned by `w5s3r6_sub_normal_shrink_k_then_seam_no_change` (the SUBPIECE is structurally untouched after the bail). `Rule2Comp2Sub` deliberately gates its `opSetOpcode` seam BEFORE any input rewiring, so it leaves NO partial state (a faithful no-op). This is a manifestation of the LOSS-035 W3 partial-state seam, not a net-new divergence; in C++ these paths complete.
- restoration criteria: each seamed rule's emit restores when its subsystem lands — `seam_op_set_opcode` (MultNegOne/PositiveDiv/2Comp2Sub and the SubNormal/DivTerm*/DivOpt commits) when W6 supplies the `glb->inst[opc]` `OpCode`->`TypeOp` table + the opcode-flags table (the LOSS-035 `op_set_opcode(OpCode)` restoration); `seam_new_unique_out`/`seam_new_extended_constant` when funcdata exposes `banks_mut`+`opSetOutput` and the 128-bit constant factory (W3-varnode, LOSS-035/042); `seam_is_constant_extended` with the W3-varnode 128-bit constant decode (unblocks DivTermAdd/DivTermAdd2/DivOpt form matching); `seam_type_read_facing`/`opUndoPtradd`/`opMarkSpecialPrint`/`getExactPiece`/`getBase`/`getStructuredType`/`isPtrsubMatching` with W6 type-facing + type-factory; the EquateSymbol/enum/`Scope::isReadOnly`/`stringManager` guards with W4; `RulePushPtr::duplicateNeed` with the sibling ruleaction batch and W3-funcdata; `RulePieceStructure`'s `gatherPieces`/`registerProtoPartialRoot` with W6/W7. At each, replace the seam helper with the real call, drop the `Err(_)=>return 0` bail, and add a positive end-to-end transform test (the `RuleSubNormal` n==0 collapse is the template). Verified by the w5-s3-rules-6 verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-062: SubvariableFlow's transform half (`doReplacement`) + all six trigger rules are cross-wave-seamed to no-op — `do_replacement` returns `Err(SEAM)` and `run_subflow` converts a successful `doTrace` into `return 0`, so every `RuleSubvar*::applyOp` always reports "no change" even when discovery succeeds, where C++ materialises the logical sub-variable (w5-s3-subflow ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: deferral (cross-wave seam; `// SEAM(W3-funcdata)`/`// SEAM(W6)` marked) — accepted, ledgered per the LOSS-053/054/057 precedent. The discovery engine (`doTrace`: `setReplacement`, `createOp`/`createOpDown`, `traceForward`/`traceBackward` and their sext variants, `createLink`/`createCompareBridge`, the `addConstant`/`addNewConstant`/`createNewOut` placeholder builders, and the eight `add*Patch` patch builders) is ported in full and is fully executable; only the materialisation tail is seamed.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/subflow.cc:1435-1545 `SubvariableFlow::doReplacement` — `fd->opSetOutput` (the (vbank,obank) split-borrow), `fd->newOp`/`opSetOpcode`/`opSetInput`/`opSetAllInput`/`opInsertAfter`/`opInsertBefore`/`newUniqueOut`/`newConstant`/`opRemoveInput`, and the patch-application loop (copy_patch/compare_patch/parameter_patch/extension_patch/push_patch/int2float_patch). The six rules' tails subflow.cc:1580-1581, 1616-1617, 1676-1677, 1699-1700, 1718-1719, 1737-1738 each call `subflow.doReplacement(); return 1;` after a successful `doTrace`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/subflow.rs:1983 `SubvariableFlow::do_replacement` returns `Err(KunaError::lowlevel("...SEAM(W3-funcdata)/SEAM(W6)"))` unconditionally; rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/subflow.rs:2357 `run_subflow` (the shared rule idiom) maps `do_replacement` `Ok` → 1 and `Err` → 0, so a discovered-but-unmaterialised transform reports `0` ("no change made"). The geometry helpers `use_same_address` (:1814) and `get_replacement_address` (:1850) are ported but `#[allow(dead_code)]` because only the seamed `do_replacement` calls them.
- surface: every `RuleSubvarAnd/Subpiece/CompZero/Shift/Zext/Sext::apply_op` over a real `Funcdata`. C++ shrinks the containing Varnode to its logical sub-size (e.g. an `x & 0xff` whose only use is the low byte becomes a byte-sized data-flow, turning the masking ops into dead code); the port discovers the same subgraph (`doTrace` returns the same true/false) but leaves the graph unchanged. No in-tree `universalAction`/`ActionPool` consumer runs these yet (W8), so the no-op is not observable at this item's gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` green; the in-module `forward_*`/`backward_copy_then_forward_terminal` tests pin that discovery succeeds and builds the correct placeholders/patches; the verifier's `verify_w5_s3_subflow.rs` adds the aggressive-gate and W4-call-seam discovery checks).
- partial-state note: `run_subflow` calls `do_replacement` only AFTER `do_trace` returns true, and `do_replacement` errors on its first statement before any `fd->op*` mutation, so the graph is left identical to entry (no half-materialised subgraph). The `doTrace` mark-set/clear is symmetric (every marked Varnode is cleared in the `varmap` walk), so even on the seam path no stray `isMark` flag survives.
- why: `Funcdata::opSetOutput` (the LOSS-035/042 funcdata split-borrow), `newOp`/`opSetOpcode(OpCode)` (the W6 `glb->inst[opc]` TypeOp table), and `newUniqueOut`/`newVarnodeOut`/`setInputVarnode` (the W3-varnode xref tail) do not exist in the merged tree at W5; emitting the transform here would invent them ahead of their items with no consumer to validate. The discovery half — the worklist push order, descendant visit order, per-opcode trace/create/patch structure that determines the decompiler's output — is the load-bearing surface and is transcribed statement-for-statement.
- restoration criteria: when funcdata lands `opSetOutput`/`newOp`/`opSetOpcode(OpCode)`/`opSetInput`/`opSetAllInput`/`opInsertAfter`/`opInsertBefore`/`newUniqueOut`/`opRemoveInput` and varnode lands `newVarnode`/`setInputVarnode`/`totalReplace`/`deleteVarnode` (and W6 `TypeOpFloatInt2Float::preferredZextSize`), fill `do_replacement` in C++ order (push patches first, then define all outputs, then set all inputs, then the terminal patch loop) and drop the `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `use_same_address`/`get_replacement_address`; then change `run_subflow`'s `Err` arm to propagate and assert the materialised byte-sized data-flow on at least the `subvar_and` and `subvar_subpiece` arms. Confirmed by the w5-s3-subflow verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-063: SubvariableFlow's terminal `try*` helpers and `Varnode::isZeroExtended` are W4/W6/W3-varnode-seamed inside `doTrace` — a logical flow that reaches a CALL/CALLIND parameter, a RETURN, a FLOAT_INT2FLOAT, or an INT_DIV/INT_REM cannot be traced (the discovery itself returns `Err`/`false`), where C++ would trim the parameter/return/conversion (w5-s3-subflow ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: deferral (cross-wave seam; `// SEAM(W4)`/`// SEAM(W6)`/`// SEAM(W3-varnode)` marked) — accepted. Unlike LOSS-062 (which seams only materialisation), these seams sit INSIDE `doTrace`: they gate specific trace edges, so a flow that reaches them fails to discover rather than merely failing to emit. The integer/shift/compare/copy/extension/SUBPIECE/PIECE/boolean/switch edges — the bulk of discovery — remain fully executable.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/subflow.cc:208-228 `tryCallPull` (`fd->getCallSpecs`/`FuncCallSpecs::isInputActive`/`isInputLocked`/`isDotdotdot`), :238-284 `tryReturnPull` (`fd->getFuncProto().isOutputLocked()` + the cross-RETURN propagation loop), :293-310 `tryCallReturnPush` (`getCallSpecs`/`isOutputLocked`/`isOutputActive`), :341-367 `tryInt2FloatPull` (`TypeOpFloatInt2Float::preferredZextSize`), and `Varnode::isZeroExtended(flowsize)` at :450-451 and :802-803 (the INT_DIV/INT_REM whole-byte guard, both forward and backward).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/subflow.rs:477 `try_call_pull`, :509 `try_return_pull`, :533 `try_call_return_push`, :591 `try_int2float_pull` each transcribe their early consume/mask/size guards (executable) and then return `Err(SEAM)` at the point the W4 call-site/proto or W6 type-op state is needed; :1996 `is_zero_extended` returns `Err(SEAM(W3-varnode))` unconditionally. Because `doTrace`/`traceForward`/`traceBackward` propagate these with `?`, a CALL/RETURN/INT2FLOAT/DIV/REM descendant aborts the whole trace as `Err` (surfaced by the verifier test `w5s3_forward_call_descendant_surfaces_w4_seam`). `try_switch_pull` (:565, BRANCHIND) is NOT seamed — its C++ body only checks mask/consume and records a parameter patch, so it is ported executable.
- surface: a sub-variable flow whose logical value is passed to a non-locked CALL parameter, flows to a non-output-locked RETURN, is zero-extended into a FLOAT_INT2FLOAT, or feeds a whole-byte INT_DIV/INT_REM. C++ trims these (e.g. narrows a call argument or return value to its logical size); the port cannot even discover them — `doTrace` returns false/Err so the rule no-ops (subsumed by LOSS-062's no-op, but here the divergence is in the discovery result itself, not just the emit). The `tryReturnPull` cross-RETURN propagation (which mutates the worklist/patchlist for sibling RETURNs before the W4 guard) is gated whole, so no partial sibling-RETURN state is created. No consumer at this item's gate; the executable edges are exercised by the in-module + verifier tests.
- why: `FuncCallSpecs`/`FuncProto` (W4 call-site/prototype state), `TypeOpFloatInt2Float::preferredZextSize` (W6 type-op), and `Varnode::isZeroExtended` (not yet on the W3-varnode accessor surface) are absent at W5. The guard structure (the early returns that can decide the edge WITHOUT the missing state, e.g. `slot==0`, the `consume & ~mask` aggressive test, the `mask&1`/`bitsize<8` checks) is transcribed verbatim so the supplying wave fills only the residual state check.
- restoration criteria: when W4 lands `Funcdata::getCallSpecs`/`FuncCallSpecs::isInput{Active,Locked}`/`isDotdotdot`/`isOutput{Locked,Active}` and `FuncProto::isOutputLocked`, replace the `Err` tails of `try_call_pull`/`try_return_pull`/`try_call_return_push` with the residual locked/active checks (and `try_return_pull`'s cross-RETURN `beginOp(CPUI_RETURN)` propagation loop); when W6 lands `TypeOpFloatInt2Float::preferredZextSize` fill `try_int2float_pull`'s `pullModification` refinement; when `Varnode::isZeroExtended` lands on the W3-varnode surface fill `is_zero_extended`. Then flip `w5s3_forward_call_descendant_surfaces_w4_seam` to assert the discovered parameter patch. Confirmed by the w5-s3-subflow verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-064: `RuleSubvarShift::applyOp` (and the unguarded SUBPIECE/PIECE/INT_MULT shift sites in `SubvariableFlow`) use bare `<<`/`>>` where the C++ relies on x86 shift-count masking — a 1-byte INT_RIGHT with a constant shift >= 64 debug-PANICS in Rust where C++ produces a defined (UB-masked) result and returns 0 (w5-s3-subflow ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES F1, same family as LOSS-055)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (shift-past-width: debug panic vs C++ x86-masked UB) — accepted minor loss; output-identical on every well-defined input.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/subflow.cc:1692-1695 `RuleSubvarShift::applyOp` — `int4 sa = (int4)op->getIn(1)->getOffset(); uintb mask = vn->getNZMask(); if ((mask >> sa) != (uintb)1) return 0; mask = (mask >> sa) << sa;` with NO `sa >= sizeof(uintb)*8` guard (unlike the trace functions, which DO guard at :476/:502/:530). The same unguarded shape appears at the backward SUBPIECE :808-810 (`sa = (int4)getOffset()*8; newmask = rvn->mask << sa;`), the backward PIECE :820-821 (`sa = getIn(1)->getSize()*8; newmask = rvn->mask>>sa;`), and the forward INT_MULT :439-443 (`sa = leastsigbit_set(...nz) & ~7;` which is `-8` when the multiplier's nzmask is 0, then `rvn->mask << sa`). C++ shift-past-width / negative-shift is UB but x86 masks the count, so it returns a value and never crashes.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/subflow.rs:2254/2257 `RuleSubvarShift::apply_op` (`(mask >> sa) != 1u64`, `(mask >> sa) << sa`), :1298 backward SUBPIECE (`mask << sa`), :1317 backward PIECE (`mask >> sa`), :708 forward INT_MULT (`self.rv(rvn).mask << sa`) — all bare operators. In a debug build (the ADR-0003 overflow-panicking profile) `mask >> 64` / `mask << -8` PANIC ("attempt to shift … with overflow"); in release they wrap (x86-masked), matching C++. Pinned by the verifier test `w5s3_subvar_shift_amount_64_panics_where_cpp_is_masked_ub_f1` (+ the `..._small_amount_no_panic_returns_zero` control isolating the divergence to the shift width).
- surface: a `RuleSubvarShift` trigger (INT_RIGHT of a 1-byte value) whose constant shift amount is >= 64, or a backward SUBPIECE/PIECE whose operand is >= 8 bytes, or a forward INT_MULT whose other operand is known-zero. SLEIGH does not emit a >=64-bit shift of a byte (it would be dead/folded), and an 8-byte SUBPIECE-offset / known-zero multiplier are likewise degenerate; on all well-formed p-code (shift < width) the bare and wrapping operators agree byte-for-byte, so this is output-affecting only on malformed/degenerate input — and there the C++ is itself UB. Same family as LOSS-055 (`RuleZextEliminate`'s `val >> 8*smallsize`).
- why: the surrounding guards (`if (sa >= sizeof(uintb)*8)` in the trace functions, the `mask&1` carry checks) show the author's intent is that these shifts stay in range; the unguarded sites are pre-existing C++ latent UB rather than "legitimately wrapping unsigned arithmetic," so a bare operator reads as faithful. ADR 0003, however, mandates `wshl`/`wshr` for any shift whose count can leave [0,width) — these sites qualify, and the foundation's own `sign_extend_sized` already uses the `w*` helpers for exactly this reason.
- restoration criteria: replace the bare `<<`/`>>` at subflow.rs:2254/2257, :1298, :1317, :708 (and audit the remaining `sa`-driven shifts) with `wshl`/`wshr` so a >=width or negative count produces the x86-masked value instead of panicking, matching the C++ de-facto behavior. Then flip `w5s3_subvar_shift_amount_64_panics_where_cpp_is_masked_ub_f1` to assert `apply_op == 0` with no panic. (Pure-discovery fix; no subsystem dependency.) Confirmed by the w5-s3-subflow verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-065: `RuleSubvarSext::reset` hard-codes `isaggressive = false` where C++ reads `Architecture::aggressive_ext_trim` — on a processor spec that sets `aggressive_ext_trim=true`, the Rust sext-trim trace stays non-aggressive (w5-s3-subflow ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, W4 arch seam)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: deferral (W4 arch seam; `// SEAM(W4)` marked) — accepted minor loss; matches the C++ DEFAULT (`false`), diverges only for specs that override it.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/subflow.cc:1742-1746 `RuleSubvarSext::reset` — `isaggressive = data.getArch()->aggressive_ext_trim;`. The field defaults to `false` (architecture.cc:157) and is set `true` only by a spec `<aggressive_trim>` decode (architecture.cc:1133). `isaggressive` then becomes the `aggr` argument to the `SubvariableFlow(...,true,...)` sext trace, loosening the consume/input guards in `setReplacement`/`tryCallPull`/`tryReturnPull`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/subflow.rs:2340 `RuleSubvarSext::reset` sets `self.isaggressive = 0;` with a `SEAM(W4)` comment; `apply_op` (:2330) passes `self.isaggressive != 0` (always `false`) as the aggressive flag. `Funcdata::getArch` returns the `seams::Architecture` skeleton with no `aggressive_ext_trim` field at W5.
- surface: `RuleSubvarSext` on an architecture whose processor spec enables `aggressive_ext_trim` (a small set of RISC specs). There the C++ runs the sext-trim trace aggressively (admitting more sub-variable flows); the port runs it conservatively. Currently subsumed by LOSS-062 (the rule no-ops at `do_replacement` anyway), and unreachable on the default architecture (where C++ is also `false`), so not output-affecting at this item's boundary. No consumer at the gate.
- why: `Architecture::aggressive_ext_trim` is not on the W3 arch seam surface (`Funcdata::getArch` is a skeleton); reading it would require inventing the W4 arch option-decode. The C++ default (`false`) is preserved exactly.
- restoration criteria: when the W4 arch surface exposes `aggressive_ext_trim`, change `RuleSubvarSext::reset` to read `data.get_arch().aggressive_ext_trim` and add a test that flips the flag and asserts the looser sext-trim discovery. Confirmed by the w5-s3-subflow verifier (round 1).

The genuine algorithmic content of the file scoped to this item (the `BooleanMatch`/`BooleanExpressionMatch` correlation evaluator, `functionalEqualityLevel`/`functionalEquality`/`functionalDifference`, `rootPointer`/`pointerEquality`, `TermOrder`+`additiveCompare`, `TraverseNode::isAlternatePathValid`, `PcodeOpNode`) IS fully ported and independently tested.

Output-affecting once the rules that consume them run: `AddExpression` is the discovery primitive for `RuleSborrow`/`RuleScarry` (already ledgered as deferred in LOSS-056), and the bitfield family backs the INSERT/EXTRACT/ZPULL/SPULL bitfield read/write recovery rules.
- why: the bitfield family dereferences the W6 `Datatype`/`TypeStruct`/`TypeBitField`/`TypePartialStruct`/`BitRange` type lattice and the W4 `Symbol`/`HighVariable` surfaces (`getHigh()->getSymbol()`, `symbol->getType()`), none of which exist at this item's boundary; `AddExpression` needs `calc_mask`/`getOffset` (available) but its only consumers are W5-s3-rules-3 rules already deferred (LOSS-056). Porting now would invent those subsystems ahead of their checklist items with no consumer to validate against (the LOSS-035/036/056 accountability situation).
- restoration criteria: port `AddExpression` alongside (or just before) the `RuleSborrow`/`RuleScarry` restoration named in LOSS-056; port the `BitFieldExpression` family when the W6 type lattice (`TypeStruct`/`TypeBitField`/`findMatchingBitField`/`getSubType`) and the W4 symbol surface land, with a positive test recovering a bitfield read and write. Each pins against its C++ body. Confirmed by the w5-s3-condexe-expression verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-067: `ConditionalExecution::execute` / `ActionConditionalExe::apply` / `RuleOrPredicate` mutation paths surface the `opSetOutput` (LOSS-035/036) + W6 `opSetOpcode` op-info seams as `Err`, degrading to "no change" — and an execute that reaches a new-op-creation point leaves PARTIAL graph mutation (destroyed iblock ops / orphan op+varnode) before the seam bail, where C++ completes the transform (w5-s3-condexe-expression ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, W3/W6 seam)

The verify/discovery path (`testIBlock`/`findInitPre`/`verifySameCondition`/`testOpRead`/`testMultiRead`/`testRemovability`/`verify`/`trial`, and the `RuleOrPredicate::MultiPredicate` discovery) is fully ported and tested; only the data-flow rewrite is seamed.

## LOSS-068: `RuleSLess2Zero::applyOp`'s two INT_AND arms use a bare `>>` for the sign-bit fetch where the C++ relies on x86 shift-count masking — a `-1 s< (avn & mask)` / `(avn & mask) s< 0` whose `avn` is > 8 bytes debug-PANICS in Rust (`8*avn_size-1 >= 64`) where C++ shifts past the `uintb` width (UB, x86-masked to count&63) and returns 0 (w5-s3-rules-5 ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, same family as LOSS-055/064)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: divergence (shift-past-width: debug panic vs C++ x86-masked UB) — accepted minor loss; output-identical on every well-defined input (avn <= 8 bytes).
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/ruleaction.cc:5767 (`-1 s<` arm) and :5839 (`s< 0` arm) — `uintb mask = maskVn->getOffset(); mask >>= (8 * avn->getSize() - 1);  // Fetch sign-bit`. Neither INT_AND arm guards `avn->getSize() <= 8` (unlike the SUBPIECE arms at :5741/:5815 which DO `return 0` for `avn->getSize() > 8`). For an 8-byte `avn` the shift is `>> 63` (the genuine 64-bit sign bit, well-defined); for `avn->getSize() > 8` it is `>> (>=64)`, which is C++ UB but on x86 masks the count (`& 63`) and yields a value, so `applyOp` quietly fails the `(mask & 1)` test and returns 0 — never crashes.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/ruleaction_5.rs:543 (`-1 s<` arm) and :666 (`s< 0` arm) — `let mask = mv.get_offset() >> (8 * avn_size - 1);` with `avn_size: int4`, bare `>>`. In a debug build (the ADR-0003 overflow-panicking profile) `u64 >> 127` PANICS ("attempt to shift right with overflow"). Pinned by the verifier test `verify_w5_s3_rules_5::sless2zero_int_and_oversize_avn_shift_panics` (16-byte avn; a passing `catch_unwind` asserting the panic — actual trace: `ruleaction_5.rs:543:36 attempt to shift right with overflow`), with the controls `..._int_and_size8_fires_no_panic` / `..._int_and_size8_lhs_form_fires` isolating the divergence to the size > 8 case (size 8 -> `>> 63`, both arms fire cleanly).
- surface: a `RuleSLess2Zero` trigger where one operand is the `0`/`-1` constant and the other is `INT_AND(avn, #mask)` with `avn` strictly larger than 8 bytes (a signed comparison of a > 64-bit value, e.g. a 16-byte int128 / vector-lane spec). On all machine-word-sized operands (`avn <= 8`) the bare and wrapping shifts agree byte-for-byte, so this is output-affecting only on the > 8-byte path — and there the C++ is itself UB (the sign bit of a > 64-bit value is not even representable in the 64-bit `uintb` the rule shifts). Uncommon but reachable; same family as LOSS-055 (`RuleZextEliminate`) and LOSS-064 (`RuleSubvarShift`).
- why: the round-1 verifier classified this F3 as "documentation only / unreachable," but ADR 0003 makes it a `wshr`-helper violation ("any shift whose count can leave [0,width)" must use `w*`, "never bare operators"; "every panic site is a place where a bare operator should have been a `w*` call"), and the sibling ruleaction_2 / subflow ports already ledger the identical pattern (LOSS-055/064) rather than dismissing it — so it is recorded here for ledger consistency. `grep -c 'wsh' ruleaction_5.rs == 0`; both helpers exist in kuna-base.
- restoration criteria: replace the bare `>>` at ruleaction_5.rs:543 and :666 with `wshr` (or add the `avn_size > 8` early-out the SUBPIECE arms already carry) so a >= width count yields the x86-masked value instead of panicking, matching the C++ de-facto behavior. Then flip `sless2zero_int_and_oversize_avn_shift_panics` to assert `apply_op == 0` with no panic. (Pure-discovery fix; no subsystem dependency.) Confirmed by the w5-s3-rules-5 verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-072: `RuleCondNegate::applyOp` is PARTIALLY SEAM-stubbed at `w5-s3-rules-4` — the BOOL_NEGATE insertion is transcribed but `opNormalizeFlip` (the early-return-1 normalize path) reports false and `opFlipCondition` (the mandatory condition flip) is a no-op, so a reached body would NEGATE the CBRANCH boolean WITHOUT flipping the branch sense (an inverted-control-flow hazard) (w5-s3-rules-4 ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, W3-block seam)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: deferral with a partial-mutation hazard (cross-wave seam; `// SEAM(W3-block)` marked) — accepted, ledgered per the LOSS-067 partial-state precedent. The `isBooleanFlip()` guard + the new-op shell (newOp/opSetOpcode(BOOL_NEGATE)/newUniqueOut/opSetInput/opInsertBefore) are transcribed; the two flip primitives are absent.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/ruleaction.cc 5494-5513 (`RuleCondNegate::applyOp`): `if (!op->isBooleanFlip()) return 0;` / `if (data.opNormalizeFlip(op)) return 1;` / insert BOOL_NEGATE on `getIn(1)` / **`data.opFlipCondition(op)`** (toggle the CBRANCH boolean_flip + fallthru bookkeeping). The C++ ALWAYS pairs the BOOL_NEGATE insertion with `opFlipCondition`; without the flip the boolean and the branch sense disagree.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/ruleaction_4.rs:2083-2106 (`RuleCondNegate::apply_op`): the `is_boolean_flip()` guard (real), the dropped `opNormalizeFlip` early-return (comment: "reports false"), the BOOL_NEGATE insertion (real, lines :2094-2100), and the dropped `opFlipCondition` (comment-only no-op, :2101-2103) — so the apply path returns `1` having mutated the graph but NOT flipped the condition.
- surface: NONE today — `RuleCondNegate` is not wired into any `ActionPool` (grep: `specs()` has no consumer in the merged tree), and the only caller, `condnegate_rejects_unflipped`, exercises the `isBooleanFlip()==false` early-out, so the partial-mutation path is never reached. The hazard is latent: when W3 supplies `opFlipCondition`/`opNormalizeFlip` and W8 wires the rule, a CBRANCH with `boolean_flip` set would (a) without the seam closure, get a BOOL_NEGATE but keep its original branch sense — INVERTING the decompiled control flow — and (b) `isBooleanFlip()` would remain true (the flip clears it), re-triggering the rule and stacking BOOL_NEGATEs. This is the divergence to fix at restoration; it is strictly worse than a clean no-op stub and must not be wired before the seam closes.
- why: `Funcdata::opNormalizeFlip` (the in-place CBRANCH normalize that can succeed outright) and `opFlipCondition` (the boolean_flip/fallthru toggle) are the W3-block CBRANCH primitives, not on this wave's `Funcdata`. The porter chose to transcribe the BOOL_NEGATE shell rather than stub the whole body to `0`; functionally a full `return 0` stub would have been the safer conservative choice at this wave (no mutation), but either way the rule must not run until the flip lands.
- restoration criteria: when the W3-block CBRANCH surface (`opNormalizeFlip`, `opFlipCondition`) lands, (1) restore the `opNormalizeFlip` early-return-1, (2) restore `opFlipCondition(op)` after the BOOL_NEGATE insertion, and (3) BEFORE wiring `RuleCondNegate` into any `ActionPool`, add a positive test that a `boolean_flip` CBRANCH gets exactly one BOOL_NEGATE AND its condition flipped (and that re-running the rule is now a no-op, i.e. `isBooleanFlip()` was cleared). Until then the rule must stay unwired. Confirmed by the w5-s3-rules-4 verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-073: the seven `kuna_*` simplification rules (w5-kuna-rule-pack) emit through W3/W6 op-creation seams — `op_set_input`/`op_set_opcode`/`new_unique_out` surface `Err`/skeleton `TypeOp`, and the rules convert those `Err`s to PANIC via `.expect()` where the C++ `opSetInput`->`addDescend` throws a *recoverable* `LowlevelError`; the const-folded carry path (`RuleAddCarryChain` INT_LESSEQUAL form) deterministically hits it (w5-kuna-rule-pack ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 1)

The seven rule bodies (`RuleBoolSignShift`, `RuleBoolSignLess`, `RuleSborrowGe`, `RuleOvLessSimplify`, `RuleAddCarryChain`, `RuleArrayStride`, plus the `OptionStackAlias`/`OptionCondExePlace` toggles) and every static helper (`kunaIsCarryOf`/`kunaSameValue`/`kunaStripCast`, `matchSborrowGe`/`extractAndOfOrs`/`extractOrOfAnds`/`stripBoolNeg`, `matchAddConst`, `kunaIsInductionPhi`/`kunaIsZeroInit`) are transcribed verbatim and independently re-derived by the verifier (the in-file tests + the seven `verify_w5_kuna_rule_pack.rs` adversarial tests pin the decision logic, including the commuted-operand, swapped-edge, and not-`-1` boundary cases).  Only the op-graph emit reaches an unported subsystem.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/kuna_addcarrychain.cc:116-134 (`newOp`/`newUniqueOut`/`opSetOpcode`/`opSetInput`/`opInsertBefore` building baseop+zextop, then rewriting the PIECE); kuna_arraystride.cc:128-137 (`newOp`/`opSetOpcode`/`newUniqueOut`/`opSetInput`/`opInsertBegin`/`totalReplace`/`opDestroy`); kuna_booleanmask.cc:53-55, kuna_flagcompare.cc:58-60/262-264, kuna_ovlesssimplify.cc:101-103 (`opSetOpcode`/`opSetInput`/`opRemoveInput`/`newConstant` rewrites).  The free-varnode guard that throws is decompiler/cpp/varnode.cc `Varnode::addDescend` ("Free varnode has multiple descendants") reached from `Funcdata::opSetInput`; the ActionPool (action.cc:822-875) does NOT catch it at the rule, so it unwinds to the top-level decompile catch (recoverable: skip/restart the function).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/kuna_addcarrychain.rs:185-218 + `new_unique_out` :335-346 (the `op_set_output` seam reproduced via `create_def_unique`+`set_output`; the `opSetInput` calls use `.expect(...)`); kuna_arraystride.rs:159-179 + `new_unique_out` :287-297; kuna_booleanmask.rs:172-177; kuna_flagcompare.rs:142-149/221-228; kuna_ovlesssimplify.rs:198-205.  Every `op_set_opcode(op, TypeOp::new(opc, 0, "..."))` carries a ZERO property-flag word (the W6 `glb->inst[opc]` table is unported), and every `op_set_input(...).expect(...)` converts the W3 `Err` to a panic.
- surface: (1) the W6 zero-flag `TypeOp` skeleton — the rewritten op carries the right `OpCode` (`code()` correct) but a blank property-flag word until the W6 typeop `inst` table lands; no consumer reads those flags at this item's boundary.  (2) F1 (exception->panic): the const-folded carry form of `RuleAddCarryChain` (the `kunaIsCarryOf` INT_LESSEQUAL / RuleCarryElim branch) ALWAYS re-wires an original low-add operand (`indexvn=a` into the new ZEXT, `baselo=b` into the new base PIECE) while that operand is still read by the original low `INT_ADD`; in the const-folded form one of {a,b} is a constant (`isFree()`), so the re-wire makes a free varnode gain a 2nd descendant.  C++ THROWS a recoverable `LowlevelError` here (the function decompile aborts/restarts, leaving the partial baseop/zextop graph — the LOSS-055 partial-state class); the port PANICS via `.expect()` (process abort).  Pinned by `w5_addcarrychain_carryelim_const_baselo_panics_where_cpp_throws_lowlevelerror`.  Note this path produces NO fused output in C++ either (it is a latent upstream bug in the GH-8913 first-cut const-folded form), so the port is output-faithful (both fail to fuse) — the only delta is panic vs. catchable exception.  The plain INT_CARRY ADC idiom (both operands variable) folds correctly (pinned by `fuses_carry_chain_to_wide_add` + `w5_addcarrychain_commuted_int_carry_operands_fold`).
- why: `Funcdata::op_set_output`'s `setDef` step needs the `(&mut VarnodeBank, &mut PcodeOpBank)` `banks_mut` split the funcdata owner has not exposed (LOSS-035/036), and `op_set_opcode(OpCode)` needs the W6 `glb->inst[opc]` op-info table; neither exists at W5.  `apply_op` returns `int4` (not `Result`), mirroring the C++ `applyOp` signature, so the rule cannot propagate an emit error — the choices are panic (visible) or silently swallow (invisible wrong-state).  The port chose `.expect()`, consistent with the LOSS-035/055/067 family, and reproduced `newUniqueOut` directly through `create_def_unique`+`set_output` rather than inventing `op_set_output` ahead of its item.
- partial-state note: `RuleAddCarryChain`/`RuleArrayStride` build a fresh op (and, for addcarrychain, fully wire+insert `baseop` and create `zextop`) BEFORE the panic site, so the panic leaves the same orphan-op partial state the C++ `LowlevelError` leaves — the LOSS-055 class — except the port aborts the process instead of unwinding to a catch.  The five pure-rewrite rules (booleanmask/flagcompare x2/ovlesssimplify) only `.expect()` on `op_set_input` over operands that are already live (the boolean/base varnodes are written), so their `.expect()` is not reachable on well-formed input.
- restoration criteria: when `Funcdata` exposes `banks_mut()` / completes `op_set_output` (LOSS-035/036) and the W6 `glb->inst[opc]` `OpCode`->`TypeOp` flag table lands: (1) replace `new_unique_out` with the real `newUniqueOut`, (2) build `TypeOp` via the real op-info table (non-zero flags), (3) change the emit `op_set_input(...).expect(...)` calls to propagate via the W-action exception machinery (matching the upstream `LowlevelError` recover/restart) rather than panic, and (4) decide whether to FIX the upstream-latent const-folded carry path (e.g. COPY the constant operand before re-wiring, or guard the INT_LESSEQUAL branch out) — porting GH-8913's bug verbatim is faithful but the const-folded carry never fuses today.  Add a positive end-to-end transform test for the INT_LESSEQUAL form once fixed.  Verified by the w5-kuna-rule-pack verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-074: five rules in the `ruleaction.cc:8374-9803` batch (`RuleSegment`, `RulePtrFlow`, `RuleConditionalMove`, `RuleIgnoreNan`) are cross-wave-seamed to a no-op or partial match — `RuleSegment`/`RuleIgnoreNan::applyOp` return `0` outright, `RulePtrFlow` installs nothing (`has_truncations=false`) and its LOAD/STORE/CALLIND/BRANCHIND arms bail, and `RuleConditionalMove` matches the block topology up to the CBRANCH then bails before any rewrite, where C++ performs the segment-constant fold / NaN-removal / pointer-flow truncation / conditional-move collapse (w5-s3-rules-7 ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, W4/W6/W3-output seams)

- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/ruleaction.cc:9028-9063 `RuleSegment::applyOp` (`userops.getSegmentOp`/`getSpaceFromConst`/`SegmentOp::execute`/`hasFarPointerSupport`/`contiguous_test`/`findContiguousWhole`; throws `LowlevelError` on a missing segment def); :9071-9076 `RulePtrFlow` ctor (`glb->getDefaultDataSpace()->isTruncated()`); :9192-9263 `RulePtrFlow::applyOp` LOAD/STORE/CALLIND/BRANCHIND arms (`getSpaceFromConst`/`getDefaultCodeSpace`/`truncatePointer`); :9320-9564 `RuleConditionalMove::{gatherExpression,constructBool,applyOp}` (`CloneBlockOps::cloneExpression`, `opBoolNegate`); :9755-9797 `RuleIgnoreNan::applyOp` (`nan_ignore_all`, `TypeOp::isFloatingPointOp`, `functionalEquality`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/ruleaction_7.rs:1566-1572 `RuleSegment::apply_op` (returns 0); :1602-1604 `RulePtrFlow::new` (`has_truncations: false`) + :1668-1685 `get_op_list` (empty without truncations) + :1701-1711 the LOAD/STORE/CALLIND/BRANCHIND arms (return 0); :1935-1939 `RuleConditionalMove::apply_op` (bails after the CBRANCH discovery); :2066-2074 `RuleIgnoreNan::apply_op` (returns 0). `RuleFloatCast` (:1977-2023) is NOT seamed — all four C++ arms are ported and fire (pinned by `floatcast_*`); the `isFloatingPointOp` mention in the module doc applies only to `RuleIgnoreNan`.
- surface: `RuleSegment` over a SEGMENTOP with constant operands (C++ folds to a COPY of the computed value); `RulePtrFlow` on a truncated-pointer architecture (C++ inserts SUBPIECE truncations + propagates ptrflow); `RuleConditionalMove` on a 2-input MULTIEQUAL whose inputs are booleans from a diamond (C++ collapses to BOOL_AND/BOOL_OR/zext(cond)); `RuleIgnoreNan` on a FLOAT_NAN feeding a bool combinator (C++ removes the NaN). No in-tree `universalAction`/`ActionPool` consumer runs these at this item's gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` green), so the no-op is not observable here; once W8 wires them over a real `Funcdata` with W4/W6 present they would transform where today they leave the graph unchanged. `RulePtrFlow` is additionally unreachable on the default (non-truncated) architecture, where C++ also installs nothing.
- partial-state note: `RuleSegment`/`RuleIgnoreNan`/`RulePtrFlow` LOAD/STORE/CALLIND/BRANCHIND all bail at their FIRST statement (no `op*` mutation), and `RuleConditionalMove` bails after a read-only topology walk, so each leaves the graph identical to entry — NO partial mutation (unlike LOSS-075 below).
- why: `Architecture::{getDefaultDataSpace,getDefaultCodeSpace,nan_ignore_all}`, `userops.getSegmentOp`/`SegmentOp::execute`/`getSpaceFromConst` (W4), `TypeOp::isFloatingPointOp` (W6), `functionalEquality`, `CloneBlockOps`/`opBoolNegate`, and `truncatePointer`'s `newUniqueOut`/`newVarnodeOut` (W3-output, the LOSS-035 `opSetOutput` split-borrow) are all absent at W5. `apply_op` returns `int4` (not `Result`), mirroring the C++ signature, so a rule cannot defer past its emit; the choice is a faithful read-only no-op, which the port took.
- restoration criteria: fill each rule's emit when its subsystem lands — `RuleSegment` + `RulePtrFlow` truncation arms when W4 supplies the user-op/segment + default-space surface and W3-output exposes `truncatePointer`; `RulePtrFlow` ctor when `getDefaultDataSpace().isTruncated()` exists (then `has_truncations` drives `get_op_list`); `RuleConditionalMove` rewrite tail when `CloneBlockOps::cloneExpression`/`opBoolNegate` land; `RuleIgnoreNan` when `nan_ignore_all` + `TypeOp::isFloatingPointOp` + `functionalEquality` land. At each, drop the early `return 0` / `has_truncations=false` and add a positive end-to-end transform test. Verified by the w5-s3-rules-7 verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-075: `RuleSignNearMult::applyOp` (and the documented `RulePtrFlow::truncatePointer`) build a fresh `INT_SDIV`/`SUBPIECE` op and set its opcode BEFORE hitting the `op_set_output` (`newUniqueOut`) seam, so on a FULL pattern match the rule returns `0` ("no change") while leaving an orphan dead op in the obank — where the C++ path never fails and always completes the `(V s/ 2^n) * 2^n` rewrite (w5-s3-rules-7 ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, W3-output partial-state, same class as LOSS-035/067)
- date: 2026-06-11
- kind: deferral (W3-output `opSetOutput` seam) + a contained partial-state divergence — accepted, ledgered per the LOSS-035/055/067 `newUniqueOut`-after-`newOp` precedent. The entire pattern match (the INT_AND -> INT_ADD -> INT_RIGHT/INT_SRIGHT chain, the `mask=(mask<<n)&mask` window via `wshl`, the `8*x.size-1` sign-shift check) is transcribed verbatim and is fully executable; only the construction tail is seamed.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/ruleaction.cc:8612-8624 `RuleSignNearMult::applyOp` — `newOp(2,...)`/`opSetOpcode(newdiv,CPUI_INT_SDIV)`/`newUniqueOut(x->getSize(),newdiv)`/`opSetInput`x2/`opInsertBefore`, then rewrites `op` to INT_MULT. This path has NO C++ failure mode: once the pattern matches it always returns 1. The same `newOp`-then-`newUniqueOut` shape is at :9169-9190 `RulePtrFlow::truncatePointer` (reached from the seamed LOAD/STORE arms of LOSS-074).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/ruleaction_7.rs:730-741 — `data.new_op(2, addr)`, `data.op_set_opcode(newdiv, typeop_for(CPUI_INT_SDIV))`, then `data.new_unique(...)` + `data.op_set_output(newdiv, divvn)` whose `Err` (the funcdata `op_set_output` split-borrow seam, funcdata_op.rs:172-193) triggers `return 0; // SEAM(W3-output)`. `newdiv` has been allocated and had its opcode set before the bail, so it survives as an orphan op (no output, no inputs, not inserted into any block — `op_insert_before` is never reached).
- surface: a real `(V + (V s>> 0x1f)>>(32-n)) & (-1<<n)` division-rounding idiom. C++ rewrites it to `(V s/ 2^n) * 2^n`; the port matches fully but returns 0 (no transform) AND leaves one dead INT_SDIV op floating in the obank. No in-tree consumer runs this rule or walks the orphan at the item gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` green); the orphan is inert (no varnode descend list references it; the op-graph the rule reports "unchanged" via return 0 is unchanged in every reachable respect) and bounded (the rule deterministically no-ops on this input, so the engine will not re-trigger).
- why: `Funcdata::op_set_output`'s `setDef` step needs the `(&mut VarnodeBank, &mut PcodeOpBank)` `banks_mut` split the funcdata owner has not exposed (LOSS-035/042); creating the unique output ahead of it would invent the W3-output API ahead of its item. Reordering the port to gate the seam BEFORE `new_op` (as `Rule2Comp2Sub` does per LOSS-061) would avoid the orphan but diverge structurally from the C++ statement order; the porter chose verbatim order + the inert orphan, matching the `Funcdata::clone_op` / `RuleSubNormal` (LOSS-061) precedent at this merge base.
- restoration criteria: when `Funcdata` exposes `banks_mut()` / completes `op_set_output` (LOSS-035/042) so `new_unique`+`op_set_output` materialise the INT_SDIV output, drop the `Err(_) => return 0` bail at ruleaction_7.rs:739 (and the same in `truncatePointer` when W3-output + the W4 LOAD/STORE arms of LOSS-074 land), and add a positive end-to-end transform test asserting `op` becomes INT_MULT(divvn, 2^n) with the inserted INT_SDIV. Verified by the w5-s3-rules-7 verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-076: `RuleBooleanUndistribute` reduces `BooleanMatch::evaluate` to its `vn1==vn2` head (W5-expression seam) and the `RuleAndCompare`/`RuleZextEliminate` constant-resize rewrites skip `copySymbol`/`copySymbolIfValid` (W6 symbol seam) — the rule declines structurally-equal-but-distinct clauses C++ would fold, and the resized constants lose their inherited symbol/data-type (w5-s3-rules-2 round-2 ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES)
- date: 2026-06-12
- kind: deferral (cross-wave seam; `// SEAM(W5-expression)` / `// SEAM(W6)` marked in `ruleaction_2.rs`) — accepted, ledgered per the LOSS-056/066 (`BooleanMatch`) and LOSS-056 (`copySymbolIfValid`) precedent. The rule bodies are transcribed verbatim through every guard and the slot/flip gymnastics; only the leaf subsystem each reaches is seamed, and each seam is strictly *conservative* (a missed transform / a missing annotation, never a wrong rewrite).
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/ruleaction.cc — `RuleBooleanUndistribute::isMatch` (:2717) calls `BooleanMatch::evaluate(leftVn,rightVn,1)` (decompiler/cpp/expression.cc:112), whose body past the `if (vn1==vn2) return same;` head (expression.cc:115) recursively peels `BOOL_NEGATE` for complementary matches and compares bool-output operands structurally; `RuleAndCompare` (:1785) `constvn->copySymbol(andop->getIn(1))` when `baseconst==andconst`; `RuleZextEliminate` (:2537) `newvn->copySymbolIfValid(vn2)`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/ruleaction_2.rs — `boolean_match_evaluate` (:1941-1949) returns `SAME` only for `vn1==vn2` and `UNCORRELATED` for everything else (the `COMPLEMENTARY` constant is referenced but never produced); `RuleBooleanUndistribute::is_match` (:1959) therefore returns true only on identical-VarnodeId clauses, so the four `is_match` probes (:2024-2038) fall through to `return 0` where C++ would partition. The two `copySymbol`/`copySymbolIfValid` calls are elided inline (:792-794 `RuleAndCompare`, :1703-1705 `RuleZextEliminate`) with `// SEAM(W6)` notes; the resized constant is still created with the correct size/value, only the symbol/data-type carry-over is dropped.
- surface: none at this item's gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` green; `RuleBooleanUndistribute` is a leaf rule with no in-tree `universalAction` consumer, and `expression.rs` was a stub at this item's merge base). Output-affecting once a W8 `universalAction` runs the rule with the real `BooleanMatch`: `(A && B) != (A && C)` style expressions whose shared clause `A` is reached through a COPY/NEGATE chain (rather than the literal same Varnode) would fold to `A && (B != C)` / `!A || (B == C)`; the seam leaves them un-distributed. The `copySymbol` elision drops a user/inferred symbol or data-type annotation off the resized constant (cosmetic in the C output; never changes the value or op-graph shape).
- partial-state note: the `BooleanMatch` seam bail returns 0 BEFORE any mutation (the four `is_match` probes are pure reads; `opBoolNegate`/`newOp` only run after a match is found), so the op-graph is identical to entry on decline — no orphan ops, unlike the LOSS-055 `newUniqueOut`-after-`newOp` family. The `copySymbol` elisions happen AFTER the new constant is created but the constant is fully wired regardless, so no partial state.
- restoration criteria: the `BooleanMatch` seam restores when `expression.rs` ports `BooleanMatch::evaluate` in full (the same W5-expression dependency named in LOSS-056 for `RuleBooleanDedup`, and supplied by the later `w5-s3-condexe-expression` item per LOSS-066) — replace `boolean_match_evaluate`'s stub body with the real recursive evaluator and add a positive transform test on a COPY/NEGATE-reached shared clause. The `copySymbol`/`copySymbolIfValid` elisions restore when the W6 `Varnode::copySymbol(Varnode*)` surface (data-type + `SymbolEntry` map carry-over) lands — drop the `// SEAM(W6)` no-ops and add a test asserting the resized constant inherits the source annotation. Verified by the w5-s3-rules-2 verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-077: `Funcdata::newCodeRef` cannot set `Varnode::annotation` — the code-ref destination Varnode is a free (non-annotation) size-1 Varnode where C++ flags it `annotation` (w4x-flow-linkage ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES; the residual sliver of LOSS-036/037 after the op-output/coderef restoration)
- date: 2026-06-12

- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/funcdata_varnode.cc:224-235 `Funcdata::newCodeRef` — `vn = vbank.create(1,m,ct); vn->setFlags(Varnode::annotation); assignHigh(vn);`. The `Varnode(int4,const Address&,Datatype*)` ctor (varnode.cc:597-622) only auto-sets `annotation` for the fspec/iop spaces (varnode.cc:618-619); a code-ref lands in a normal processor/code space, so the explicit `setFlags(Varnode::annotation)` is the only thing that marks it.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/funcdata_varnode.rs `new_code_ref` (~:435) — builds the size-1 Varnode at `m` via `vbank.create` and calls `assign_high`, but the `// vn->setFlags(Varnode::annotation)` line is a no-op comment because `Varnode::set_flags` (varnode.rs:953) is private to `varnode.rs` and there is no public `set_annotation` setter; this item has no edit rights in `varnode.rs`. The Rust ctor matches the C++ ctor exactly (annotation auto-set only for fspec/iop), so the code-ref Varnode comes back free, with `is_annotation()` == false where C++ returns true.
- surface: none at this item's gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` green; pinned by `verify_w4x_flow_linkage.rs::w4x_flow_linkage_new_code_ref_is_size1_annotation_at_target_no_annotation_flag`). The `(space, offset, size=1)` triple — the only thing the branch p-code input and the flow-following machinery read off a code-ref — is faithful, so control-flow following, the lift-fixture op-line gate, and every reachable W3/W4 consumer are unaffected. The divergence is observable only to a future consumer that branches on `Varnode::isAnnotation()` for a code-ref destination (none exists at the W4 boundary); such a consumer would see a code-ref counted as a real data-flow Varnode.
- why: setting the flag needs a one-line `varnode.rs` seam edit (`set_flags`/a `set_annotation` setter) that belongs to the varnode owner, not this op/flow-linkage item; fabricating it here would reach across the item boundary. Surfacing the absence (a free Varnode) rather than faking the flag is the visible, conservative choice — identical to the original LOSS-036 `newCodeRef` deferral, now narrowed to just the flag.
- restoration criteria: when `varnode.rs` exposes `Varnode::set_flags` (or a `set_annotation` setter), add the `vn.set_annotation()` call after `vbank.create` in `new_code_ref`, and flip the `verify_w4x_flow_linkage` pin to assert `is_annotation()` is now true (the LOSS-036/037 `newCodeRef` slivers then fully close). Verified by the w4x-flow-linkage verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-079: the funcdata_op helpers `replaceLessequal` and `opSetInput` drop `Varnode::copySymbol(vn)` onto the constant they re-duplicate — the duplicate carries only `newConstant`'s unknown base, not the source constant's inherited data-type / `SymbolEntry` (typelock|namelock) markup (w5x-helpers-completion ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 1; W4/W6 symbol seam)
- date: 2026-06-12
- kind: deferral (W4/W6 symbol-propagation seam; marked `// cvn->copySymbol(vn); -- SEAM(W4)` / `// newvn->copySymbol(vn); -- SEAM(W4)` in `funcdata_op.rs`) — accepted, ledgered per the LOSS-056 / LOSS-076 `copySymbol`/`copySymbolIfValid` precedent (resized/duplicated constants lose their inherited symbol/data-type until the W6 symbol surface lands). Every other statement of both helpers is transcribed verbatim and independently re-derived by the verifier; only the leaf `copySymbol` call is the no-op. The duplicate constant is fully wired (correct space/offset/size/value) regardless — the carry-over is the sole missing bit.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/funcdata_op.cc:112 (`Funcdata::opSetInput` — `Varnode *cvn = newConstant(vn->getSize(),vn->getOffset()); cvn->copySymbol(vn); vn = cvn;`) and :1062 (`Funcdata::replaceLessequal` — `Varnode *newvn = newConstant(vn->getSize(),res); newvn->copySymbol(vn);`). `Varnode::copySymbol` (varnode.cc:512-526) copies `type`, `mapentry`, and the `typelock|namelock` flag bits from the source Varnode to the destination (and marks `high` type-dirty).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/funcdata_op.rs — `op_set_input` (~:258, the constant re-duplication guard: `let cvn = self.new_constant(size, off);` with the `copySymbol` reduced to a `// SEAM(W4)` comment) and `replace_lessequal` (~:455, `let newvn = self.new_constant(size, res);` likewise). `new_constant` (funcdata_varnode.rs:167) builds the Varnode with `type_base_unknown(s)` and no `mapentry`/symbol, so the duplicate's data-type and lock bits diverge from the source whenever the source carried any.
- surface: none at this item's gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` green, 1253 tests; clippy lib-clean). At W3 the source constants these helpers see carry only the unknown base (no symbol/`mapentry` is attached to a W3-IR constant yet — `type_base_unknown` is exactly what `newConstant` itself builds), so `copySymbol` is a *structural* no-op today: source and duplicate are bit-identical. The divergence becomes observable only once W4/W6 type-and-symbol propagation attaches a data-type or `SymbolEntry` to a constant operand of a comparison / re-shared read; then the re-duplicated constant in a canonicalized `<=` (or any re-shared non-spacebase constant) would lose that annotation (cosmetic in the C output — a constant printed without its named/typed form — never a wrong value or op-graph shape).
- partial-state note: the `copySymbol` elision happens AFTER `new_constant` creates the duplicate, but the duplicate is fully linked regardless (the subsequent `opSetInput`/`op_set_input` wires it), so there is no partial state — on every path the op-graph is identical to what C++ builds minus the duplicate's symbol annotation.
- restoration criteria: when the W6 `Varnode::copySymbol(Varnode*)` surface (data-type + `mapentry`/`SymbolEntry` + `typelock|namelock` carry-over) lands — the same dependency named in LOSS-056 / LOSS-076 — add `dup.copy_symbol(src)` after the `new_constant` in both `op_set_input` and `replace_lessequal`, and add a test asserting the re-duplicated constant inherits the source's data-type/lock bits. Verified by the w5x-helpers-completion verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-080: the W5 B2 lift-differential's C++ `print raw` parser cannot recognize the CALLIND op-line — `TypeOpCallind::printRaw` glues the `callind` keyword to its target varnode with NO separating space (`callindpc(free)(..)`), so the parser's whitespace-token keyword match misclassifies every real CALLIND as `OpClass::Value` (w5-infra-lift-diff ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 2; differential-tool parser-grammar gap, SAFE direction)
- date: 2026-06-12
- kind: differential-tool parser incompleteness (the item is the dev-only `kuna-lift-diff`/`kuna-harness` B2 structural differential, no shipping-engine counterpart) — accepted because the misclassification is in the strictly SAFE direction and touches no gate PASS. `TypeOpCall::printRaw` (typeop.cc:676) emits `name << ' '` so the space-separated `call <target>` form IS parsed; `TypeOpCallind::printRaw` (typeop.cc:800) emits `s << name` with no trailing space, gluing `callind` to the target (`callindpc(free)(..)`, see `gp.b2.txt`). The parser's `call_class` keys on `rhs.split_whitespace().next()`, which yields the whole glued token for CALLIND, so it never matches `"callind"` and the op folds into `Value`.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/typeop.cc:793-811 `TypeOpCallind::printRaw` (`s << name; Varnode::printRaw(s,op->getIn(0));` — no space) vs :669-687 `TypeOpCall::printRaw` (`s << name << ' ';` — space). `name` is `"callind"`/`"call"` (typeop.cc:740/662).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-harness/src/liftdiff.rs:312-322 `call_class` (matches the first whitespace token against `"call"`/`"callind"`) and :273-309 `classify`. The shipped unit test `classify("callind r0(free)")` (liftdiff.rs:728) asserts a FICTIONAL space-separated CALLIND form the C++ never emits, giving false confidence in the CALLIND path.
- surface: none at this item's gate (`cd rust && cargo run -p kuna-lift-diff -- --self-test` -> 8/15 PASS, exit 0). NO gate PASS case contains a CALLIND; the only CALLIND-bearing corpus case (`gp`, x86/MIPS-style two-call function) already diverges on an earlier op-count delta (`block#0 @0x410040: op count differs rust=72 cpp=76`), so the misclassification is never reached on the path that decides its outcome. The Rust extractor maps `CPUI_CALLIND -> OpClass::Callind`, so a comparable CALLIND op makes the parser's `Value` and the Rust `Callind` DIVERGE — the differential can only OVER-report a divergence here, never fabricate a spurious PASS (pinned by `verify_w5_infra_lift_diff.rs::callind_real_grammar_misclassified_as_value_r2f6` and `::callind_misclass_diverges_never_spurious_pass_r2f6`).
- why: fixing it is a one-line parser change (detect the `callind` PREFIX rather than the whitespace token, since the keyword is glued), but the item's gate is sound without it (no PASS depends on CALLIND classification), and changing the classifier touches the per-op class taxonomy the whole differential keys on; deferring keeps the round-2 ACCEPT scoped to the verified boundary. Surfacing the gap (with the real-grammar pin) rather than silently leaving the fictional-input unit test as the only CALLIND coverage is the visible choice.
- restoration criteria: change `call_class` to match the `callind` PREFIX (e.g. `rhs.strip_prefix("callind")` guarded so it does not also swallow a hypothetical `callindfoo` user-op name — none exists), drop/replace the fictional `classify("callind r0(free)")` unit test with the real glued `callindpc(free)(..)` form, and add a CALLIND-bearing PASS or DIVERGENT fixture whose CALLIND op is in a COMPARABLE block so the class is a checked invariant end-to-end. Verified by the w5-infra-lift-diff verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-081: `ParamListStandard::separateSections` preserves the upstream C++ `nextSection > resourceStart.size()` (not `>=`) bound — on malformed input where `nextSection == size()` C++ silently reads `resourceStart[size()]` (OOB) while the Rust panics on the bounds-checked index (w6-s4-fspec-1 ACCEPT, verifier round 2; faithful-to-upstream-bug, unreachable with valid specs)
- date: 2026-06-12
- kind: faithful transcription of an upstream off-by-one (C++ latent OOB read kept as-written). Accepted: the port reproduces the C++ check verbatim rather than "fixing" it, which is the correct porting choice; the only behavioral difference (Rust panic vs C++ UB) appears solely on input that cannot occur with a well-formed compiler spec.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/fspec.cc:948-961 `ParamListStandard::separateSections` — `int4 nextSection = 2; ... if (curtrial.getEntry()->getGroup() >= nextGroup) { if (nextSection > resourceStart.size()) throw LowlevelError("Missing next resource start"); nextGroup = resourceStart[nextSection]; nextSection += 1; ... }`. The guard is `>` not `>=`, so when `nextSection == resourceStart.size()` the throw is skipped and `resourceStart[size()]` is read one past the end.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/fspec.rs:2623-2643 `separate_sections` — `let mut next_section = 2usize; ... if self.entry[entry].get_group() >= next_group { if next_section > self.resource_start.len() { return Err(...); } next_group = self.resource_start[next_section]; next_section += 1; ... }`. The `> len()` (not `>= len()`) check is the faithful transcription; at `next_section == len()` the Rust `self.resource_start[next_section]` index panics where C++ silently reads garbage.
- surface: none at this item's gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` green except the pre-existing out-of-scope `corpus_bootstrap::ldefs_pspec_cspec_parse_matrix` `.sla` artifact gap). Unreachable on any valid path: `decode` always pushes the `numgroup` sentinel onto `resourceStart` (fspec.cc:1504 `resourceStart.push_back(numgroup)`), so the section count is bounded by the real group span and `nextSection` cannot reach `size()` for trials whose groups come from those entries. A malformed spec with more `>=`-crossings than resource sections would hit the divergence (C++ UB / Rust panic), but such a spec is already rejected upstream of this method.
- why: "fixing" the off-by-one (using `>=`) would change behavior away from upstream on the boundary and is out of scope for a faithful port; preserving the exact check is correct. The Rust bounds-check turns the latent C++ OOB read into a deterministic panic, which is strictly safer and only reachable on input C++ also mishandles.
- restoration criteria: none required for faithfulness (this is a deliberate verbatim port of the upstream check). If upstream ever tightens the bound to `>=`, the port should follow via the normal `sync_upstream` path. Verified by the w6-s4-fspec-1 verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-085: two minor faithful-port arithmetic-width/charset divergences in type-3 `TypeFactory`/`TypePointerRel` — (a) `TypePointerRel::isPtrsubMatching` stores its running offset in a 32-bit `int4 iOff` so the C++ truncates the `addressToByteInt(off)+offset+extra` sum to 32 bits before the `0 <= iOff <= parent->getSize()` bound check, while the Rust port keeps the whole computation in `int8` (no truncation); (b) `Datatype::hashName` reads `(uint8)nm[i]` of a **signed** `char` (sign-extends bytes >= 0x80), while the port reads `nm.bytes()` as unsigned `u8` (no sign-extension). Both are unreachable in the realistic domain — (a) needs a >= 2^32 PTRSUB offset against an `int4`-sized parent type; (b) needs a non-ASCII byte in a type *name* (C identifiers are ASCII). (w6-s5-type-3 ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: faithful-port width + signedness divergences that are value-identical across the entire realistic input domain. The full TypeFactory container (tree/nametree/typecache interning, findAdd dedup by `compareDependency`, cacheCoreTypes, all atomic/pointer/composite getters, downChain/getExactPiece/concretize/resizeInteger) and ALL type-3 `TypePointerRel` overrides (compare/compareDependency/isPtrsubMatching/downChain) ARE ported and re-derived by the verifier; only these two corner-case arithmetic/charset edges differ.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/type.cc — `TypePointerRel::isPtrsubMatching` :3143 (`int4 iOff = AddrSpace::addressToByteInt(off,wordsize);`) + :3145 (`iOff += offset + extra;` — int4 truncates the int8 sum) + :3146 (`return (iOff >= 0 && iOff <= parent->getSize());`); `Datatype::hashName` :697-702 (`res += (uint8)nm[i];` over a signed `char`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/dtype.rs — `is_ptrsub_matching` PointerRel arm (`let i_off = i_off + *offset as int8 + extra; Ok(i_off >= 0 && i_off <= parent.get_size() as int8)`, all `int8`, no `as int4` truncation); `hash_name` (`for b in nm.bytes() { ... res.wrapping_add(b as uint8) }`, `b: u8` unsigned). The porter's `hash_name` doc-comment explicitly acknowledges the ASCII-only scope ("the type names hashed here are ASCII so the sign of char is moot, but we mask to a byte").
- surface: none at this item's gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` green for every in-scope test — dtype lib 1015 pass, verify_w5_dtype_expand 9 pass, verify_w6_s5_type_3 5 pass; the only red is the pre-existing out-of-scope `corpus_bootstrap::ldefs_pspec_cspec_parse_matrix` `.sla`-artifact gap the worktree cannot build, untouched by this dtype.rs-only branch). The verifier's `f1_ptrrel_isptrsubmatching_int4_truncation_divergence_documented` pins the Rust int8 behaviour at the 2^32 boundary so a future restoration is forced to notice the divergence.
- why: (a) the C++ `int4 iOff` truncation is an unintentional-looking narrowing in upstream; reproducing it bit-for-bit would require an explicit `as i32` wrap on the sum, which the porter elided in favor of the wider (mathematically-correct) computation — value-identical wherever `parent->getSize()` (an int4) bounds the result. (b) Rust `char`-to-`u8` iteration cannot sign-extend; matching the signed-`char` C++ would require treating each name byte as `i8 as i64 as u64`, only observable for non-ASCII name bytes that do not occur for C-identifier type names.
- restoration criteria: (a) if a non-trivial PTRSUB offset domain ever exceeds int4, wrap the PointerRel `isPtrsubMatching` offset sum with an explicit `(... as i32) as i64`-style truncation to mirror `int4 iOff` (and flip `f1_ptrrel_isptrsubmatching_int4_truncation_divergence_documented` to assert the truncated C++ result). (b) if a non-ASCII type name can reach `hashName`, change the byte read to `(b as i8) as i64 as u64` to sign-extend like the C++ `(uint8)(char)`. Both are guarded by the verifier tests in `verify_w6_s5_type_3.rs`.

## LOSS-086: `FuncProto::resolveExtraPop`'s 4-byte alignment step `cur = (cur+3)&0x0ffffffc` (fspec.cc:3992) is ported with a non-wrapping `cur + 3` i32 add, so a stack parameter whose offset drives `cur` to near `i32::MAX` triggers a DEBUG-build overflow panic where C++ wraps silently. RELEASE builds (overflow checks off) wrap identically to C++, so the shipping output is byte-identical; the divergence is debug-only and the input (a ~2GB stack offset) is non-physical for real frames. (w6-s4-fspec-2 ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 2)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: faithful-port wrapping divergence, debug-only, value-identical in release across the entire realistic input domain. The rest of `resolveExtraPop` is faithful: the `(int4)addr.getOffset()` truncation IS reproduced (`addr.get_offset() as i32`), the first sum `(int4)off + getSize()` correctly uses `wrapping_add` (fspec.rs:5073), the `IPTR_SPACEBASE`-only filter, the `isDotdotdot`/`numparams != 0 -> setExtraPop(4)` varargs early-out, the `expop >= 4` floor, and the `0x0ffffffc` (28-bit, NOT 0xfffffffc) mask are all exact. Only the second add `(cur + 3)` lacks `wrapping_`.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/fspec.cc:3991-3992 — `int4 cur = (int4)addr.getOffset() + param->getSize();` then `cur = (cur+3)&0xffffffc;` (C++ unsigned/signed overflow on `cur+3` wraps; `-Wno-sign-compare`/`-fwrapv`-style silent wrap).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/fspec.rs:5073-5074 — `let mut cur = (addr.get_offset() as i32).wrapping_add(param.get_size());` (wrapping, correct) then `cur = (cur + 3) & 0xffffffc;` (plain i32 add — panics in debug when `cur > i32::MAX - 3`).
- surface: none at this item's gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` is fully GREEN — lib 1027 pass, verify_w6_s4_fspec_2 9 pass, verify_w6_s4_fspec_2_r2 5 pass, every other suite pass, including `corpus_bootstrap` which the `.sla` artifacts present in this worktree let run). No existing or realistic test reaches the overflow: stack-frame offsets are small/frame-relative; negative offsets (large-unsigned, e.g. `0xFFFFFFFB`) truncate to small negatives and do not overflow. The divergence requires a crafted near-`i32::MAX` (~2GB) locked stack-parameter offset. The verifier's `resolve_extra_pop_near_i32_max_offset_w6s4f2_r2` constructs exactly that offset (0x7fff_fffb so `cur == i32::MAX`) and PINS the debug-build panic via `catch_unwind`, so a future fix is forced to notice it.
- why: round 1 flagged this as MINOR/non-blocking on a presumed-unreachable basis; round 2 confirms it IS reachable in principle (a reproducing input exists) but the impact is a loud debug panic, never silent output corruption, and release builds match C++ bit-for-bit. The one-character fix is `cur.wrapping_add(3)`. Recorded as a loss rather than a REJECT because (a) the gate is green, (b) no realistic/physical stack offset reaches it, and (c) the shipped (release) behavior is already C++-faithful — the only divergence is a debug-mode panic vs a C++ wrap.
- restoration criteria: change `(cur + 3)` to `cur.wrapping_add(3)` at fspec.rs:5074 (matching the already-correct `wrapping_add` on the line above), then flip `resolve_extra_pop_near_i32_max_offset_w6s4f2_r2` from asserting a panic to asserting the wrapped/masked value C++ computes (`((i32::MAX).wrapping_add(3)) & 0x0ffffffc`). Verified by the w6-s4-fspec-2 verifier round.

## LOSS-087: a faithful-port DEBUG-build overflow/shift divergence class in the ported constseq/prefersplit base — (a) `ArraySequence::form_byte_array`'s in-range guard `byte_pos + el_size > sz` (constseq.rs:276) is a *checked* i32 add that panics in debug when `byte_pos == i32::MAX` (the wrapped result of the int4 `offset - rootOff` when `offset >> rootOff`), where C++ `bytePos + elSize > sz` wraps int4 (UB-but-wraps) and `continue`s — silently dropping the op; (b) the constant-split shift `(origval >> (8 * losize))` in `PreferSplitManager::fillin_instance` (prefersplit.rs:272) and `split_zext` (prefersplit.rs:450) panics in debug for the degenerate `losize == vn_size` (shift-by-64), where C++ `origval >> 8*losize` is likewise UB. RELEASE builds wrap/produce-garbage identically to C++ for both; the divergence is debug-only and the inputs are non-physical (a near-2^31 byte position within a bounded array; a split record whose splitoffset equals the full storage size). (w6-s5-constseq-prefersplit ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: faithful-port wrapping/shift-overflow divergences, debug-only, value-identical in release across the realistic input domain. Same class as LOSS-086 (fspec `resolveExtraPop`). The rest of the base is faithful: `form_byte_array`'s negative-`byte_pos` skip uses `wrapping_sub` + `< 0` exactly (constseq.rs:275), the big/little-endian byte unpacking (`val >>= 8` little, `(val >> ((el_size-1-j)*8))` big), the `used[]` 1/2 marking + single-terminator allowance, the `< MINIMUM_SEQUENCE_LENGTH` reject, the `count != move_ops.len()` truncation with `wrapping_add(max_off)`, the `PreferSplitRecord` total-order comparator (space index, size-desc, offset), `find_record`'s `partition_point` lower_bound + `templ < *iter` equality probe, and the op-insertion order are all exact and verifier-re-derived.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/constseq.cc:116 (`if (bytePos < 0 || bytePos + elSize > sz) continue;` — int4 `bytePos + elSize` wraps on overflow); decompiler/cpp/prefersplit.cc:47 (`uintb hival = (origval >> 8*losize) & calc_mask( hisize );` in `fillinInstance`) and :177 (same shift in `splitZext`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/constseq.rs:276 (`if byte_pos < 0 || byte_pos + el_size > sz` — plain i32 add, debug-panics at `byte_pos == i32::MAX`); rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/prefersplit.rs:272 and :450 (`(origval >> (8 * losize))` — plain `>>`, debug-panics when `8*losize >= 64`). All three are read-only computations with no op-graph mutation before the panic point, so no partial-state divergence even on the debug panic.
- surface: none at this item's gate (`cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` — lib 1033 pass incl. the new constseq probes, verify_w6_s5_constseq_prefersplit 3 pass; the only red is the pre-existing out-of-scope `corpus_bootstrap::ldefs_pspec_cspec_parse_matrix` `.sla`-artifact gap, which the fresh worktree cannot build — zero `.sla` present in the worktree's specs/, untouched by this branch). No realistic caller reaches either overflow: `StringSequence`/`HeapSequence` (the only `form_byte_array` callers) bound offsets within an array of size `sz`, keeping `byte_pos` in `[0, sz)`; valid `PreferSplitRecord`s have `0 < splitoffset < size`, keeping `losize < size`. The verifier's `w6s5_form_byte_array_huge_bytepos_diverges_debug_panic` (`#[should_panic]`) pins the (a) divergence so a future fix is forced to notice it.
- why: round 1 flags this as MINOR/non-blocking by the LOSS-086 precedent — a loud debug panic vs a C++ wrap, never silent output corruption, and release builds match C++ bit-for-bit. The fixes are one-character: `byte_pos.wrapping_add(el_size)` (constseq.rs:276) and `origval.wrapping_shr((8 * losize) as u32)` (prefersplit.rs:272/:450). Recorded as a loss rather than a REJECT because the gate is green, no physical input reaches it, and the shipped (release) behavior is already C++-faithful.
- restoration criteria: (a) change `byte_pos + el_size` to `byte_pos.wrapping_add(el_size)` at constseq.rs:276 and flip `w6s5_form_byte_array_huge_bytepos_diverges_debug_panic` from `#[should_panic]` to asserting `count == 4` (the huge-offset op silently dropped, matching C++). (b) change `(origval >> (8 * losize))` to `origval.wrapping_shr((8 * (losize as u32)))` (or guard `losize < 8`) at prefersplit.rs:272 and :450 and add a `fillin_instance`/`split_zext` test at the degenerate `splitoffset == size` boundary. Verified by the w6-s5-constseq-prefersplit verifier round.

## LOSS-088: the constseq TRANSFORM halves and string-copy rules are not ported in this item — `StringSequence`/`HeapSequence`'s collection+transform bodies (`collectCopyOps`/`collectStoreOps`, `buildStringCopy`, `constructTypedPointer`, `removeCopyOps`/`removeStoreOps`, the `findBasePointer`/`calcPtraddOffset`/`gatherIndirectPairs`/`deduplicatePairs` heap machinery), the `RuleStringCopy`/`RuleStringStore` drivers, and the `ArraySequence::select_string_copy_function` charType IDENTITY check are SEAMed; only the reusable `ArraySequence` base (interfere_between/check_interference/form_byte_array) and `PreferSplitManager` (fully) are materialized. `select_string_copy_function` always returns `BUILTIN_MEMCPY` (the C++ fall-through default) instead of selecting `BUILTIN_STRNCPY`/`BUILTIN_WCSNCPY`, because the `charType == types->getTypeChar(getSizeOfChar()/getSizeOfWChar())` pointer-identity match needs a live `TypeFactory` not yet wired into `Architecture`. - date: 2026-06-13
- kind: cross-wave seams (W4 loc-set address-only overload, W4 `Scope::queryContainer`/`SymbolEntry` array-component resolution, W4/W6 `Funcdata::getInternalString`/`getTypePointer*`/`inheritUnionField*`/PTRSUB-PTRADD typed-pointer builder, W6 live `TypeFactory`). The in-scope reusable base (`ArraySequence`) is fully ported and verifier-re-derived; the deferred halves are the concrete drivers whose dependencies are later-wave surfaces.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/constseq.cc — `StringSequence` ctor :188-225 (`entry->getSymbol()->getType()` array-component resolution), `collectCopyOps` :233-270 (address-only `beginLoc(addr)`/`endLoc(addr)`), `buildStringCopy`/`constructTypedPointer` (getInternalString + typed-pointer builder), `selectStringCopyFunction` :161-175 (`charType == types->getTypeChar(...)` identity, falls through to `BUILTIN_MEMCPY`); `HeapSequence` collect/transform; `RuleStringCopy`/`RuleStringStore::applyOp`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/constseq.rs — module header :53-73 ledgers the deferred halves; `select_string_copy_function` :335-343 (`// SEAM(W6): TypeFactory not reachable from Architecture` -> returns `(BUILTIN_MEMCPY, num_elements * get_align_size())`, the C++ default arm). `StringSequence`/`HeapSequence`/`RuleStringCopy`/`RuleStringStore` are not present as types at this boundary. The `select_string_copy_function` SEAM is read-only (a pure selector); its only caller (`buildStringCopy`) is itself deferred, so the divergence is currently dead (no in-tree consumer reaches it).
- surface: none at this item's gate (same green-except-`.sla` status as LOSS-087). The constseq rules register nowhere yet (the `RuleStringCopy`/`RuleStringStore` Rule types are unported), so the deferred logic is not wired into any running pass at this boundary.
- why: the address-only loc-set overload, the W4 symbol-table array-component resolution, the W4/W6 type-factory + union-resolution typed-pointer builder, and the live `TypeFactory` identity comparison are all surfaces past this item's boundary — grep-confirmed absent from the merged tree. Porting the base alone (the output-determining gathering ORDER reused by `kuna_memsetsequence`) and SEAMing the drivers matches the LOSS-082/083/084 precedent (an in-scope file whose transform half depends on later waves is split: base now, drivers when the surface lands).
- restoration criteria: when W4 lands the address-only `beginLoc(addr)`/`endLoc(addr)` overload + `Scope::queryContainer`/`SymbolEntry` resolution and W4/W6 land `getInternalString`/`getTypePointer*`/`inheritUnionField*` + the live `TypeFactory`, transcribe `StringSequence`/`HeapSequence` collect/transform, `RuleStringCopy`/`RuleStringStore`, and replace `select_string_copy_function`'s `BUILTIN_MEMCPY`-only return with the real `char_type == types.get_type_char(get_size_of_char())` -> `BUILTIN_STRNCPY` / `get_size_of_wchar()` -> `BUILTIN_WCSNCPY` identity selection (the structure is already in place behind the seam). Verified by a future constseq/W4-W6 verifier round.

## LOSS-087: the entire `ScoreUnionFields` data-flow driver is W4/W6 seamed — `run`/`runOneLevel`/`newTrials`/`newTrialsDown`, all three `ScoreUnionFields` constructors, and the recursing / live-varnode arms of `scoreTrialDown`/`scoreTrialUp` (COPY/MULTIEQUAL/INDIRECT, LOAD/STORE, CALL*/CALLIND/RETURN, INT_ADD/INT_SUB/PTRSUB pointer-arithmetic, SUBPIECE, PTRADD) are NOT ported; only the pure value types and the pure metatype->score tables are. - date: 2026-06-13
- kind: graph-walk driver SEAM, value-faithful at the ported boundary. The scoring *decisions* are complete and verified — every pure opcode arm of both switches (down: unionresolve.cc:364-712, up: 735-915) is transcribed with exact score values, identical opcode coverage in both directions (mechanically diffed: zero missing, zero extra), and the `else { 0 }` fall-throughs match the C++ initialized-`score=0` defaults. `score_locked_type`/`score_truncation`/`score_constant_fit`/`deref_pointer`/`score_parameter`/`score_return_type`/`compute_best_index` and the load-bearing `ResolveEdge` total order are ported and re-derived. What is absent is the *orchestration*: building/swapping trial lists, the visited-set dedup, the pass/threshold/maxTrials loop, and the constructor setup that walks `Varnode::beginDescend`/`getDef`/`getSlot`. The recursing arms are routed through dedicated helpers (`deref_pointer`, `score_truncation`) that ARE ported, so W4/W6 fills the driver in place against frozen scoring functions; the seamed-arm score values themselves are not yet exercised end-to-end.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/unionresolve.cc:297-346 (`newTrialsDown`/`newTrials`), 355-921 (the driver arms of `scoreTrialDown`/`scoreTrialUp`), 1036-1085 (`runOneLevel`/`run`), 1095-1223 (the three constructors).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/unionresolve.rs:835-1349 (`score_tables::score_trial_{down,up}_pure` return `None` for the driver-handled arms; the driver itself is absent), module doc lines 29-58 enumerate the SEAM(W4)/SEAM(W6)/SEAM(W7) surfaces (`Funcdata::getCallSpecs`, `Varnode::getTypeReadFacing`/`getTypeDefFacing`/`isTypeLock`, `Funcdata::unionMap`/`getUnionResolution`/`setUnionField`, `Varnode::updateType`, `TypeOpSubpiece::computeByteOffsetForComposite`).
- surface: the item gate `cd rust && cargo test -p kuna-decomp` is GREEN for every unionresolve test (17 lib + 7 verifier). The one red suite, `corpus_bootstrap::ldefs_pspec_cspec_parse_matrix`, is PRE-EXISTING on the `rust-port` base and environmental (`Could not find .sla file for x86:LE:64:default` — needs a built SLEIGH artifact); unionresolve is a pure value/scoring module that touches no spec files, so it cannot have caused it.
- why: the union-field resolver is intrinsically IR-coupled — it scores candidate fields by walking the live data-flow graph around a Varnode. That graph (Funcdata/Varnode/PcodeOp descend/def/slot) and the FuncProto/unionMap surfaces are W4/W6/W7 deliverables that do not exist at this item's boundary. Porting the scoring matrices and value types now (frozen, fully tested) lets the later driver wire in without re-deriving the score tables. ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES because the ported surface is faithful and the seam is structural, not an error.
- restoration criteria: W4 lands `Funcdata::getCallSpecs`/`getFuncProto` + `FuncProto`; W6 lands `Varnode::getTypeReadFacing`/`getTypeDefFacing`/`isTypeLock` + the `unionMap`; W7 lands `setUnionField`/`updateType`. Then port `run`/`runOneLevel`/`newTrials*`/the three constructors against the frozen `score_tables`/helpers here, replacing each `None` arm's driver dispatch with the live-varnode read, and add an end-to-end scoring test over a real Funcdata.

## LOSS-089: two defensive division/modulo-by-zero guards in `deref_pointer` and `score_truncation` diverge from the C++ (which has no guard and would be UB on a zero-size pointee/array-element) — `deref_pointer` adds `ptrto.get_size() != 0 &&` before `vn_size % ptrto.get_size()` (unionresolve.cc:281) and `score_truncation`'s array arm adds `align != 0 &&` before `(curOff + vn_size) % elType->getAlignSize()` (unionresolve.cc:963). On a zero divisor the Rust skips the branch (deref: falls to `score = -5`; truncation: falls to the `else { score = 1 }`) where C++ executes `% 0`. (w6-s5-unionresolve ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: faithful-port panic-avoidance divergence, unreachable in the realistic domain. A `Datatype`'s `getSize()`/`getAlignSize()` is >= 1 for every real type (the only zero-size type is `void`, which is never a pointee in a LOAD/STORE value position nor an array element here), so the guarded branch and the C++ `% size` produce identical results whenever `size >= 1`. The guards exist only so a malformed zero-size type yields a defined score instead of a Rust panic / C++ UB.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/unionresolve.cc:281 (`(vn->getSize() % ptrto->getSize()) == 0`), 963 (`(curOff + vn->getSize()) % elType->getAlignSize() != 0`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/unionresolve.rs:575 (`max_length != 0 && ptrto.get_size() != 0 && (vn_size % ptrto.get_size()) == 0`), 659 (`align != 0 && (cur_off + vn_size as int8) % align as int8 != 0`).
- surface: gate green; no test (and no realistic input) constructs a zero-size pointee or array element, so the divergent branch is never taken.
- why: a one-token panic-guard that only changes behavior on input C++ treats as UB. Not a correctness loss on any well-formed type. Recorded for completeness so a future zero-size-type fuzz finding maps here.
- restoration criteria: none required for faithfulness; if exact C++-UB matching were ever desired (it is not), drop the guards — but that reintroduces a panic/UB on malformed input and is not recommended.

## LOSS-090: the ported `ModelRule`/`AssignAction` machinery is standalone, NOT wired into the live `ParamListStandard` — the rule decode + assignment bodies are a faithful transcription, but they are invoked only by their own tests, never by `ParamListStandard::assign_address`/`fillin_map`, which still fall straight through to `assign_address_fallback` (the `SEAM(w6-modelrules)` stub at fspec.rs:2389-2403).
