What is missing is the *caller side*: `ParamListStandard` does not own a `Vec<ModelRule>`, so no live prototype assignment ever consults a model rule. ConvertToPointer's recursion into `resource.assign_address` therefore also hits the fallback stub rather than re-entering the rule set.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/fspec.cc `ParamListStandard::assignAddress` (iterates `modelRules`, first-non-fail then fallback) and `modelrules.hh:531-554` (`ModelRule` owned by the resource).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/fspec.rs:2389-2403 (`assign_address` SEAM stub: `// model_rules is currently empty`, straight to `assign_address_fallback`); rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/modelrules.rs:23-46 (seam doc) and the standalone `ModelRule`/`AssignAction` with `resource`/`manager` threaded as args.
- surface: gate green (1065 lib tests + the modelrules lib/verify suites all pass; the only failing test, `corpus_bootstrap::ldefs_pspec_cspec_parse_matrix`, is the pre-existing missing-`.sla`-artifact environment failure, unmodified by this item). No live decompile consults a `<modelrule>` yet, so no behavioral regression at this boundary — the loss is reachability, not correctness, of the ported logic.

Materializing the faithful (frozen, tested) rule machinery now lets the later fspec-owned step plant it without re-deriving any rule body.
- restoration criteria: an fspec-owned item replaces the placeholder `ModelRule` with `modelrules::ModelRule`, adds `model_rules: Vec<ModelRule>` to `ParamListStandard`, populates it in the cspec decode path, and has `assign_address`/`fillin_map` iterate it (first-non-fail, then fallback) — then an end-to-end test asserting a `<modelrule>`-driven assignment overrides the metatype fallback.

## LOSS-091: two unreachable parse divergences in `SizeRestrictedFilter::initFromSizeList` (modelrules.cc:280-294) — (a) on a size value in (2^31, 2^63] the Rust `parse_size_list` parses i64 then truncates `as int4` (modelrules.rs:2216-2221), where C++ `istream >> int4` sets failbit and clamps to `INT_MAX` (2147483647); (b) whitespace skipping uses Rust `char::is_whitespace` (Unicode) vs C++ `istream >> ws` (`isspace`, C locale), so a byte like 0xA0 is skipped by Rust but not by C++. (w6-s4-modelrules ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: faithful-port edge divergence, non-physical inputs only. `<datatype sizes="...">` lists enumerate byte sizes of data-types — every real value is a small positive integer (1..a few thousand) of ASCII digits separated by `,`/space. Neither a >2^31 size nor a non-ASCII whitespace byte occurs in any shipped cspec; both branches diverge only on hand-crafted malformed input, and any non-positive parsed value already throws "Bad filter size" in both ports.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/modelrules.cc:282-294 (`s >> val; if (val <= 0) throw; sizes.insert(val);`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/modelrules.rs:2188-2237 (`parse_size_list`: i64 parse + `as int4`, and `(str[i] as char).is_whitespace()`).
- surface: gate green; the `v_w6_s4_modelrules_size_list_parse_parity` adversarial test pins the realistic domain (comma/space lists, the `val<=0` throw, the set-derived min/max), which matches C++ exactly. The two divergent branches are not reachable from any valid spec.
- why: the realistic size-list domain (small positive ASCII-separated integers) is reproduced bit-for-bit; matching C++'s overflow-clamp / locale-`isspace` quirks exactly would add complexity for input both ports treat as malformed.
- restoration criteria: none required for faithfulness; if exact matching were wanted, clamp the parse to `INT_MAX` on i32 overflow and gate the whitespace skip on `is_ascii_whitespace()` (the C-locale `isspace` set).

## LOSS-092: `bitfield.cc`'s two transform engines (`BitFieldInsertTransform`/`BitFieldPullTransform`) and all six rule `applyOp` bodies are unported — `bitfield.rs` ports only `BitRange` + `BitFieldNodeState` + the rule op-list/specs skeleton; every `apply_op` returns 0 at the transform hand-off (w6-s5-bitfield ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 1)

## LOSS-097: the incremental-dominator side maps iterate in `KunaNodeOrder`, not C++ raw-pointer order — a deliberate determinism improvement, proven output-equivalent
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: faithful-port iteration-order divergence (output-equivalent; Rust is *more* deterministic than the C++ it ports)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/kuna_regiongraph.cc:843-875 (`initDfs`, `updateInvertedDomtree`) — `doms` / `dfs` / `invertedDomTree` are `map<KunaRegionNode *,...>` with the DEFAULT comparator (`std::less<ptr>` = raw allocation-pointer order), unlike `NodeMap` which is explicitly `KunaNodeOrder` (kuna_regiongraph.hh:113). C++ thus visits these maps in pointer order, which is non-deterministic across runs / allocators.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/kuna_regiongraph.rs:1363-1432 (`compute_dfs` iterates `doms_in_order(pool)`; `update_inverted_domtree` iterates `doms_in_order(pool)`) — keyed/iterated in `KunaNodeKey` = `(addr, ident)` = `KunaNodeOrder`.
- surface: NONE observable. `compute_dfs` only ever *inserts* `u` into the set `res[v]` while climbing a fixed dom chain — the final per-node frontier sets are identical regardless of the order `u` are visited (set semantics). `update_inverted_domtree` builds `inverted_dom_tree[d]` lists whose ORDER is consumed only by `graph_updated` for dominatee re-pointing and membership, and every consumer (`idom()`, `df()`, `dominates()`, `verify()`) treats these as sets keyed by node identity, never reading list order into output. Verified by `verify_w7_s7_kuna_regiongraph::graph_updated_multi_dominatee_matches_scratch` (incremental patch with multiple simultaneous dominatees re-pointed == independent from-scratch recomputation).
- why: the C++ here is itself non-deterministic (pointer order); transcribing it literally would require reproducing `std::less<ptr>`, which is impossible and undesirable. `KunaNodeOrder` is the file's own stated determinism contract (kuna_regiongraph.hh:18-24). Recorded as a loss only because it is a literal divergence from the C++ map comparator; behaviorally it is strictly an improvement with no output change.
- restoration criteria: none required — this is the intended end state. If a future consumer is added that reads the per-dominator list ORDER into observable output, it must be re-checked against this `KunaNodeOrder` choice.

## LOSS-098: `ActionStripStackGuard` is detection-only — the terminal `removeBranch`/`removeUnreachableBlocks` CFG surgery is the W4/W8 funcdata_block seam, so the Action returns 0 (no change) even when a real `-fstack-protector` canary epilogue is detected
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: deferral (sub-stage seam; default-off option, byte-identical to upstream regardless)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/kuna_stackguard.cc:150-152 (`ActionStripStackGuard::apply`) — after pinning the canary CBRANCH, selecting the corrupted-canary (fail) successor, the handler-call safety check, and the victim-edge index `idx`, the C++ does `data.removeBranch(h,idx); data.removeUnreachableBlocks(false,true); return 1;`. The first call turns the CBRANCH into a fall-through to the canary-OK successor and patches MULTIEQUALs in the removed block; the second collects the orphaned `__stack_chk_fail` handler block. `return 1` reports the change so the repeating fullloop re-invokes and self-gates.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/kuna_stackguard.rs:319-329 (`ActionStripStackGuard::apply`) — the entire detection path is realized faithfully (option gate, per-block `dynamic_cast<BlockBasic>`/`sizeOut()==2`/`lastOp()==CBRANCH` scan, `is_canary_compare` boolean-flip-aware fail-successor selection, `block_has_call` safety, and the `idx` victim-edge search). At the marked seam point it stops with `let _ = (h, idx); return 0;` — it cannot mutate the block graph yet, so it reports no change and the option is structurally inert.
- surface: NONE on the regression suite — the option defaults off (`strip_stack_guard=false`, architecture.rs:370) so the Action is gated out; with the option *on*, the divergence is that kuna-rust keeps the canary epilogue (returns 0) where kuna-C++ strips it (returns 1). The C++ primitives are themselves already cut in the merged tree (LOSS-035/036 `removeBranch`/`removeUnreachableBlocks` in funcdata_op; LOSS-078 the same pair in coreaction_early). Pinned by `verify_w7_s6_dynamic_pack::t5_stackguard_action_inert_both_gates` and the in-crate `action_detects_but_does_not_mutate_when_on` (both assert the CFG is untouched with the gate on, flagged to flip to `1` + one fewer edge when the seam lands).
- restoration criteria: when `Funcdata::removeBranch` + `Funcdata::removeUnreachableBlocks` land (the W4/W8 funcdata_block wave that also restores LOSS-035/036/078), the two calls drop into `apply` at the marked point and `return 1` is restored; the two stackguard tests flip their expectation to a mutated CFG.

## LOSS-099: the `OptionDynamicHashMax` / `OptionStackGuard` ArchOption decision bodies are ported, but wiring them into the option database / console dispatch is a later wave; `DynamicHash::unique_hash` reads the collision budget as a parameter rather than inline from `Architecture::dynamic_hash_maxdup_high`
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: deferral (option-registration + flag-threading seam; default-equivalent)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/kuna_dynamichashmax.cc:14-21 (`OptionDynamicHashMax::apply` writes `glb->dynamic_hash_maxdup_high`) and kuna_stackguard.cc:157-164 (`OptionStackGuard::apply` writes `glb->strip_stack_guard`); dynamic.cc:438/503 read `fd->getArch()->dynamic_hash_maxdup_high` *inline* inside `uniqueHash` to pick `maxduplicates` (8 vs 16). kuna's runtime default is ON (16): architecture.cc:1438 `dynamic_hash_maxdup_high = true` (DIV-3, GH-8467).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/kuna_dynamichashmax.rs (`DynamicHashMaxOption::apply`/`max_duplicates`) and kuna_stackguard.rs (`StackGuardOption::apply`) port the decision bodies; the live arch flags exist (architecture.rs:250 `dynamic_hash_maxdup_high`, :271 `strip_stack_guard`, with the DIV-3 default-on at :413). `DynamicHash::unique_hash`/`unique_hash_op` (dynamic.rs:696,:759) take `maxduplicates: uint4` as a parameter instead of reading the arch flag inline (SEAM(W4): the `Funcdata::get_arch` seam does not carry kuna analysis flags — same gate-threading the sibling kuna Actions/Rules use).
- surface: NONE on the regression suite (no upstream test exercises >8 same-address collisions, so budget 8 vs 16 is byte-identical there). The divergences-that-could-matter are both seam-internal: (a) registering the two new `ArchOption` subclasses into the option-name dispatch table + console (`kuna_console.cc:232` reads `dynamic_hash_maxdup_high` for status) is the options/console wave's surface, not this pack's; (b) the call site `buildDynamicSymbol` (funcdata_varnode.cc:1318) that feeds the resolved budget into `unique_hash` is out of this item's scope. The `DynamicHashMaxOption::default()` models the *option-class* neutral state (off=8); the runtime arch default is on=16 and is set in architecture.rs, matching kuna-C++.
- restoration criteria: when the option database / console dispatch wave lands, the two `ArchOption` subclasses register by name and the console status query reads the flags; when `buildDynamicSymbol` is ported it threads `arch.dynamic_hash_maxdup_high ? 16 : 8` into `unique_hash`. No behavior change is expected — only the wiring closes.

## LOSS-100: blockaction `negateCondition` ports only the edge-swap (topology); the data-flow op-flag flip + change-count are deferred
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: deferral
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/block.cc:294 (`FlowBlock::negateCondition` base = `swapEdges(); return false`), block.hh:547 (`BlockCopy::negateCondition` = `copy->negateCondition(true)` → the underlying `BlockBasic` CBRANCH `boolean_flip`/`fallthru_true` op-flag flip), block.cc:2355 (`BlockBasic::negateCondition`). Called from the rules at blockaction.cc:1359/1363/1401/1462/1505/1540/1567.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/blockaction.rs:1416 (`negate_condition` does `graph.swap_edges(bl)` then returns `false`).
- surface: In the real C++ collapse the `sblocks` graph is `BlockCopy` nodes, so `negateCondition` resolves to `BlockBasic::negateCondition` and flips the underlying CBRANCH op flag and returns whether a data-flow change was made. The Rust port matches only the `FlowBlock` *base* (swap edges, return `false`), so (a) `dataflow_changecount` is never incremented in the standalone path — `CollapseStructure::get_change_count()` is always 0, and the `count += collapse.getChangeCount()` in `ActionBlockStructure` adds nothing; (b) the CBRANCH op-flag flip on `bblocks` is never applied. The *topology* swap that the collapse rules key on (true/false out-edge ordering) IS applied, so the structuring decisions are faithful; only the down-stream data-flow/print effect of the flip and its change-count are missing.
- why: the op-flag flip needs the op bank / cross-arena `bblocks` CBRANCH that `block.rs` left as `// SEAM(W7)`; not available at this item's boundary.
- restoration criteria: thread the `BlockCopy`→`BlockBasic` CBRANCH op-flag flip (and the `BlockCondition` opcode flip) through the op bank so `negate_condition` returns the true data-flow-change bool, then drop the always-`false` return.

## LOSS-101: blockaction `isComplex` returns the `FlowBlock` base default (`true`), not the `BlockCopy`→`BlockBasic` statement count
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: deferral
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/block.hh:254 (`FlowBlock::isComplex` base = `true`), block.hh:549 (`BlockCopy::isComplex` = `copy->isComplex()`), block.cc:2403 (`BlockBasic::isComplex` counts statements against `glb->max_implied_ref`). Consumed at blockaction.cc:1342 (`ruleBlockOr`) and 1538 (`ruleBlockWhileDo`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/blockaction.rs:1435 (`is_complex` returns `true`).
- surface: in the real collapse `isComplex` on a `BlockCopy` resolves to the underlying basic block's statement count, so it is usually *false* for a short condition block. The always-`true` port (a) makes `ruleBlockOr` always hit `if (orblock->isComplex()) continue;` → `ruleBlockOr`/`collapseConditions` never collapses an OR/AND condition; and (b) makes `ruleBlockWhileDo` always take `overflow = true`, which inverts the `(i==0)!=overflow` negate choice and unconditionally calls `setOverflowSyntax()` on every structured while/do. Both are observable output divergences in the standalone engine.
- why: the statement-count `BlockBasic::isComplex` needs the op intrusive list + `max_implied_ref`, left as `// SEAM(W7)` in `block.rs`.
- restoration criteria: port `BlockBasic::isComplex` (op-list statement count vs `max_implied_ref`) and route `BlockCopy::isComplex` through it.

## LOSS-107: `VariableGroup::pieceSet` duplicate-(offset,size) guard is dead — the `PieceKey` id-tiebreak makes the BTreeSet unable to reproduce `std::set<VariablePiece*,PieceCompareByOffset>` dedup, so `addPiece`'s "Duplicate VariablePiece" throw never fires
- date: 2026-06-13
- item: w7-s6-variable-cover
- kind: exception->Result parity divergence on a precondition-violation path (LOSS-089 defensive-guard precedent)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/variable.cc:33-39 (`PieceCompareByOffset::operator()` compares only offset then size), :43-52 (`VariableGroup::addPiece`: `if (!pieceSet.insert(piece).second) throw LowlevelError("Duplicate VariablePiece")`), :78-89 (`combineGroups` relies on the throw — "There must be no matching VariablePieces with the same size and offset … or a LowlevelError exception is thrown").
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/variable.rs:174-202 (`PieceKey::cmp` appends a unique `id` after (offset,size) so two distinct pieces with the same (offset,size) are NOT equal keys), :1476-1495 (`group_add_piece`: `BTreeSet::insert` therefore always returns `true`, so the `Err("Duplicate VariablePiece")` branch is unreachable).
- surface: only on a precondition violation — two genuinely-overlapping pieces with identical (offset,size) being combined into one group via `groupWith`'s `(Some,Some)` `combineGroups` path (the `mergeGroups` path explicitly *handles* such duplicates, so it is unaffected). On that input the C++ aborts the action with a `LowlevelError` (action restart/recover); the Rust silently inserts a second set member, leaving the group with a duplicate that `updateIntersections`/`combineGroups` then iterate over. The normal merge flow maintains the no-duplicate invariant, so neither side hits it on well-formed input; the gate is green. Pinned by `tests/verify_w7_s6_variable_cover.rs::group_with_equal_offset_size_should_error_like_cpp_but_does_not` (asserts the live `Ok(())`, flagged to flip to `is_err()` when restored).
- why: ADR 0002 transcribes the pointer-keyed `std::set` to a `BTreeSet<PieceKey>`; to keep distinct pieces with equal (offset,size) addressable by id (the `pieceSet.find(piece)` in `mergeGroups` keys on offset/size, but the set must still hold the right *count* of members), the port made `PieceKey` totally ordered by appending the id. That choice is what removes the (offset,size)-collision rejection. Classified MINOR/loss by the LOSS-089 precedent (a defensive guard whose divergence only manifests on input the C++ also rejects, never silent corruption of well-formed output).
- restoration criteria: have `group_add_piece` reject (return `Err("Duplicate VariablePiece")`) when the group already contains a piece with the same (offset,size) — e.g. probe `find_by_offset_size(offset,size)` before insert — restoring the C++ `std::set` dedup semantics; then flip the witness test to expect `is_err()`.

## LOSS-108: `RangeHint::isConstAbsorbable` widens the `highind * getAlignSize()` product to 64-bit before multiplying, where C++ multiplies `int4 * int4` (32-bit, truncating) then widens
- date: 2026-06-13
- item: w7-s6-varmap
- kind: integer-width divergence on int32-overflow (unreachable on well-formed frames)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/varmap.cc:46-47 (`if (highind > 0) end += highind * type->getAlignSize();` — `highind` is `int4`, `getAlignSize()` returns `int4` (type.hh:245), so the product is computed at 32 bits and only then promoted to `intb` for `end +=`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/varmap.rs:172 (`end = end.wadd((self.highind as intb).wmul(self.type_.get_align_size() as intb));` — both operands widened to `intb`/`i64` *before* the `wmul`, so the product is the full 64-bit value).
- surface: only when `highind * alignSize >= 2^31`. C++ truncates the product to its low 32 bits (e.g. `0x10000 * 0x10000` → `0`), making `end` small/negative and flipping the `b->sstart > end` absorbability test versus the Rust wide value. On a real stack frame `alignSize <= 8`, so this needs `highind >= 2^28` (~268M array elements on the stack) — an index no real frame produces, so neither side reaches it on well-formed input and the gate is green. Pinned by `tests/verify_w7_s6_varmap_r3.rs::r3_const_absorbable_int4_mul_overflow_is_wide_in_rust` (asserts the current Rust wide branch) and the 32-bit-safe agreement by `::r3_const_absorbable_highind_mul_boundary_32bit_safe`.
- why: the porter applied ADR-0003's `w*` widening uniformly; the C++ here is an unwidened `int4 * int4`, so to be byte-faithful on overflow the multiply would have to be done at i32 (`(self.highind.wmul(get_align_size())) as intb`). Classified MINOR/loss (unreachable on well-formed frames, same pathological-frame class as the now-repaired F2; never corrupts well-formed output).
- restoration criteria: compute the product at i32 (`(self.highind as int4).wmul(self.type_.get_align_size())` then `as intb`) to reproduce the C++ 32-bit truncation, and flip the witness test to assert `!is_const_absorbable` on the degenerate overflow input.

Add an install-side test that asserts a `switch` is emitted from the recorded hint.

## LOSS-111: the lowered-switch DETECT pass iterates `var_count` / `cmpmap` in slotmap creation order, not C++ raw-pointer order — a deterministic divergence in two tie-break-sensitive picks (LOSS-097 class)
- date: 2026-06-13
- item: w7-s7-kuna-loweredswitch
- kind: faithful-port iteration-order divergence (Rust is *more* deterministic than the C++ pointer order it ports; output-equivalent except on spurious-cascade ties)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/kuna_loweredswitch.cc:455-462 (`std::map<Varnode *,int4> varCount`; the switch-variable pick `if ((*hit).second > bestCount) { bestCount=...; swvar=(*hit).first; }` — strict `>`, so the FIRST var at the max count in `std::map<Varnode*>` POINTER order wins on a tie) and :454,:481-490 (`std::map<BlockBasic *,CmpNode> cmpmap`; the head pick `for(mit...) { if isChild ... continue; head=(*mit).first; break; }` — the FIRST non-child cascade node in `std::map<BlockBasic*>` POINTER order). Raw `Varnode *`/`BlockBasic *` pointer order is non-deterministic across runs/allocators.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/kuna_loweredswitch.rs:723,758-762 (`var_count: BTreeMap<VarnodeId,int4>`; the same strict-`>` pick) and :722,798-807 (`cmpmap: BTreeMap<BlockId,CmpNode>`; the same first-non-child head pick). `VarnodeId`/`BlockId` are slotmap `new_key_type!` keys whose `Ord` is `(idx,version)` (slotmap-1.1.1 src/lib.rs:245 `#[derive(...,Ord)]`), i.e. ARENA CREATION ORDER on a fresh map — deterministic, but NOT the C++ allocation-pointer order.
- surface: on a genuine GCC-lowered switch there is exactly ONE dispatch variable and the cascade head is unique, so neither tie fires and the recovered record is identical. The divergence is only observable when two distinct varnodes tie at the same (>1) cascade-node count, or when multiple disconnected non-child cascade nodes exist for the chosen variable — both spurious/overlapping-cascade shapes, not real lowered switches. In those cases C++ picks by (non-deterministic) pointer order and the Rust port picks deterministically by creation order. Pinned by verify_w7_s7_kuna_loweredswitch.rs::v1_var_count_tie_is_deterministic_first_created_wins (two equal-size cascades on two switch variables; the earlier-created variable's cascade is the one recorded).
- why: the C++ map comparator here is the default `std::less<ptr>` (raw allocation-pointer order), which is impossible and undesirable to reproduce; the slotmap-key `BTreeMap` order is the codebase's standard determinism contract (same choice as LOSS-097's `KunaNodeOrder`). Recorded as a loss only because it is a literal divergence from the C++ map comparator; behaviorally it is strictly more deterministic with no change on well-formed lowered switches.
- restoration criteria: none required — this is the intended end state (deterministic over non-deterministic). If a future install-side consumer makes the tie-break observable in output, re-check the creation-order choice against a concrete C++ oracle on the same input.

The transcription is what is verified; real-pipeline correctness depends on the future `Funcdata` `MergeContext` impl honoring each seam's documented contract.
- restoration criteria: the W7/W8 wave adds `impl MergeContext for Funcdata`, after which the merge passes run on real IR; re-verify against a C++ oracle decompilation (the datatest corpus) at that point.

## LOSS-113: `Merge::mergeLinear`'s `compareHighByBlock` sort is a STABLE `sort_by`, where the C++ uses unstable `std::sort` — for two distinct highs that compare fully-equal (same earliest-cover-block, same first-instance address, same/both-null defining-op address) the Rust preserves input order while `std::sort` may reorder, which can change the `mergeLinear` stack-representative pick
- date: 2026-06-13
- item: w7-s6-merge
- kind: faithful-port comparator-tie / sort-stability divergence (Rust is *more* deterministic; LOSS-097/111 class)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/merge.cc:282 (`sort(highvec.begin(),highvec.end(),compareHighByBlock)` — `std::sort`, unstable) with the comparator decompiler/cpp/merge.hh:152-174 (`compareHighByBlock`): equal cover `compareTo`, equal first-instance `getAddr()`, and equal/both-null `getDef()->getAddr()` yield a "not-less in either direction" tie among distinct highs.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/merge.rs:1111-1121 (`sort_high_by_block` reconstructs `Ordering` from the two-direction `compare_high_by_block` and feeds `highvec.sort_by` — a STABLE sort).
- surface: only observable when two genuinely distinct HighVariables in the same `mergeByDatatype` type-group compare fully-equal under the three-key chain — uncommon, since highs entering `mergeLinear` generally have distinct earliest-cover blocks. On a tie the downstream `mergeLinear` stack pick (which high becomes the merge target) can differ from a given libstdc++ `std::sort`. The other `compareCopyByInVarnode` sort (merge.cc:1308) is NOT affected: its tie-break runs down to the unique `SeqNum::order`, so no ties exist and stable-vs-unstable is irrelevant. The `compareByHigh` raw-pointer sort in `snipOutputInterference` (merge.cc:822) is a seam (LOSS-112) but is order-insensitive for output (it only groups inputs by high to allocate one COPY per distinct high).
- why: `std::sort`'s order among equal elements is unspecified by the C++ standard; the Rust stable sort is a deterministic refinement (same rationale as LOSS-097's `KunaNodeOrder` and LOSS-111). Pinned by verify_w7_s6_merge.rs::w7_s6_merge_compare_high_by_block_tiebreaks (the null-def asymmetry and the strict-weak-order tie) and ::w7_s6_merge_by_datatype_preserves_discovery_order (group-at-a-time discovery-order preservation).
- restoration criteria: none required — deterministic-over-unspecified. If a concrete C++ oracle ever shows a tie producing a different merge target on real input, the stable choice would need re-derivation against that oracle's libstdc++ `std::sort` order.

## LOSS-115 (2026-06-13, W8 w8-s9-prettyprint)
`EmitMarkup` (the XML-markup low-level back-end) supports only the **packed**
encoder path. C++ `EmitMarkup::setPackedOutput(bool)` (prettyprint.cc:351-360)
swaps the bound `Encoder*` between a `PackedEncode` (default) and an `XmlEncode`
(unpacked). The Rust port wires `PackedEncode` only; `set_packed_output(false)`
records the flag (`self.packed`) but does not install an unpacked `XmlEncode`,
because the port's `PackedEncode` is stateless (holds only `&mut Vec<u8>`) and a
borrowing `XmlEncode` would need a persistent depth/tag-status encoder threaded
through every `with_encoder` call.
- surface: `print C xml` after an explicit `option ... ` selecting unpacked XML
  markup output. The only markup path the datatests / corpus exercise is the
  **packed** default, which the port reproduces byte-for-byte (PackedEncode is
  stateless, so per-call fresh encoders append the identical stream a single
  persistent encoder would — validated by the verifier's markup tests). The
  unpacked XmlEncode rendering is never on a tested path.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/prettyprint.cc:351-360 (`EmitMarkup::setPackedOutput`),
  342-349 (`setOutputStream` default `PackedEncode`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/prettyprint.rs (`EmitMarkup::with_encoder`,
  `set_packed_output`; the type-level doc on `EmitMarkup`).
- restoration criteria: when an unpacked-XML markup datatest/corpus path is added
  (or `XmlEncode` gains a buffer-owning constructor in kuna-base), thread the
  `packed` flag through `with_encoder` to select `XmlEncode` vs `PackedEncode`,
  pinned against the C++ `print C xml` unpacked oracle; that item's verifier
  checks this entry off.

## LOSS-115: `simpleSignature`'s per-call `<call>` element list is dropped — the `<signatures>` body emits the flags + `<sig>` features but omits the trailing `<call space=.. offset=..>` elements, because `Funcdata::numCalls`/`getCallSpecs` (the W4 FuncCallSpecs surface) is not threaded onto the W3 `Funcdata`
- date: 2026-06-13
- item: w8-sig-pack
- kind: deferral (W4 call-spec surface seam; partial-port of `simpleSignature`)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/signature.cc:1119-1129 (`uint4 numcalls = fd->numCalls(); for(...) { FuncCallSpecs *fc = fd->getCallSpecs(i); const Address &addr(fc->getEntryAddress()); if (!addr.isInvalid()) { openElement(ELEM_CALL); writeSpace(ATTRIB_SPACE, addr.getSpace()); writeUnsignedInteger(ATTRIB_OFFSET, addr.getOffset()); closeElement(ELEM_CALL); } }` — the `<call>` elements are written AFTER the `<sig>` list and before `</signatures>`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/signature.rs:1555-1558 (`// SEAM(W4)` — `let _ = (&ATTRIB_INDEX, &ELEM_CALL);` keeps the ids referenced; the per-call loop is absent).
- surface: for a function with at least one non-invalid call entry address, the C++ `<signatures>` element contains one `<call>` child per such call; the Rust port emits none. The `<sig>` feature list (the actual signature payload) is byte-identical; only the call-site address annotations are missing. Functions with no calls (or only invalid-address calls) encode identically. The `save signatures`/`debugSignature` (`<signaturedesc>`) path does NOT use `<call>` and is unaffected.
- why: `Funcdata::numCalls()`/`getCallSpecs(i)->getEntryAddress()` reach the `FuncCallSpecs` list (W4 `fspec` call-spec subsystem), which is not yet attached to the W3 arena `Funcdata` in the merged tree (same class of gap as the other W4 callspec seams). The ELEM_CALL/ATTRIB ids are ported and referenced so the future wiring is a body fill, not a re-derivation.
- restoration criteria: when W4 threads the `FuncCallSpecs` list onto `Funcdata`, port the `numCalls`/`getCallSpecs` loop into `simple_signature` (after the `<sig>` loop, before `close_element(ELEM_SIGNATURES)`), pinned against signature.cc:1119-1129, and add a test asserting one `<call>` per non-invalid call entry.

## LOSS-116: `IfcPrintSignatures` (`print signatures`) renders the SORTED bare-hash list, not the C++ `SigManager::print` order/format — the per-feature origin string (`Varnode::printRaw` / `FlowBlock::printHeader`) and the unsorted `sigs`-vector emission order are dropped (W8 print surface)
- date: 2026-06-13
- item: w8-sig-pack
- kind: deferral (W8 print surface seam; human-readable `print signatures` console output only)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/signature.cc:719-725 (`SigManager::print` iterates `sigs` in INSERTION order — block sigs then varnode sigs, NOT sorted) and :62-68 (`Signature::print`: `s << '*'; printOrigin(s); s << " = 0x" << hex << setw(8) << setfill('0') << sig`), where `printOrigin` is overridden per feature kind: `VarnodeSignature::printOrigin` -> `vn->printRaw(s)` (signature.hh:188), `BlockSignature::printOrigin` -> `bl->printHeader(s)` (signature.hh:206), `CopySignature::printOrigin` -> `"Copies in " + bl->printHeader(s)` (signature.cc:661-666).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/analyzesigs.rs:117-124 (`print_signatures` builds the lines from `smanage.get_signature_vector()` — the SORTED hash list — and emits `* = 0x{h:08x}` with no origin string; the doc-comment records the `SEAM(W8)` for `printOrigin`).
- surface: the `print signatures` console command output differs in (1) ORDER — C++ prints in `sigs`-vector order (block-index/op order, then create-index order), Rust prints hash-ascending; and (2) the per-line ORIGIN prefix — C++ prints `*<origin> = 0x........` where `<origin>` is the rendered Varnode/block header, Rust prints `* = 0x........` (bare `*`). The hash VALUES printed are the same set (just reordered). The XML `save`/`saveall`/`debug` encode paths (`<gensig>`/`<varsig>`/`<blocksig>`/`<copysig>`) are NOT affected by this — they go through `encode`, and the `<varsig>`/`<blocksig>` encodes are themselves separately seamed.
- why: `Varnode::printRaw` and `FlowBlock::printHeader` are the W8 print/render plane (the same `// SEAM(W8)` the print module skeletons carry); without them a faithful `printOrigin` cannot be produced, so the port emits the hashes it does have. The sorted order was chosen so the SEAMed output is at least deterministic.
- restoration criteria: when the W8 print surface lands (`Varnode::printRaw`, `FlowBlock::printHeader`), restore `SigManager::print`'s insertion-order walk and route each feature through a kind-dispatched `print_origin` (pinned against signature.cc:62-68 + the three `printOrigin` overrides), dropping the sorted-hash fallback.

- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/coreaction_render.rs — each `apply` transcribes its C++ body as a `// SEAM(W8-funcdata)`-noted comment (same iteration order, branch structure, and `count += 1` points) and returns 0. Each de-seamed body is pinned to its `coreaction.cc` anchor above; the verifier of that item checks this entry off.  Verifier hunt list for this item: clean — the only realized arithmetic is the i32 (`int4`) counter zeroing/gating (no signedness/width/wrap exposure since the increments live in the unrealized bodies), no HashMap/HashSet/sort_unstable/partial_cmp, no bare `as`, no `todo!`/`unimplemented!`; clippy clean; the four-module union (early∪protos∪cleanup∪render) covers exactly the 63 live `Action` classes with no gap or overlap (the 3 commented-out upstream classes correctly excluded).  Pinned by 4 adversarial tests in rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w8_s9_coreaction_render.rs (ctor name+flags partition with a non-schedule group token, `reset` non-chaining vs default base-reset, seam totality under 16x repetition incl. both StackPtrFlow stack/no-stack paths, and `clone_filtered` independence + parameterization carry-through).

Otherwise permanent-by-design.

## LOSS-123: IfaceTerm raw-terminal setup (`__TERMINAL__` tcgetattr/tcsetattr) unported — interactive single-keystroke editing only
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: paradigm-degradation
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/ifaceterm.hh:40-44 (is_terminal/ifd/itty members), ifaceterm.cc:24-50 (ctor raw-mode tcgetattr/tcsetattr) and 61-65 (dtor tcsetattr restore); all under `#ifdef __TERMINAL__`

- surface: live-terminal-only. The C++ ctor itself takes the `errno==ENOTTY -> is_terminal=false; return` branch whenever stdin is a pipe (kuna's only drive mode: `kuna/decompile.py` and the datatest runner pipe stdin), so the raw-mode setup never runs under the harness. The deterministic batch path — the full `IfaceTerm::readLine` editing/echo/redraw switch, history scroll, and TAB `doCompletion` — IS ported and pinned (interface.rs/ifaceterm.rs tests incl. verifier adversarial tests). Only true-TTY single-keystroke delivery (arrow keys / live editing on a real terminal) is dropped, and on a pipe even C++ does not enable it.
- why: termios raw-mode is interactive ergonomics with no test/oracle surface; every kuna oracle path drives the console over a pipe where C++ takes the non-terminal branch anyway. Reproducing tcsetattr would add OS-specific FFI for behavior no harness can observe.
- restoration criteria: only if an interactive `decomp_dbg` over a real TTY becomes a tested target; then add a termios raw-mode shim in IfaceTerm construction guarded on isatty(), with restore-on-drop, validated by an expect-style interactive test. The deterministic readLine logic needs no change.

## LOSS-124: `grammar.rs::parse_c_integer` full-string-parses the C-declaration NUMBER token where C++ `istringstream >> intb` (with `unsetf(dec|hex|oct)`) PREFIX-parses, so lexer-accepted letter/underscore-suffixed integers (`1z`, `123abc`, `08`, `5_0`, `007_0`) diverge — and the port's doc-comment falsely claims faithfulness "for every reachable input" (w9-con-grammar REJECT F1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: divergence (integer parse — trailing-garbage / bad-base-digit truncation). **NOT an accepted loss at filing time** — recorded by the verifier for the porter's work order; the REJECT requires a faithful prefix-parse fix (see restoration criteria), or an explicit team decision to downgrade to a ledgered minor loss with the false comment corrected.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/grammar.cc:1796-1804 (`GrammarToken::set(integer)`): `istringstream s(charstring); s.unsetf(ios::dec|ios::hex|ios::oct); intb val; s >> val; value.integer=(uintb)val;`. With the basefield cleared, integer `operator>>` does strtol-style auto-base extraction (0x/0X->hex, leading 0->octal, else decimal) and STOPS at the first character invalid for the detected base, leaving the parsed prefix. The lexer's `number` state (grammar.cc:2094-2125) accepts ASCII letters A-Z/a-z and `_` as "part of the number", so `1z`/`123abc`/`08`/`5_0`/`007_0` are single NUMBER tokens reaching this conversion.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-console/src/grammar.rs:235-259 `parse_c_integer`. It `trim()`s, strips an optional `-`/`+`, detects base from the prefix, then `i64::from_str_radix(digits, radix).unwrap_or(i64::MAX)` over the WHOLE remaining body. Because `from_str_radix` requires every char to be a valid digit, any trailing letter/underscore (or a non-octal digit after a leading 0) yields `i64::MAX` instead of the C++ prefix value. The doc-comment (grammar.rs:230-234) asserts "the lexer corpus never produces such values, so this matches the C++ for every reachable input" — this is false: the lexer accepts the letter/underscore-suffixed tokens above.
- divergence table (g++ -std=c++11 `istringstream + unsetf >> intb` oracle vs port): `"1z"` cpp=1 port=i64::MAX; `"123abc"` cpp=123 port=i64::MAX; `"08"` cpp=0 (octal, stops at '8') port=i64::MAX; `"5_0"` cpp=5 port=i64::MAX; `"007_0"` cpp=7 port=i64::MAX. Clean inputs all agree (`0`/`255`/`0xff`/`0777`/`-1`). Both saturate to i64::MAX on a true >i64 overflow (`0xffffffffffffffff`, `99999999999999999999`), so the overflow path coincidentally matches.
- surface: every NUMBER value in the C-declaration grammar — `direct_declarator '[' assignment_expression ']'` array size (`newArray((int4)*num)`), `enumerator '=' NUMBER` enum value, `struct_declarator ':' NUMBER` bitfield width. Observable end-to-end: `int4 a[1z]` builds a valid `int4[1]` in C++ (arraysize 1) but in the port `arraysize=(i64::MAX as int4)=-1` fails `ArrayModifier::isValid` (`arraysize>0`) so `parse_type` errs "Parsed type is invalid". `int4 a[08]` agrees by accident (both arraysize<=0 -> invalid). No in-tree datatest/proto corpus carries a letter-suffixed number, but unlike LOSS-051 (clean save-file decimal only) these tokens are ordinary console `parse line` input and the claim of universal faithfulness is wrong.
- why: the port chose a trim-then-full-parse shape rather than transcribing the strtol scan-the-prefix-and-stop loop — exactly the LOSS-051 (`adjustvma`) and LOSS-040/045 (options `>>`) integer-extraction family. The clean-input behavior is faithful; only the lexer-accepted garbage-suffixed inputs diverge.
- restoration criteria: replace `parse_c_integer`'s full-string `from_str_radix` with a leading-prefix scan mirroring `istringstream >>`/strtol — after sign + base detection, consume the longest valid leading digit run for the detected base and return its value (0 if no leading digit), discarding trailing chars; keep the target-width `(int4)` truncation at the `newArray`/`setNumBits` call sites and the i64-overflow saturation. Then flip the verifier's four `should_panic` tests (`w9_con_grammar_parse_int_{letter_suffix,trailing_alpha,bad_octal_digit,underscore}_diverges`) to assert the oracle values (`1z`->1, `123abc`->123, `08`->0, `5_0`->5) and update `w9_con_grammar_array_size_letter_suffix_diverges` to expect `int4 a[1z]` -> a valid `int4[1]`. Correct the grammar.rs:230-234 doc-comment. Verified by the w9-con-grammar verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-125: `CommandStream::read_int` (the `IfcHistory` `>> num >> ws` path) diverges from `std::istream >> int4` on overflow, sign-only-at-eof, and INT_MIN — all unreachable via the datatest/harness corpus
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: divergence (integer extraction — same family as LOSS-040/045/051/124). **Accepted-with-losses by the w9-con-interface verifier (round 2)** because every divergent input is unreachable through any oracle-tested path: the `history` command appears in zero datatests (grep-verified) and is not driven by the Python harness; it is interactive/diagnostic only.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/interface.cc:531-552 (`IfcHistory::execute`): `s >> num >> ws;` over an `istringstream`, then `if (num > getHistorySize()) num = getHistorySize();` and `for(int4 i=num-1;i>=0;--i)`. The `operator>>(int4&)` saturates to `INT_MAX`/`INT_MIN` and sets failbit on overflow, latches eofbit when the digit/sign run reaches the buffer end, and on a sign-with-no-digit leaves the get-pointer AFTER the consumed sign (does not rewind).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-console/src/interface.rs:264-299 (`CommandStream::read_int`) + interface.rs:555-584 (`IfcHistory::execute` `let mut i = num - 1`).
- divergence table (g++ -std=c++11 `istringstream >> int >> ws` oracle vs port, fed as the `history` arg remainder):
  - `"999999999999"` (overflow): cpp num=2147483647 (prints ALL history) — port `read_int` returns **0** (`i64::parse` errs -> `unwrap_or(0)`), prints NOTHING.
  - `"+"` / `"-"` (sign, no digit, at end): cpp consumes the sign, latches eofbit -> `IfcHistory`'s post-read `!s.eof()` is false -> **no throw**, num left at C++ garbage/0. Port REWINDS the sign (`self.pos = start`) so eof stays clear -> `skip_ws` finds the non-ws `+`/`-` -> `!eof` true -> raises **"Too many parameters to history"**.
  - `"-2147483648"` (INT_MIN): cpp `num-1` wraps to INT_MAX (loop prints stale lines). Port `num - 1` is a checked subtraction -> **debug-build panic** ("attempt to subtract with overflow"); `cargo test` runs debug. (Release would wrap and match.)
  - Clean small counts all agree (`"5"`->5, `"2"`->2, `"-3"`->-3, `"5 junk"`->throw num=5, `"junk"`->throw num=0, `"+x"`->throw num=0). The doc-comment at interface.rs:293-294 ("C++ wraps/saturates the same input the same way for the small history counts in scope") is accurate for the in-scope small counts but its "saturates the same" wording over-claims for the overflow case.
- surface: only `IfcHistory` (the `history [N]` console command). Zero datatest / harness exposure. The `openfile`/`closefile`/`echo`/`quit` base commands (the harness-critical `print C` capture path) are unaffected and ported faithfully.
- why: `read_int` was written as parse-the-token-then-`.parse::<int4>()` rather than transcribing the libstdc++ `num_get` scan (saturate-on-overflow, no-rewind sign, eof-on-run-to-end), and `IfcHistory` uses a plain `num - 1`; the faithful-but-unreachable inputs were not exercised. Same shape as the LOSS-124 grammar integer family.
- restoration criteria: make `read_int` saturate to `int4::MAX`/`MIN` on digit overflow (not return 0), leave the get-pointer after a consumed sign without rewinding (latching eof if that reaches the end), and change `IfcHistory`'s `num - 1` to `wrapping_sub(1)`. Then flip the three verifier round-2 tests (`verify_w9_r2_read_int_overflow_returns_zero_not_intmax`, `verify_w9_r2_read_int_sign_only_no_eof_latch`, `verify_w9_r2_history_intmin_arg_subtraction_overflows`) to assert the C++ oracle values. Verified by the w9-con-interface verifier (round 2).

## LOSS-124 RESOLUTION NOTE (appended by w9-con-grammar verifier, round 2)
- date: 2026-06-13
- status: **RESOLVED**. The w9-con-grammar repair commit (752184b) replaced `parse_c_integer`'s full-string `from_str_radix` with a leading-prefix scanner (optional whitespace skip, optional sign, auto-base detection `0x`/`0X`->hex / leading-0->octal / else decimal, longest valid-digit run stopping at the first invalid char, per-sign signed saturation: positive overflow -> i64::MAX, negative -> i64::MIN), and corrected the false "matches the C++ for every reachable input" doc-comment. This is exactly the restoration criteria above.
- verification: round-2 verifier built a standalone `g++ -std=c++11` `istringstream + unsetf >> intb` oracle mirroring `GrammarToken::set(integer)` byte-for-byte and a 4000-token random differential corpus drawn from the lexer's number alphabet (digits, A-Z, a-z, `_`, `x`/`X`, leading `-`). The repaired `parse_c_integer` agrees with the oracle on all 4000 tokens AND on the hand-picked edges: `1z`->1, `123abc`->123, `08`->0, `5_0`->5, `007_0`->7, `0x`/`0X`/`0xx`->0, `0x10g5`->16, sign-on-hex (`-0x1`->-1, `-0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF`->i64::MIN), `-010`->-8, both-direction overflow saturation, exact i64::MIN/MAX boundaries, and leading-whitespace skip. Test: `grammar::tests::w9_con_grammar_v5_differential_against_cpp_oracle` (+ v1/v2/v3 and the flipped `w9_con_grammar_parse_int_*` tests). The end-to-end `int4 a[1z]` now builds a valid `int4[1]`.
- residual: the `(int4)` truncation at `new_array` (`num as int4`, grammar.rs:1427) and `set_num_bits` (`n as int4`, grammar.rs:1679) is faithful (low-32-bit two's-complement reinterpretation, matching C++ `(int4)*num`), verified end-to-end by `w9_con_grammar_v4_array_size_int4_truncation_zero_rejected` (`int4 a[4294967296]` truncates to 0 -> invalid; `int4 b[4294967297]` -> valid `int4[1]`).

The repair commit 752184b added `// cast:` notes inside `parse_c_integer` (grammar.rs:286-310) but not elsewhere.
- surface: none observable. The `as usize` index casts are on `int4` values the lexer guarantees non-negative (token-start invariants); `as uint4`/`as u8` re-tag ASCII bytes the lexer bounded to <127; `as int4` is the deliberate low-32-bit two's-complement truncation matching C++ `(int4)` (verified end-to-end by `w9_con_grammar_v4`). No divergence from C++ on any input.
- why: convention drift — the porter commented the casts in the file touched by the F1 repair but not the pre-existing ones in the lexer/AST.
- restoration criteria: add a `// cast:` justification to each bare `as` in grammar.rs (or a single module-level `// casts in this file are all faithful re-tags / bounded indices; see ...` waiver the verification protocol accepts), then drop this loss. No behavioral change.

## LOSS-127: `ifacedecomp.rs` engine-touching command bodies routed through `engine_unavailable` — every command's post-guard engine call (the decompile drive, the print stack, the parse grammars, the Architecture loader/types/context/ArchOptionContext surface) is unwired pending the Architecture integration W-item (w9-con-ifacedecomp ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: cross-item dependency (deferred engine integration). **Accepted-with-losses by the w9-con-ifacedecomp verifier (round 1)** because the missing surface is genuinely not exposed by the merged rust-port tree (verified below), the port faithfully reproduces every part expressible against the merged API, and the gap is single-site wireable when the integration item lands.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/ifacedecomp.cc — the engine calls at the tail of essentially every `Ifc*::execute`: `IfcDecompile` (889) `allacts.getCurrent()->reset/perform`; `IfcPrintCStruct`/`Flat`/`Globals`/`Types`/`Xml` (923-987) `dcp->conf->print->docFunction`/`docAllGlobals`; `IfcOption` (304) `dcp->conf->options->set(...)`; `IfcParseFile`/`IfcParseLine` (347,384) `parse_C`; the whole `map`/`force`/`override`/`set`/`global`/`volatile`/`readonly`/`comment instruction` family `parse_machaddr`/`parse_type`/`Scope::add*`; `IfcReadSymbols`/`IfcAdjustVma`/`IfcFuncload`/`IfcAddrrangeLoad` the `Architecture::loader` surface; `IfcSetcontextrange`/`IfcSettrackedrange` `Architecture::context`; the graph/produce/callgraph/test commands their own subsystems.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-console/src/ifacedecomp.rs — `engine_unavailable(entry)` (ifacedecomp.rs:175) returns `IfaceError::execution("engine integration not yet ported: <entry> ...")`; ~80 command bodies end in `Err(engine_unavailable(...))`. `render_engine_error` (ifacedecomp.rs:1786, the ParseError/RecovError/LowlevelError/DecoderError catch arms of C++ `execute`) is correspondingly unreachable today (no command body lets a KunaError escape).
- surface: large but uniform. **What IS ported and pinned** (verified faithful, round 1): the 105-token `registerCom` sequence byte-identical in order+content (positional diff vs ifacedecomp.cc:37-147, minus the console-only `source` and the CPUI_RULECOMPILE/OPACTION_DEBUG/TYPEPROP_DEBUG-gated commands — all correctly absent); every command's argument-parse ORDER and its pre-engine `IfaceParseError`/`IfaceExecutionError` text and null-guard (`conf`/`fd`/`cgraph`/`testCollection`) ORDER (conf-then-fd where C++ does so; `break action`/`break start` empty-arg as IfaceExecutionError not parse); the `IfcDecompile` "No code for <name>"/"Clearing old decompilation"/"Decompiling <name>" multi-line output up to the drive; the `execute()` exception->prefix grammar for the three reachable IfaceError kinds + the transcribed engine-error arms with their `abortFunction` side effects; `mainloop` pop-script driver. **What is dropped**: the actual engine effect of every command (the decompiled C, the disassembly, symbol-table mutations, option application, the "Decompilation complete"/"Break at .." trailer) and the handful of *pre-engine* errors that sit behind an integer/grammar read routed into the loss (`IfcOption` "Too many option parameters" is ported; `IfcSetcontextrange` "Missing context value", `IfcMapconvert`'s post-format reads, the `IfcParseFile` "Unable to open file" are behind the loss boundary). Consequence: **no datatest runs end-to-end through this Rust console** — `print C`/`decompile`/`load function`/`map addr`/`option .. on` all resolve+dispatch (verified: never "Invalid command") then hit `engine_unavailable`. The datatest parity signal for the deep decompiler is carried by the C++ `decomp_test_dbg`, not this port, until the integration item lands.
- merged-tree verification (why this is a real gap, not a porting shortcut): in rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/architecture.rs the merged `Architecture` exposes `options: OptionDatabase` and `allacts: ActionDatabase` as public fields but provides NO `print`/`types`/`loader`/`context` accessor and does NOT implement `ArchOptionContext` (grep: only test-only `RecordingContext` (options/tests.rs:59) and `Ctx` (tests/verify_w4_fw_options.rs:47) impl it). `OptionDatabase::set` (options.rs:1930) requires `&mut dyn ArchOptionContext`, so `IfcOption` genuinely cannot call it against the real architecture. The address/C grammars (`parse_machaddr`, `parse_C`, `parse_type`, `parse_protopieces`) have no ported entry point on the merged surface. The loss claim is accurate.
- why: the W9 console item was scoped (checklist depends_on w9-con-interface + W8-gate) to land the command *surface* — registration, parse order, diagnostics, driver — ahead of the Architecture-level integration layer that ties the engine internals (lift/flow/SSA/print stack, all already merged as building blocks) into the `Architecture` public API the console commands call. Faithfully porting the parse/guard/string surface now, with the engine calls fail-closed behind a self-describing `IfaceExecutionError`, keeps the port faithful-where-expressible and makes each engine site a single-line wire-up later, rather than blocking the whole console behind the integration item.
- restoration criteria: when the Architecture-integration W-item adds `print()`/`types()`/`loader()`/`context()` accessors + an `impl ArchOptionContext for Architecture`, ports the `parse_machaddr`/`parse_varnode` (pcodeparse) and `parse_C`/`parse_type`/`parse_protopieces` (grammar) console entry points, and assembles the `allacts.getCurrent()->reset/perform` decompile drive + loader-backed `followFlow`, replace each `engine_unavailable(<entry>)` with the real call (the `<entry>` string names the exact C++ target at each site), restore the pre-engine errors currently behind the loss boundary (`IfcSetcontextrange` "Missing context value", `IfcParseFile` "Unable to open file: ", `IfcMapconvert`/`IfcMapunionfacet` post-token validation), wire `render_engine_error` into the `execute()` catch path for escaping `KunaError`s, and re-pin against the upstream datatest corpus end-to-end (the `print C`/`decompile` parity oracle). No change to the registration/parse/guard/driver structure is needed.

## LOSS-128: `IfcCodeDataTarget` (`codedata target <name>`) drops the BFD import-table lookup — the `LoadImageBfd::getImportTable` scan is unported, so the command takes the C++ not-found branch for EVERY name (w9-con-codedata ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: dependency-substitution (libbfd unported; specific instance of LOSS-005)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/codedata.cc:732-754 `IfcCodeDataTarget::execute` — after `s>>ws`/eof-guard/`s>>token`, C++ does `LoadImageBfd *loadbfd = (LoadImageBfd *)dcp->conf->loader; loadbfd->getImportTable(irec);` then linear-searches `irec[i].funcname == token`; on a hit it calls `codedata->addTarget(irec[i].funcname, irec[i].thunkaddress, 1)`, on a miss prints "Unable to find reference to call <token>". The file's own header note marks the Target command as BFD/GPL-3.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-console/src/codedata.rs:1254-1266 `IfcCodeDataTarget::execute` — the `s.skip_ws()`/`eof`→IfaceParseError("Missing system call name")/`read_token` parse surface is faithful; the BFD lookup is replaced by a comment ("No BFD loader in the Rust port -> the import table is empty -> the loop falls through to the not-found branch for every token") and the command unconditionally emits `status.file_out("Unable to find reference to call <token>\n")`.
- surface: the `codedata target <name>` console command — it can never register a target via the import table, so a subsequent `codedata run` finds no target hits sourced from `target`. No datatest exercises `codedata target` (grep-verified: `codedata` appears in zero `decompiler/datatests/*.xml`); the command is interactive/diagnostic. The `CodeDataAnalysis::add_target` analysis path itself IS fully ported and is driven directly by the tests (`run_model_target_hits_sorted_and_dumped`), so target-hit detection is faithful when a target is registered through the API rather than the BFD table.
- why: `loadimage_bfd.{cc,hh}` (`LoadImageBfd`/`ImportRecord`/`getImportTable`) is GPL-3 and is not part of the kuna Rust port (no `ImportRecord`/`LoadImageBfd` type exists on the merged surface, same boundary as LOSS-005). The "Unable to find reference" branch is the exact C++ behavior for the empty-import-table case, so the observable output for any name is byte-identical to the C++ miss path.
- restoration criteria: when the W11 loader item (LOSS-005, `object`-crate ELF loader) exposes an import/relocation table on the console `Architecture::loader` surface, replace the unconditional not-found path with the linear `funcname == token` scan and the `addTarget(name, thunkaddress, 1)` hit branch; re-pin against the C++ oracle on a binary with a known PLT/import (the only input that distinguishes the hit branch). The parse/eof/error surface needs no change.

## LOSS-129: `codedata init` / `codedata run` command bodies are stubbed to an execution error pending the `decompile` IfaceData/`Architecture` wiring — the analysis (`CodeDataAnalysis::init`/`run_model`) is fully ported but the two commands cannot reach the `Architecture` (w9-con-codedata ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: cross-item dependency (deferred engine integration; same class as LOSS-127)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/codedata.cc:718-760 — `IfaceCodeDataCommand::setData` caches `dcp = (IfaceDecompData *)status->getData("decompile")`; `IfcCodeDataInit::execute` (726) calls `codedata->init(dcp->conf)` and `IfcCodeDataRun::execute` (756) calls `codedata->runModel()` (which reads `glb->loader`/`glb->translate`/`getDefaultCodeSpace` cached by init).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-console/src/codedata.rs:1224-1288 — `no_architecture(what)` (1224) returns `IfaceError::execution("No architecture loaded (codedata <what> needs the 'decompile' module data)")`; `IfcCodeDataInit::execute` (1234) and `IfcCodeDataRun::execute` (1279) return it. The `CodeDataAnalysis::init(&dyn Translate)` and `run_model(&dyn Translate, &mut dyn LoadImage, &AddrSpaceManager)` methods are fully ported and tested directly (`run_model_straightline_with_call`, `run_model_target_hits_sorted_and_dumped`, `init_resets_all_state`).
- surface: the `codedata init` and `codedata run` console commands (the only two that need the `Architecture`). The five `dump` commands and `target` do NOT need it and are wired through the `codedata` module's own `IfaceData` (`codedata_mut`). The structural deviation (engine `Translate`/`LoadImage`/`AddrSpaceManager` threaded per call rather than cached as `glb`) is documented in the module header; the computation is byte-faithful — confirmed by the end-to-end `run_model` tests against a synthetic `Translate`/`LoadImage`. No datatest exercises `codedata init`/`run` (grep-verified).
- why: the `"decompile"` module's `IfaceData` (`IfaceDecompData`, ported in `w9-con-ifacedecomp`) surfaces the `Architecture`, but the `Architecture` does not yet expose the `translate()`/`loader()`/`AddrSpaceManager` accessors the per-call threading needs on the merged console surface (same gap LOSS-127 documents for ifacedecomp's engine sites). Stubbing the two architecture-needing bodies to a self-describing execution error keeps the command surface (registration order, `create_data`, the dump/target paths, the parse/error grammar) faithful and testable now, and makes each site single-line wireable when the integration item lands.
- restoration criteria: when the Architecture-integration W-item exposes the console `Architecture`'s `translate()`/`loader()`/space-manager accessors and `IfaceCodeDataCommand::setData`'s `get_data("decompile")` → conf fetch, replace `no_architecture("init")` with `codedata.init(conf.translate())` and `no_architecture("run")` with `codedata.run_model(conf.translate(), conf.loader_mut(), conf.spaces())`; re-pin against the C++ oracle by running `codedata run` on a real load image (the dump-command outputs are already pinned by this item's tests). No change to the registration/dump/target/parse structure is needed.

## LOSS-130: kuna console engine-touching command bodies routed through `engine_unavailable` — `stage status` (3-line output), `restarts`, `pipeline <variant>` (the decompile drive), `quality` (goto walk), the `region tree/blocks/walk` adapter, and every *routable* `kassert` store mutation are unwired pending the Architecture/print/drive/SEAM(W5/W7/W8) integration (w9-con-kuna-console ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, round 1)

- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-console/src/kuna_console.rs — `engine_unavailable(entry)` (136); `IfcKunaStageStatus::execute` (425, after the `No load image present` guard), `IfcKunaRestarts::execute` (674, after `No function selected`), `IfcKunaPipeline::execute` (728, after the guards + `hasNoCode` branch + the `Unknown pipeline variant` parse error), `IfcKunaQuality::execute` (785, after `No function selected` + `hasNoStructBlocks`), the `Dispatch::{ForceGoto,DeadcodeDelay,MultistageJump,FlowOverride,ProtoLock,Retype,Isolate,Rename,Option(_)}` arms (625-651), `IfcKunaRegionStub::execute` (835).
- surface: every kuna command body that reads live decompiler state. The pre-engine parts of each command ARE ported and tested: the `dcp->conf`/`dcp->fd` null guards with exact text (`No load image present` / `No function selected`), `pipeline`'s `list` form + `(current)` marker + `Unknown pipeline variant` parse error + `No code for <name>` branch, `quality`'s `No structured blocks for <name> (decompile first)` branch, and the full `kassert` validation+routing-decision core (parse-error precedence, `kassert list`, the LATENT/Unroutable text arms — reachable only with a program loaded, behind the image guard). Both `engine_unavailable` streams use the byte-faithful `Execution error: ` driver prefix. The one behavioral note within the boundary: the routable-`kassert` arms collapse the C++ per-substage `IfaceParseError` (arg-count) / function-scoped `IfaceExecutionError("No function selected")` into one `engine_unavailable` execution error — unreachable in any unit-tested or harness path because the top-of-command image guard fires first with no program loaded, and the routable mutation only exists once the W5 stores land.

Routing each engine read through a self-describing execution error keeps the registration order, the prefix-expansion surface (the datatests' `stage cat`→`stage catalog` etc.), and every expressible guard/branch/validation faithful and testable now — exactly the LOSS-127 (ifacedecomp) / LOSS-129 (codedata) pattern.
- restoration criteria: when the Architecture-integration + SEAM(W5/W7/W8) items land, wire each site to its named C++ entry point (the `engine_unavailable` message records it verbatim): `stage status` → emit the three lines from `allacts.getCurrentName()` + `present_lessequal` + `print->getArrayNotation()`; `restarts` → `kunaDumpRestarts`; `pipeline <variant>` → the save/switch/`reset`/`perform`/restore drive; `quality` → `kunaCountGotos` over the block arena; the `kassert` `Dispatch` arms → the per-substage arg-count validation + store mutation + the `kassert applied: ...` line + `assertLog.push`; `region *` → `buildFromBlockGraph`. Re-pin against the C++ oracle via `tests/stages/kuna-console.xml` (KUNA-CONSOLE #7/#8: `pipeline variant: decompile` / `compareform: original`), `kuna-assert.xml`, `kuna-pipeline-quality.xml`, `kuna-restarts.xml`, and `kuna-regions.xml`, which already string-match the C++ binary's full output. No change to the command registration/prefix/guard/validation structure is needed.

## LOSS-131: `Architecture::build_action` omits `resetDefaults`'s `setCurrent("decompile")` — after `init_post_engine` there is no current action; the `run_pipeline` decompile drive compensates by calling `set_current("decompile")` itself, a deviation from C++ `IfcDecompile::execute` which relies on the init invariant (w9x-arch-engine-glue ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: partial transcription (compensated by the drive; observable end-to-end behavior is correct)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/architecture.cc:590 `Architecture::buildAction` -> decompiler/cpp/action.cc:986 `ActionDatabase::resetDefaults`, whose tail (action.cc:999-1003) clears the old derived roots from `actionmap`, re-registers only the universal, calls `buildDefaultGroups()`, then `setCurrent("decompile")` — establishing the post-init invariant `getCurrent() == actionmap["decompile"] != null`. decompiler/cpp/ifacedecomp.cc:907 `IfcDecompile::execute` then calls `allacts.getCurrent()->reset(*fd)` / `->perform(*fd)` WITHOUT any `setCurrent`, relying on that invariant.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/architecture.rs `build_action` calls only `crate::universalaction::install_universal(...)` (which does `build_default_groups` + `set_universal` but NOT `set_current`), leaving `ActionDatabase::currentactname` empty; `init_post_engine` therefore returns with `has_current_action() == false`. rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/decompile_drive.rs `run_pipeline` papers over this by calling `arch.allacts.set_current("decompile")` before `get_current_mut()` — a structural addition C++ `IfcDecompile` does not have.
- surface: narrow and behaviorally compensated. The `set_current("decompile")` in `run_pipeline` derives the SAME filtered "decompile" root the C++ init seeds (verified: `set_current` -> `derive_action(UNIVERSAL_NAME, "decompile")` -> filtered clone of the universal tree), so the e2e decompile path is observably correct. The gap is only visible to a consumer that reads `getCurrent()`/`getCurrentName()` immediately after init without a prior `set_current` — e.g. a faithful `IfcDecompile` drive, `stage status` reading `getCurrentName()`, or `option setaction` toggling relative to the current root. Pinned by the verifier test `w9x_decompile_func_runs_perform_and_prints` (asserts the observed post-init `!has_current_action()`).
- why: the item ported the subsystem construction + the build-flow/perform/print drive; `build_action` was transcribed as the universal-install step but the `resetDefaults` tail (`setCurrent` + the derived-root reset) was not folded in, and the drive's own `set_current` masked the omission in the gate.
- restoration criteria: add the `resetDefaults` tail to `build_action` (clear non-universal entries from `actionmap`, re-register universal, `build_default_groups`, then `set_current("decompile")`), and remove the compensating `arch.allacts.set_current("decompile")` from `run_pipeline` so the drive matches C++ `IfcDecompile::execute` (bare `getCurrent()->reset/perform`). Re-run `decompile_e2e` (must still produce the 5 shells) and flip the verifier test's assertion to `assert!(arch.has_current_action())`.

## LOSS-132: `ArchOptionContext::set_default_extra_pop` drops the C++ eval-model spread — `option extrapop N` (global form) mutates only `defaultfp`, not `evalfp_current`/`evalfp_called` (w9x-arch-engine-glue ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: partial transcription (observable only with a distinct eval model, itself a W6 cspec seam)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/options.cc:279-286 `OptionExtraPop::apply` global branch: `glb->defaultfp->setExtraPop(expop); if (glb->evalfp_current != 0) glb->evalfp_current->setExtraPop(expop); if (glb->evalfp_called != 0) glb->evalfp_called->setExtraPop(expop);` — the extrapop is spread to all THREE evaluation models.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/architecture.rs `set_default_extra_pop` mutates only `self.defaultfp` (`Rc::make_mut(fp).set_extra_pop(expop)` + re-publish into the registry); the `evalfp_current` spread is skipped, and `evalfp_called` does not exist on the merged `Architecture`. The trait doc even reads "(+ eval-model spreads)" but the impl does not perform them.
- surface: observable only when `evalfp_current` has been explicitly pointed at a model DISTINCT from `defaultfp` (via `option evalcurrentmodel <name>`), which requires a multi-model cspec — itself a W6 seam. With the single seeded default model (`build_default_proto`), `eval_fp_current()` falls back to `defaultfp`, so the dropped spread is unobservable. `evalfp_called` has no field at all (a genuine future seam).
- why: the eval-model registry beyond the single `defaultfp`/`evalfp_current` pair is part of the W6 cspec proto-model decode; the porter wired the `defaultfp` write (the only model the seeded bootstrap distinguishes) and noted the spread in the doc without implementing it.
- restoration criteria: after the `defaultfp` write, also `set_extra_pop` on `evalfp_current` when it is `Some` and distinct, and on `evalfp_called` once that field lands with the W6 multi-model cspec. Re-pin against the C++ oracle on a cspec that registers a distinct eval model (the only input that distinguishes the spread).
