## LOSS-134: the `[space,offset,size]` address size specifier is parsed decimal-only in the kuna console seam, not in the C++ user-selected base (w10-setup-integration, round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: partial transcription (latent; no datatest exercises it)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/grammar.cc:3122-3125 `parse_machaddr`: `if (tok == ',') { s.unsetf(ios::dec | ios::hex | ios::oct); s >> size; ... }` — the optional size in the bracket form is read in the user-selected base (a `0x`/`0X` prefix is hex, a leading `0` is octal).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-console/src/ifacedecomp.rs `parse_machaddr` bracket branch calls `s.read_int()` (rust/crates/kuna-console/src/interface.rs:264 `read_int`), which parses an optional sign + decimal digits ONLY (`is_ascii_digit`). A hex size like `[ram,0x1000,0x10]` stops at `x` and reads `0`, so `volatile`/`readonly` then errors "Must specify a size" where C++ would read 16.
- surface: all three bracket-with-size forms in the datatests use decimal sizes (`[ram,0x1000,16]`, `[ram,0x210000,64]`, `[register,8,8]`), so the gap is unobservable in the suite. Pinned by the verifier test `w10_adv_bracket_hex_size_diverges_from_cpp`.
- why: the seam reuses the decimal-only `read_int` (built for `IfcHistory`) rather than a user-base integer reader.
- restoration criteria: route the bracket size through a user-base integer parse (`0x`->hex, leading `0`->octal, else decimal, into `int4`), then drop/flip the divergence-pinning assertion in `w10_adv_bracket_hex_size_diverges_from_cpp`.

## LOSS-131: analysis pass BODIES decline at integration seams (the M3 gap, reframed)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: deferral (the true M3 work)
- discovered by: w10-printc-body differential diagnosis (the printer is correct; the IR feeding it is raw).
- surface: the universalAction pipeline RUNS (B0 byte-equal registration) but most pass BODIES are no-ops at end-to-end integration — they were ported as dispatch + early-out guards + logic-validated-on-hand-built-inputs, then `return 0` / `Err(SEAM...)` at the real Funcdata/Architecture/cross-wave boundary. Concretely on `boolless`: 23 alive ops reach the printer vs the C++ decompiled ~7; sblocks (structured blocks) is EMPTY (ActionBlockStructure::apply is a SEAM stub — buildCopy cross-arena seed + collapseAll never invoked); varnodes carry no symbols/recovered types; Funcdata::funcp is the unit-stub seams::FuncProto. The printer renders this raw IR, which cannot byte-match the oracle. Current honest parity: 21/675 datatests (47/83 files decompile; the C++ oracle stays 675/675 untouched).
- the per-item gates did not catch this: each pass's LOGIC was verified on synthetic inputs in isolation; the END-TO-END pipeline is what reveals the bodies declining at the seams where they meet the real engine.
- the M3 work (un-seam in dependency order): (1) heritage actually applied to the live Funcdata (real SSA def-use); (2) ActionDeadCode + the simplification rule bodies actually transforming (reducing 23->~7 ops); (3) merge forming real HighVariables; (4) ActionInferTypes/proto-recovery populating Funcdata.funcp = real fspec::FuncProto + varnode types; (5) ActionBlockStructure buildCopy + collapseAll collapsing the CFG into structured sblocks; then printc emits real C.
- restoration criteria: VERTICAL-SLICE strategy — drive ONE function (boolless) to byte-match the C++ oracle `print C`, forcing every seam on its critical path closed in dependency order; then horizontally expand function-by-function, looping run-datatests->fix-top-seam until KUNA_ENGINE=rust run_tests --all --baseline = PARITY OK (M3). This is multiple focused waves of real analysis-integration work, not a cosmetic grind.

## LOSS-132: dual AddrSpaceManager — THE keystone blocker starving the analysis pipeline
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: paradigm-degradation (cross-wave architecture-bootstrap; the single highest-leverage M3 unblock)
- discovered by: w10-heritage-apply (which faithfully ported the heritage DRIVER — W5 had only the substrate — and proved it builds correct SSA on a single-manager diamond, then found this).
- surface: the port carries TWO AddrSpaceManagers. The SLEIGH engine lifts varnodes into the engine's space set (register idx 9, INTMEM idx 5, ...); but Funcdata.glb (the ArchHandle / seams::Architecture, built by Architecture::build_arch_handle -> ir_boundary_manager) carries a STRIPPED ir-boundary manager (const/unique/CODE/iop/fspec) with DIFFERENT Rc identities AND indices. Every analysis pass iterates spaces via glb.manage() and keys state by space — so it reaches 0/N of the real lifted varnodes. This is why the whole pipeline no-ops end-to-end (LOSS-131): heritage gets 0 varnodes, so deadcode/simplify/merge/types/structuring all operate on nothing. C++ has ONE manager: Architecture IS the AddrSpaceManager, with iop/fspec inserted into it (architecture.cc:638).
- why it exists: a W2/W4 porting convenience — Architecture owning the single engine manager raised Rust borrow/lifetime issues, so a separate stripped manager was bolted onto the ArchHandle. The per-item gates never caught it because each pass was tested on hand-built single-manager Funcdata fixtures; only the real lift+analyze path exposes the mismatch.
- restoration criteria (THE next keystone): unify to the C++ single-manager model — the engine's AddrSpaceManager is the one true manager; insert iop/fspec into it; share it as glb. Touches: the engine manager construction, build_arch_handle/ir_boundary_manager, the glb lifetime/borrow model, and re-greening the W3 IR tests that assumed the stripped manager. Pinned by tests/heritage_b3.rs (auto-flips to real B3 SSA parity once glb covers the engine spaces). Once closed, re-run the boolless vertical slice — heritage will reach the real varnodes and the deadcode/simplify/merge/types/structuring un-seams become tractable.

## LOSS-135: ActionDeadCode's four sub-seams (markConsumedParameters, INDIRECT indirect-source marking, BRANCHIND jump-table switch-var mask, holdStackAliasStores) take the conservative C++ default, not the recovered value (w10-deadcode-simplify ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: deferral (cross-wave seam — the deadcode body is fully transcribed; four leaf calls reach un-ported subsystems and fall through to the value C++ also computes when that subsystem is absent)
- discovered by: w10-deadcode-simplify verification (ActionDeadCode::apply un-seamed; the body is faithful coreaction.cc:3714-4290, these four calls within it reach W4/W6/W7 surfaces not at this boundary).
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/coreaction.cc — `markConsumedParameters` :4233-4235 (`for(i=0;i<data.numCalls();++i)`; with no `FuncCallSpecs` recovered, `numCalls()==0` and the loop is a true no-op); the CPUI_INDIRECT indirect-source block :3808-3821 (`PcodeOp::getOpFromConst`+`setIndirectSource`/`characterizeOverlap` — holds the indop alive against later `opDestroyRecursive`); the CPUI_BRANCHIND switch-var mask :4205-4213 (`jt->getSwitchVarConsume()`; when `findJumpTable`==0 the C++ uses `mask=~0`); `holdStackAliasStores` :4104-4144 (kuna GH-8500, gated on `arch.stack_alias_deadstore`, default-off).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/coreaction_render.rs — `deadcode_apply` markConsumedParameters comment-noop (`num_calls()`->0); `dc_propagate_consumed` CPUI_INDIRECT arm `SEAM(op-from-const)` (the outer backward `pushConsumed(outc,in0)` IS applied; only the indirect-source mark is skipped); `deadcode_apply` BRANCHIND `find_jump_table`->None->`mask=~0`; the `holdStackAliasStores` comment-noop (option default-off). Supporting seam stubs: `Funcdata::{num_calls,find_jump_table,get_active_output}`, `FuncProto::{is_output_locked,get_return_bytes_consumed}` (all return the un-recovered default).
- surface: for any function with no recovered call-specs / no jump table / no stack-alias-store option (the merged-tree default, and true of the boolless B4 gate fixture), all four reduce to exactly the C++ value, so the post-deadcode IR is the same. The only observable divergence is the skipped INDIRECT indirect-source mark: a true CPUI_INDIRECT whose IOP-space input resolves to a still-live COPY/op would be held alive by the C++ `setIndirectSource` but is NOT held by the Rust, so deadcode could prune one op the C++ keeps (an over-prune, never a wrong rewrite, never an under-prune). Not reachable on boolless (no such INDIRECT/IOP pattern survives to B4). Pinned by tests/deadcode_b4.rs (op count 21->9, no INT_MULT survives, RETURN survives) + coreaction_render::tests::dc_* (the consume-mask transfer functions exact vs C++).
- restoration criteria: markConsumedParameters restores when `FuncCallSpecs` recovery lands (W6/W7) — replace `num_calls()`->0 with the real spec count and transcribe the body (already documented inline); the INDIRECT indirect-source mark + BRANCHIND switch-var mask restore when `PcodeOp::getOpFromConst` / `JumpTable::getSwitchVarConsume` land (W7) — replace the `mask=~0` / skip with the real read; holdStackAliasStores is already fully transcribed and flips on with the `stack_alias_deadstore` option. At each, add a positive test asserting the held/masked op survives (or the masked input's narrower consume). Verified by the w10-deadcode-simplify verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-136: w10-proto-recovery residual seams + change-signal divergence — the real-FuncProto/AncestorRealistic port is faithful, but ActionPrototypeTypes bumps `count` where C++ does not, and four leaf branches take the conservative path (rport/w10-proto-recovery ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 1)
- date: 2026-06-13
- kind: mix — (a) one confirmed change-signal divergence (count) that is observationally inert in the merged-tree driver, and (b) four cross-wave seams that take the C++-default value. This wave CLOSES the LOSS-042 family-(d) sliver (`checkCallDoubleUse`/`onlyOpUse`/`ancestorOpUse`/`initActiveOutput`/the whole `AncestorRealistic` class) — those are now ported and adversarially tested; this entry records only the residuals.
- discovered by: w10-proto-recovery verification (Funcdata.funcp swapped to fspec::FuncProto; ActionPrototypeTypes/Heritage::guardReturns/ActionReturnRecovery un-seamed so boolless recovers `return(#0x0) ACC`).
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/coreaction.cc:4843-4939 (`ActionPrototypeTypes::apply` contains NO `count += 1` — it always returns with count 0); funcdata_varnode.cc:2098-2101 (`AncestorRealistic::enterNode` INDIRECT-non-store `if (trial->isKilledByCall()) return pop_fail;`); funcdata_varnode.cc:1900 (`onlyOpUse` reads `activeoutput` to gate the RETURN-mid-analysis branch); coreaction.cc:4862-4869 (`ActionPrototypeTypes` RETURN-strip loop skips `op->isDead()`); fspec.cc:1453-1512 (`ParamListStandard::decode` full attribute/`<group>`/`<modelrule>`/splitFloat handling).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/coreaction_protos.rs (`ActionPrototypeTypes::apply` `self.base.count += 1` on the RETURN strip and on `init_active_output` — both gratuitous vs C++; the strip loop iterates `iter_code(CPUI_RETURN)` with NO dead-op skip); rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/funcdata_varnode.rs (`AncestorRealistic::enter_node` INDIRECT-non-store omits the killedbycall->pop_fail branch; `only_op_use` reads `self.get_active_output().is_some()` which is `None` during `ActionReturnRecovery` because the container was moved out by `take_active_output`); rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/architecture.rs (`decode_default_proto`/`decode_pentry_list` — a simplified subset of `ParamListStandard::decode`: `<pentry>` lists + numgroup + finish_decode only; no `<group>`/`<modelrule>`/pointermax/splitFloat-resource-section/spacebase/autoKilledByCall-effect handling).
- surface: NONE on the live recovery path (verified empirically by the verifier).
  - (a) count: C++ `ActionPrototypeTypes::apply` never signals a change; the Rust bumps count twice. The action is `rule_onceperfunc` and the universal restart group restarts on `hasRestartPending()` (action.cc:562), NOT on count, and `decompile_drive::run_pipeline` does not loop on the returned count — so the spurious count changes only `count_apply` statistics, never output/control-flow. Proven: removing both bumps keeps `tests/proto_b4.rs` green (ACC still recovered). The prior verifier test `every_proto_action_apply_is_inert` was retargeted to `proto_action_apply_change_signal_contract`, which still pins the other 8 actions at count==0 and documents prototypetypes==1 with an explicit reason — an honest update, not a weakening that hides a wrong rewrite.
  - (b) killedbycall: the recovered 8051 ACC output trial IS `killedbycall` (register space; fspec.cc:1975), so the omitted enterNode branch WOULD be consulted in C++; but the ACC ancestry never reaches an INDIRECT-non-store node, so restoring the C++ `pop_fail` (verifier patch experiment) leaves the recovery unchanged. Inert here; could over-accept a trial on a different function whose return register flows through a call-INDIRECT.
  - (c) active-output-during-take: `only_op_use`'s `activeoutput != NULL` branch (the non-RETURN-opmatch case) is dead on the realized path because `ActionReturnRecovery` always passes a RETURN opmatch (the first `if opmatch is RETURN` branch wins), so the `get_active_output()`==None-while-taken-out condition is never observed.
  - (d) dead-op-skip: the RETURN-strip loop would strip in0 (and bump count) on a DEAD RETURN op where C++ skips it; no dead RETURN ops exist when ActionPrototypeTypes runs (pre-heritage/pre-deadcode), so latent.
  - (e) cspec subset: a default_proto using `<group>`/`<modelrule>`/pointermax/multi-class splitFloat would decode incompletely; the 8051 default model is plain `<pentry>` lists, and any decode failure falls back to the name-only model (recovery declines gracefully). Pinned by tests/proto_b4.rs + verify_w10_proto_recovery_adversarial.rs (T1-T4: in0->#0x0, in1==ACC from the trial machinery, register-space output, no PIECE; register-vs-spacebase killedbycall boundary; deriveOutputMap recovers nothing without an active trial).
- restoration criteria: (a) drop the two `self.base.count += 1` in `ActionPrototypeTypes::apply` to match C++ (returns 0 with count 0) — no test except the change-signal-contract assertion needs updating (flip prototypetypes back to count==0); (b) thread the trial's `is_killed_by_call()` into `AncestorRealistic::execute` and restore the `enter_node` INDIRECT-non-store `pop_fail` (the verifier's patch shows the exact wiring — a `trial_killed_by_call` param + one branch); (c) when a non-RETURN opmatch caller (ActionActiveParam input recovery) lands, give `only_op_use` the active-output state without moving the container out (e.g. read a saved flag), restoring the C++ `activeoutput != NULL` read; (d) add the `is_dead()` skip to the RETURN-strip loop; (e) extend `decode_default_proto` to the full `ParamListStandard::decode` (group/modelrule/splitFloat/spacebase/effect) as other processors' cspecs require it. Each restoration adds a positive test against the C++ oracle on a function that exercises the now-live branch. Verified by the w10-proto-recovery verifier (round 1).

- surface: on the boolless B5 slice the body recovers EXACTLY (named `v1` local, the `v1 = dat_52` trim COPY, the `// acc` storage comment, the if-body `v1 = 1`, `return v1`, the global staying `dat_52`); the ONLY residual is the output/local type name `undefined1` vs `uint1` (W8). Pinned by tests/print_b5_boolless.rs (the 5 existing + 3 verifier adversarial tests: w10_merge_trim_copy_is_real_ir_artifact, w10_merged_high_has_multiple_instances_and_acc_storage, w10_naming_conditional_other_function_gets_no_vn_name).

Replace the name-sorted decl emission with the C++ scope storage-address walk (and a numeric `vN` order). Wire `ActionOutputPrototype`'s high-off `updateOutputNoTypes` arm and the size-type-locked override; thread `data.isHighOn()`. The piece/symbol/multi-entry/dominant-copy MergeContext arms restore when VariablePiece overlap groups (W7), the multi-entry scope walk (W4), and findCommonBlock/totalReplace (W7) are exposed across the seam. Each restoration adds a positive test on a function that exercises the now-live arm. Verified by the w10-unseam-merge-types verifier (round 1).

The C++ oracle is untouched (207/207 unit + 675/675 datatest PARITY OK). The Rust full-datatest count still lags (LOSS-131/132, the dual-manager M3 gap) so the per-un-seam gate is the B5 golden, which is MET. Beyond the integer slice the declines are output-affecting: (1) a LOAD/STORE through a typed pointer never propagates the pointee type to/from the value (no `*(p)` typed deref recovery); (2) an enum flowing through XOR/AND/OR, or a float through sign-bit XOR/AND, never carries its type across; (3) PIECE/SUBPIECE never resize a composite/pointer (no struct-field type flow through extract/concat); (4) a pointer through PTRADD/PTRSUB/INT_ADD with a non-trivial offset or a struct-containing parent declines (no array-index / struct-member pointer recovery); (5) a struct-mid relptr compared against another value keeps the relptr instead of de-relativizing to a bare pointer (the C++ "give the other side a chance" heuristic), which can over-propagate the relptr; (6) pointer-alias propagation to spacebase-relative varnodes (`propagateSpacebaseRef`) never runs — stack/global aliased varnodes do not receive the pointer's pointee type; (7) a union-typed varnode is not resolved in-flow. The lattice MONOTONICITY and the `uint1` stability are proven (verifier tests below). Pinned by tests/print_b5_boolless.rs (the existing `w10_boolless_full_byte_parity_modulo_type_inference` now asserts EXACT oracle equality + `uint1 v1;` + no `undefined1`, plus the 3 verifier adversarial tests: verify_w10_infertypes_uint1_is_real_metatype_not_string, verify_w10_infertypes_is_settled_fixpoint_refining_the_seed, verify_w10_infertypes_no_uint1_acc_leak_into_condconst).
- restoration criteria: transcribe the 11 declining `propagateType` overrides verbatim (each gated on its metatype precondition exactly as C++ — XOR/AND on `isEnumType()||(FLOAT && floatSignManipulation!=MAX)`, OR on `isEnumType()`, LOAD/STORE via propagateToPointer/propagateFromPointer + getSpaceFromConst, PIECE/SUBPIECE via resizePointer/findTruncation, PTRADD/PTRSUB/SEGMENT/NEW per their bodies); complete `propagate_across_compare` with the relptr-STRUCT bare-pointer arm (`relPtr->getParent()->getMetatype()==TYPE_STRUCT && getByteOffset()>=0`) once the relptr byte-offset accessor is exposed; complete `propagate_add_in2_out` with the full `propagateAddPointer` command 0/1/2/3 analysis + the downChain parent->getTypePointerRel wrap; thread `needsResolution`/`resolveInFlow` once W8 union resolution lands; add the type-locked SymbolEntry `getExactPiece` seed to `get_local_type`/`build_localtypes`; restore `propagateSpacebaseRef`/`propagateRef` once `findSpacebaseInput` + `getScopeLocal()->getSpaceId()` + the spacebase-input surface exist; wire `applyTypeRecommendations` with the W4 ScopeLocal. Fix the module docstring to state the actual 5-arm coverage. At each, add a positive end-to-end type-flow test (an enum through OR, a pointer through LOAD, a struct-field through PTRSUB) against the C++ oracle. Verified by the w10-infertypes verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-139: w10-refinement-loops — emitBlockGoto always emits the trailing `goto` when a target is present, because `gotoPrints()`'s `getParent()->nextFlowAfter(this)` suppression is unported (an OVER-emit: a redundant `goto LAB_x;` to the fallthrough block the C++ would drop). [recorded under a REJECTED branch — entry stands as the departure record; the branch is rejected for an unrelated reason, see review w10-refinement-loops]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: paradigm-degradation
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/block.cc:2929 (BlockGoto::gotoPrints — `nextbl = getParent()->nextFlowAfter(this); gotobl = getGotoTarget()->getFrontLeaf(); return gotobl != nextbl`); decompiler/cpp/printc.cc:2924 (`if (bl->gotoPrints())`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/printc.rs `emit_block_goto` — emits the trailing goto unconditionally `if let Some(target) = ...get_goto_target()`, with no `nextFlowAfter` comparison.
- surface: a structured `BlockGoto` whose target IS the next-printed block renders a redundant `goto LAB_<idx>;` that the C++ oracle suppresses. Never an under-emit. No corpus datatest has a goto-suppression assertion, so unobservable on the current gate.
- why: `nextFlowAfter` (the flow-order traversal that decides the next-printed leaf) is not yet ported; gating on it requires the full block flow-ordering surface.
- restoration criteria: port `FlowBlock::nextFlowAfter` and gate the trailing-goto emit on `getGotoTarget()->getFrontLeaf() != getParent()->nextFlowAfter(this)`, then add a positive test on a function whose goto targets the fallthrough.

(emitAnyLabelStatement/emitCommentBlockTree are pre-existing seams already skipped in emit_block_copy.)

## LOSS-141: w10-refinement-loops — splitPieces inserts the SUBPIECE ops via `op_insert_after(def)` per piece, which (because op_insert_after recomputes the insertion point from `def` each call) lays the pieces in REVERSE block order relative to the C++ fixed-iterator `opInsert(bl,insertiter)`. [recorded under a REJECTED branch — entry stands as the departure record]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: paradigm-degradation
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/heritage.cc:564 splitPieces — `insertiter = insertop->getBasicIter(); ++insertiter;` then `opInsert(newop,bl,insertiter)` for every piece (one fixed iterator -> pieces land in iteration order just after the write).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/heritage.rs `split_pieces` Some(anchor) arm — `fd.op_insert_after(newop, anchor)` per piece; `op_insert_after` (funcdata_op.rs:1052) recomputes `point = prev.successor` each call, so successive pieces insert between `def` and the previously-inserted piece (reverse of C++).
- surface: the relative in-block order of the independent defining SUBPIECE ops produced by a write-refinement differs from C++. They are independent definitions (same input `startvn`, distinct outputs), so dataflow is unaffected; a deterministic-emission or op-sequence assertion over a refined write could observe the order flip. Not exercised by any oracle-matching corpus output on this branch (the only refinement-reaching file mis-lifts — see review).
- why: the Rust `split_pieces` reused the `op_insert_after` helper rather than threading a fixed post-write insertion iterator.
- restoration criteria: thread a single fixed insertion point (the op immediately after `def`, captured once) and `op_insert(... , point)` each piece before it, matching the C++ fixed-iterator order; cover with a refined-write IR-order test.

## LOSS-142: w10-refinement-loops (Round 2) — SnapshotRegisterLookup resolves `getRegister(name)` by inverting the `varnode_xref` location->name map, where C++ `SleighBase::getRegister` resolves through the SLEIGH symbol table (`findSymbol(nm)`). On a well-formed spec the two agree (the xref is a name bijection); they diverge only for a duplicate-location register name (the C++ `errorPairs` case), where the symbol table holds both names but the inverted xref keeps only the xref-winner. [accepted under an ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES branch]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: paradigm-degradation (benign on valid specs)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/sleighbase.cc:`SleighBase::getRegister` (findSymbol(nm) -> VarnodeSymbol -> getFixedVarnode); sleighbase.cc:91 buildXrefs (`varnode_xref.insert`, first-wins, duplicate -> errorPairs).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-sleigh/src/sleighbase.rs `SnapshotRegisterLookup::from_base` (`by_name` = inverse of `get_all_registers()`/`varnode_xref`) + `get_register` (`by_name.get(nm)`). Installed via `Sleigh::install_register_lookup` (sleigh.rs) on the manager's `RegisterLookup` slot (the kuna stand-in for the C++ `AddrSpace::trans` back-pointer).
- surface: a register name whose fixed VarnodeData location collides with another register's (so it lost the `varnode_xref` first-wins insert) would not resolve by name through the snapshot, whereas the C++ symbol table would still find it. This is an upstream-fatal spec condition (`errorPairs` is reported and rejected), so no valid pspec/cspec triggers it. The name->storage direction is otherwise exact, and the location->name directions (`get_register_name`/`get_exact_register_name`) reuse the SAME factored algorithms as `SleighBase` (verbatim move, byte-identical). Used only for the `<context_data>`/`<tracked_set>` register names (e.g. `DF`) and the `<addr name=…>` pentry path — all single-location names. The snapshot avoids an `Rc` cycle back into the owning `Sleigh` (the stated reason for not reusing the engine directly).
- why: a standalone snapshot (no SLEIGH symbol-table dependency, no `Rc` cycle) was chosen over threading the live symbol table into the manager; the inverse map is the minimal name->storage surface the spec decode needs.
- restoration criteria: when the SLEIGH symbol table is reachable from the installed `RegisterLookup`, resolve `getRegister(name)` via `findSymbol(nm)` (matching the C++ exactly, including the duplicate-location case) rather than the inverted xref; add a test with a duplicate-location register pair to pin the divergence closed.

## LOSS-143: w10-pspec-context — `parse_processor_config` dispatches ONLY the `<context_data>` branch of C++ `parseProcessorConfig`; it `find_child`s the single `context_data` element and silently ignores every other `<processor_spec>` child (volatile, incidentalcopy, jumpassist, segmentop, register_data, data_space, inferptrbounds, segmented_address, default_symbols, default_memory_blocks, address_shift_amount, properties, programcounter). C++ iterates ALL children via `peekElement` and dispatches each, throwing `LowlevelError("Unknown element in <processor_spec>")` on an unrecognized one — the Rust never throws (unknown elements are silently skipped by the `find_child` walk). [accepted under an ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES branch — context_data, the disassembly-mode-steering branch, IS faithfully wired]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: deferral
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/architecture.cc:1176-1239 (`Architecture::parseProcessorConfig` — the `peekElement` loop with branches for ELEM_PROGRAMCOUNTER/VOLATILE/INCIDENTALCOPY/CONTEXT_DATA/JUMPASSIST/SEGMENTOP/REGISTER_DATA/DATA_SPACE/INFERPTRBOUNDS/SEGMENTED_ADDRESS/DEFAULT_SYMBOLS/DEFAULT_MEMORY_BLOCKS/ADDRESS_SHIFT_AMOUNT/PROPERTIES and the trailing `throw LowlevelError("Unknown element in <processor_spec>")`); the dispatched-but-unported handlers decodeVolatile/decodeIncidentalCopy/userops.decodeJumpAssist/decodeSegmentOp/decodeRegisterData/setDefaultDataSpace/decodeInferPtrBounds.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/architecture.rs `parse_processor_config` (:995) — `find_child(&pspec, "context_data")` then `db.decode_from_spec`; no loop over the other children, no unknown-element throw. The code's own `SEAM(W6 pspec)` doc-comment (:986-994) acknowledges the deferral.
- surface: on the x86-64 corpus the skipped children are `programcounter` (a C++ skip-element too), `properties` (a C++ skip-element too), and `register_data` (assigns the DEBUG/CONTROL register GROUP flags DR0-15/CR0-15 — cosmetic register grouping that does not affect the lift of ordinary code). So on the datatest corpus the skip is unobservable. Beyond it: (1) a `<volatile>` declaration would not mark memory volatile (the C++ `decodeVolatile` -> a missing-volatile read could be CSE'd or reordered); (2) `<segmentop>`/`<jumpassist>`/`<incidentalcopy>` user-op semantics from the pspec never register (segmented-address processors, jump-table assists); (3) `<data_space>`/`<inferptrbounds>`/`<address_shift_amount>` defaults never apply (e.g. a Harvard-arch default data space, word-addressed shift); (4) `<default_symbols>`/`<default_memory_blocks>` are not registered for later resolution; (5) a malformed pspec with a typo'd element silently no-ops instead of the C++ hard error.
- why: only the `<context_data>` branch (the one steering SLEIGH's disassembly mode, the W-item's target) was needed to un-fake the 64-bit lift; the other branches are independent decode waves (volatile analysis, user-op libraries, data-space/symbol seams) not yet ported.
- restoration criteria: replace the single `find_child("context_data")` with the full C++ `peekElement` dispatch loop over every `<processor_spec>` child — porting decodeVolatile/decodeIncidentalCopy/decodeJumpAssist/decodeSegmentOp/decodeRegisterData/setDefaultDataSpace/decodeInferPtrBounds as each lands — and add the `throw` on an unrecognized element so a malformed pspec fails loudly as in C++. Cover each ported branch with a pspec that exercises it (a volatile range, a segmentop processor, a register-group assertion).

## LOSS-144: w10-spacebasespace — `decode_stack_pointer` reads the `<stackpointer>` `reversejustify` attribute as a strict `== "true"` string compare, narrower than C++ `xml_readbool` (xml.hh:391: true when the first char is `t`/`1`/`y`). A hand-written cspec writing `reversejustify="1"`, `reversejustify="yes"`, or `reversejustify="t…"` would diverge — C++ marks the stack space reverse-justified, the Rust does not. INVISIBLE on the entire vendored spec corpus: every `<stackpointer reversejustify=…>` in `specs/` writes exactly `reversejustify="true"`. [accepted under an ACCEPT branch — the core decode is faithful; this is a latent attribute-parsing narrowing with zero corpus surface]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: paradigm-degradation (benign on the vendored corpus)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/architecture.cc:995-996 (`decodeStackPointer`: `if (attribId == ATTRIB_REVERSEJUSTIFY) isreversejustify = decoder.readBool();`); decompiler/cpp/marshal.cc:279 (`XmlDecode::readBool` -> `xml_readbool`); decompiler/cpp/xml.hh:391-400 (`xml_readbool`: empty->false, first char `t`->true, `1`->true, `y`->true, else false).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/architecture.rs `decode_stack_pointer` — `let isreversejustify = attr_str(&sp, "reversejustify").map(|s| s == "true").unwrap_or(false);`. (The sibling `growth` attribute uses C++ `readString() == "negative"`, a string compare faithfully mirrored as `g == "negative"`, so only `reversejustify` is affected.)
- surface: none on any vendored pspec/cspec (all use `reversejustify="true"`). Beyond the corpus, a cspec using a non-`"true"` truthy form would leave the stack `SpacebaseSpace` without the `reverse_justification` flag, so small variables in the stack space would be justified per endianness instead of opposite — affecting only reverse-justified stack layouts (uncommon; the flag is consumed by stack-variable justification in later merge/type waves, themselves still seamed per LOSS-092/131).
- why: the strict `== "true"` was the minimal parse for the only form the corpus exercises; the general `xml_readbool` truthiness was not reproduced.
- restoration criteria: replace the `== "true"` compare with the C++ `xml_readbool` rule (empty->false, first byte in {`t`,`1`,`y`}->true, else false) — ideally via a shared `read_bool`/`xml_readbool` helper reused by every `<… reversejustify>`/boolean-attribute decode — and add a cspec test with `reversejustify="1"` (or `"yes"`) asserting the stack space carries `reverse_justification`. Confirmed by the w10-spacebasespace verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-145: w10-stackvar-promotion — `ScopeLocal::restructureVarnode` ports ONLY the layout-creating core (`gatherVarnodes`+`gatherOpen`+`gatherSymbols`+`restructure`); the entire C++ tail is dropped: the head `clearUnlockedCategory(-1)`, and the post-restructure `clearUnlockedCategory(Symbol::function_parameter)` / `clearCategory(Symbol::fake_input)` / `fakeInputSymbols()` / `state.sortAlias()` / `markUnaliased(state.getAlias())` / `checkUnaliasedReturn(state.getAlias())` / `annotateRawStackPtr()`. So unlocked-category cleanup, fake-input parameter synthesis, the alias sort, the unaliased-marking that lets later passes remove INDIRECTs, and the zero-offset raw-stack-pointer PTRSUB placeholder are all absent. [REJECT branch — recorded for when the chain is re-submitted]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: deferral
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/varmap.cc:1256-1286 (`ScopeLocal::restructureVarnode` — `clearUnlockedCategory(-1)` head + the post-`restructure` tail through `annotateRawStackPtr`); the dropped helpers `clearUnlockedCategory`/`clearCategory`/`fakeInputSymbols`/`MapState::sortAlias`/`markUnaliased`/`checkUnaliasedReturn`/`annotateRawStackPtr`.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/funcdata_spacebase.rs:231-276 (`restructure_varnode`) — the doc comment (:226-230) explicitly lists the dropped tail; the body runs gather+restructure and returns.
- surface: parameter/alias bookkeeping is not refined after layout recovery. On its own this would only matter once INDIRECT-removal/unaliased passes are reached; but combined with the un-folded LOAD (the chain does not fire end-to-end, see the verdict's F0/F1), the practical surface today is that promotion is not reproducible at all.
- why: only the disjoint-cover construction was needed to create the named-local Symbols; the alias/param tail is an independent concern coupled to AliasChecker/markUnaliased and the heritage storage-class machinery still seamed.
- restoration criteria: port the full tail in order (clearUnlockedCategory both calls, clearCategory(fake_input), fakeInputSymbols, sortAlias, markUnaliased, checkUnaliasedReturn, annotateRawStackPtr) and add a test that a zero-offset stack-pointer use produces the annotateRawStackPtr PTRSUB placeholder and that an unaliased local loses its INDIRECTs.

## LOSS-146: w10-stackvar-promotion — `MapState::gatherOpen` omits the LoadGuard/StoreGuard `addGuard` loops (the CPUI_LOAD/STORE array-step RangeHints). The AddBase/alias open-range loop is ported; the two `for (… loadGuard/storeGuard …) addGuard(...)` loops and `MapState::addGuard` itself (step/outSize reconciliation, the "LOAD size divides step -> pretend array of LOAD size", the `getAlignSize != step` unknown-type fallback, the range-locked minItems math) are not. The merged tree currently produces no load guards, so the loops are faithful-empty today.
- date: 2026-06-14

- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/varmap.cc:1241-1248 (the loadGuard/storeGuard loops) + :1004-1039 (`MapState::addGuard`).

## LOSS-147: w10-stackvar-promotion — `Funcdata::syncVarnodesWithSymbol` ADDS a SET-`addrtied` path the C++ explicitly forbids, and stubs `isUnmappedUnaliased`. C++ builds the update mask as `mapped`, and adds `addrtied|addrforce` to the mask ONLY when `(fl&addrtied)==0` — the comment is emphatic: "We can CLEAR but not SET the addrtied flag." The Rust adds an `else { mask |= addrtied }` arm so that when the resolved Symbol is itself address-tied the bit is SET on the promoted Varnode — a deliberate kuna workaround for the W3 IR skeleton not pre-tying stack storage via the heritage/storage-class bookkeeping. Separately, the C++ no-symbol/`unmappedAliasCheck` branch calls `lm->isUnmappedUnaliased(vn)` to set `nolocalalias`; the Rust hardcodes `fl = 0` (never sets `nolocalalias`) because the alias map is not built.
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: paradigm-degradation (compensating for un-ported upstream machinery)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/funcdata_varnode.cc:1077-1088 (the mask build, with the "CLEAR but not SET the addrtied flag" comment) and :999-1001 (`isUnmappedUnaliased(vnexemplar) ? nolocalalias : 0`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/funcdata_spacebase.rs:577-588 (the `else { mask |= addrtied }` SET arm) and :539-543 (`unmapped_alias_check` -> `fl = 0`, the `// do not assert unaliased` comment).
- surface: SET-addrtied paints addrtied/addrforce onto stack Varnodes that the C++ would leave untied (relying on prior storage-class tying), which can change later addrforce-sensitive passes; the `nolocalalias`-never-set side leaves unaliased stack locals without the flag that enables INDIRECT removal, so INDIRECTs that C++ would drop persist.
- why: the merged tree has no pass that pre-ties stack storage (only the return register is tied, by `mark_output_storage_addr_tied`) and no alias map at sync time; SET-addrtied reaches the same `mapped|addrtied` end-state the C++ reaches via the heritage path, and `nolocalalias=0` is the conservative choice without an alias map.
- restoration criteria: once the heritage/storage-class machinery ties stack storage upstream of sync, remove the `else { mask |= addrtied }` arm so addrtied is CLEAR-only exactly as C++; and wire `isUnmappedUnaliased` (over the built `state.getAlias()`) so `nolocalalias` is set faithfully. Add a test that an unaliased in-scope local gets `nolocalalias` and that addrtied is not spuriously set on a non-tied stack Varnode.

The ported `checkClog`/`analyzeExtraPop` bodies are faithful (verified line-by-line vs coreaction.cc:40-512) and unit-tested (clog LOAD->COPY fold, add-chain solve, indirect-guess solve, overlapping-store rejection), but on the current corpus the cross-call extra-pop solution cannot be propagated into call specs (no `FuncCallSpecs` until proto-recovery, LOSS-012/W6/W7), and the `restructureVarnode` tail (parameter/fake-input/alias refinement, LOSS-145) is absent — so a function's stack pointer is not fully normalized (e.g. `noforloop_alias` renders raw `RSP = RSP + 0xffffffffffffffd8;`) and the LOAD-fold-driven `loopvar[3]` form does not appear. The named-local promotion itself IS real and engine-driven (`int4 i [4]; // stack - 0x18`, `uint8 pchar; // stack - 0x10` observed via KUNA_DUMP on the live Rust engine, where round 1 showed raw `Stackffffffffffffffe0`).

The in-scope rewrites (clog fold, the solver's INT_ADD rewrite of a known solution) run regardless and are faithful.

Beyond it: a call that produces a value (`x = foo(...)`) renders without its output; the return register clobber is not guarded by an INDIRECT-creation, so a downstream read of the call's return register is not data-flow-separated from the pre-call value. The input-argument path does not depend on it.
- restoration criteria: uncomment `init_active_output()` in `func_link_output` and restore the `guardCalls` output-trial registration + the `newIndirectOp`/`newIndirectCreation` side-effect markers (with `holdind`/`effecttype`/`isAutoKilledByCall` exactly as heritage.cc:1469-1526) once the downstream INDIRECT-handling chain is complete; then re-pin the call-return datatests against the C++ oracle.

## LOSS-150: w10-callsite-args — `Heritage::guard` downgrades the C++ `throw LowlevelError("Free varnode with multiple reads")` invariant (a free read with `descend != 1`) to a silent "guard the read as-is and continue". This branch is NOT inert: it FIRES 24 times across the datatest corpus (measured with a one-shot probe). The C++ never hits this because its complete call-side INDIRECT chain (LOSS-149) separates the multi-read free reads; with that chain deferred, the partial recovery transiently produces multi-read free reads, and the C++ throw would abort the whole function. [accepted under an ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES branch — a faithful-degradation compensating for the deferred INDIRECT chain; produces no datatest regression]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: paradigm-degradation (compensating for un-ported upstream machinery)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/heritage.cc (`Heritage::guard` — the `oiter=vn->beginDescend()` walk; when a free read has more than one descendant the C++ throws `LowlevelError("Free varnode with multiple reads")`, which the action machinery surfaces as a function-level abort/restart).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/heritage.rs:1299-1316 (the `if descend != 1 { ... v.set_active_heritage(); continue; }` arm replacing the C++ throw, with the `SEAM(W4 call-side INDIRECT chain)` comment).
- surface: a multi-read free read is heritaged "as-is" (linked to its first use) instead of aborting the function. Verified to fire 24x on the corpus; the rendered output of the spot-checked call-heavy functions (noforloop_alias `sub_40067b(RDI)`/`sub_400440(RDI,RSI)`, deindirect `(*...)();`+`sub_100580(RDI)`, dupptr `sub_100684(RDI)`) is nonetheless correct (the recovered argument lists match the C++ ProtoModel order). No datatest regression (24 passing unchanged; corpus errors 59->57). Where the divergence could bite: a function where the multi-read free read is genuinely a heritage bug (not a transient artifact of the deferred chain) would be silently mis-linked rather than loudly rejected.
- why: with the call-side INDIRECT chain deferred (LOSS-149), the C++ invariant can be transiently violated; aborting the function (the C++ throw) is strictly worse than guarding the read, and the realized output is unaffected on the corpus.
- restoration criteria: once the call-side INDIRECT chain (LOSS-149) is complete so the invariant holds, restore the C++ `throw LowlevelError("Free varnode with multiple reads")` (with the surrounding restart/abort handling) and confirm the 24 firing sites no longer occur; add a fixture that a genuine multi-read free read is rejected, not silently linked.

## LOSS-151: w10-callsite-args — `ActionRestartGroup::apply` DROPS every restart in the live pipeline. The C++ restart re-follows flow (`startProcessing`->`followFlow`) to rebuild the IR after `data.clear()`; the Rust flow follower needs the `Architecture`'s SLEIGH translator, which is not threaded into the action loop, so a new `ctx.can_reflow` flag gates the restart. `ActionContext` derives `Default` (`can_reflow=false`) and NOTHING in the real pipeline sets it true (only the two restart-group unit tests do) — so on every restart-pending the group clears the pending flag and KEEPS the current (already-analyzed) IR instead of re-flowing. [accepted under an ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES branch — a strictly-better degradation than the pre-wave base, which called `data.clear()` then re-ran actions over an EMPTY function]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: deferral (un-ported `ActionStart` re-flow) + compensating workaround
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/coreaction.cc (`ActionStart::apply`->`startProcessing`->`followFlow`, the re-flow that rebuilds the IR on restart); decompiler/cpp/action.cc:553 (`ActionRestartGroup::apply`, the `data.getArch()->clearAnalysis(&data)` + re-run loop).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/action.rs:566 (`ActionContext::can_reflow`, `#[derive(... Default)]` => false); :838-848 (the `if !ctx.can_reflow { data.set_restart_pending(false); self.curstart = -1; return 0; }` drop arm). No setter of `can_reflow=true` exists outside `action/tests.rs`.
- surface: a sub-action that requests a restart (e.g. after a deindirect/prototype change) does NOT cause the function to be re-analyzed; the pre-restart IR is kept. On the datatest corpus this produces NO regression (the base's `data.clear()`-then-empty path was worse). Beyond it: any analysis correctness that depends on a second flow pass (override-driven re-flow, jumptable-discovered new code, prototype-injection re-lift) is silently skipped.
- why: the Rust flow follower needs the `FlowEnvironment` (SLEIGH translator + typeop table) on the `Architecture`, which the IR-boundary action loop does not carry; until that is threaded in, re-flow cannot run, and dropping the restart (keep analyzed IR) beats clearing to an empty body.
- restoration criteria: thread the `FlowEnvironment` into the action loop so `ActionStart` can re-follow flow on restart; set `can_reflow=true` in the real `decompile_drive` path; remove the drop arm so a restart genuinely re-flows. Add a test that an override/prototype change triggering a restart re-analyzes the function.

## LOSS-152: w10-callsite-args — `FuncCallSpecs::checkInputTrialUse`'s `callee_pop` stack-parameter test uses 64-bit `i64` arithmetic where C++ explicitly truncates to `int4`: C++ `(int4)(trial.getAddress().getOffset() + (trial.getSize()-1)) < expop` (fspec.cc:5628) takes the LOW 32 BITS of the (uintb) sum as a signed int4; the Rust `(off as i64 + (sz as i64 - 1)) < expop as i64` keeps the full 64-bit value. They agree whenever the offset's bit-31 and bit-63 match (true for every realistic small stack offset); they would diverge only for a stack-parameter offset >= 2^31 with differing high bits. LATENT: the `callee_pop` branch fires only for a callee that pops its own params with a recovered extrapop > 4 (the `__stdcall`/Windows convention); the gcc/SysV datatests never reach it (`callee_pop=false`), and even on the `x86:LE:32:default:win` corpus the offsets are small. [accepted under an ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES branch — a latent integer-width narrowing with effectively-zero corpus surface]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: integer-width divergence (latent, benign on the corpus)
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/fspec.cc:5627-5631 (`else if (callee_pop) { if ((int4)(trial.getAddress().getOffset() + (trial.getSize()-1)) < expop) ... }`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/funcdata_callsite.rs:114-122 (`} else if callee_pop { ... if (off as i64 + (sz as i64 - 1)) < expop as i64 { ... } }`).
- surface: none on any datatest (the branch is `callee_pop`-gated; SysV is callee_pop=false, and the win32 stack offsets are small). Beyond it, a stack-parameter offset that differs in bit-31 vs bit-63 would have the trial marked active/no-use opposite to C++.
- why: i64 was used throughout the arithmetic without reproducing the explicit C++ `(int4)` low-32 truncation.
- restoration criteria: mask the sum to `int4` exactly as C++ — `((off as i64).wrapping_add(sz as i64 - 1)) as i32) < expop` (or compute in `i64` then `as i32`) — and add a unit test with a trial offset whose bit-31/bit-63 differ asserting the int4-truncated comparison.

The verifier's three adversarial tests pin the argument recovery (count, order, deref form) on the XmlArchitecture path; the name resolution is verified on the `decomp_test_dbg` path (`might_change`/`printf`/`puts`).

## LOSS-154: w10-input-prototype — TWO departures in the input-param recovery + typing.

(F1) `PrintC::declarator_parts` (the C-declarator front/back builder transcribing `buildTypeStack`/`pushTypeStart`/`pushTypeEnd`) has its pointer-vs-array PARENTHESISATION INVERTED relative to the C++ RPN `ptr_expr`/`array_expr` precedence: a pointer-to-array `int4 (*)[1]` renders as `int4 *a[1]` (an array-of-pointer) and an array-of-pointer `int4 *[1]` renders as `int4 (*a)[1]` (a pointer-to-array) — the two C nestings come out swapped. The `pending_ptr` walk (base->outer) only wraps when an ARRAY modifier sees a *preceding* pointer, which is the array-of-pointer ordering, so it parenthesises the wrong one. The porter's own doc-comment on `declarator_parts` states the CORRECT C++ output, which the code does not produce. (F2) Newly applying a locked prototype (`ActionPrototypeTypes` forcing typed input/output Varnodes) leaks an un-cleaned SUBPIECE `SUB(r0,0)` into the body for `condconst2`'s `char zeroprop(int4 *ptrint,int4 val)` — the downstream type/cast cleanup actions that remove it are un-ported (a W8 surface) — costing exactly one datatest assertion (`condconst2` `Immediate Conditional #8`, the `SUB`-absent check). [accepted under an ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES branch — the parameter recovery itself is faithful and proven byte-equal to the C++ oracle signature; net datatest effect is +1 (25->26: +2 real gains `Conditional Constant #11`/`Namespace #1`, -1 `Immediate Conditional #8`), execution-failures flat at 28]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: (F1) latent printer bug (declarator parenthesisation inverted); (F2) deferral (un-ported downstream SUBPIECE/cast cleanup)
- cpp-anchor: (F1) decompiler/cpp/printc.cc:144-347 (`buildTypeStack`/`pushTypeStart`/`pushTypeEnd` — the `ptr_expr`-inside-`array_expr` precedence the RPN encodes; the `array_expr` token binds tighter than `ptr_expr`, so a `*` nested inside an array IS parenthesised and an array of `*` is NOT). (F2) decompiler/cpp/coreaction.cc:4843-4937 (`ActionPrototypeTypes::apply`/`extendInput` — the forced typed input/output Varnodes) + the downstream type/cast actions (`ActionInferTypes`/cast insertion) that eliminate the truncation in the oracle.
- rust-anchor: (F1) rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/printc.rs:2661 (`declarator_parts`, the `for ct_mod in stack.iter().rev().skip(1)` walk with `pending_ptr` — only the ARRAY arm wraps, and only on a *preceding* pointer). (F2) rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/coreaction_protos.rs (`ActionPrototypeTypes::apply` locked-input/output force-Varnode arms; `extend_input`) — the SUBPIECE the C++ later cleans is not cleaned because the downstream actions are seamed.
- surface: (F1) any pointer-to-array or array-of-pointer PARAMETER that is emitted as a decompiled function HEADER renders the wrong C type (silently — it parses as a different type). LATENT today: `ptrtoarray.xml` declares `int4 (*a)[1]` params but only *calls* those functions; none is `lo fu`+`print C`'d, so no passing assertion depends on the broken path. The common declarator cases (`T`, `T *`, `T[N]`, `T **`, `undefined<N> *`, `void`) are all correct (5 verifier unit tests pass). (F2) a function whose locked/recovered prototype forces a typed output (or input) narrower than its register leaks `SUB(reg,0)` into the body; costs `condconst2`'s assertion #8 and would cost any datatest with a `SUB`-absent check over such a function.
- why: (F1) the flat front/back string accumulation models only one nesting direction; the RPN's two-token precedence was collapsed to a single `pending_ptr` flag whose wrap-condition matches the array-of-pointer ordering, not pointer-to-array. (F2) the downstream type-propagation/cast-insertion actions that consume and eliminate the forced-output SUBPIECE are not yet ported, so the truncation op survives to the printer.
- restoration criteria: (F1) reproduce the `ptr_expr`/`array_expr` precedence faithfully — when walking the modifier stack, an array/function modifier must parenthesise a pending pointer ONLY for the pointer-to-array nesting (pointer OUTSIDE array in the type, i.e. the pointer is the OUTER modifier), and leave an array-of-pointer (array OUTER) unparenthesised; flip the `pointer_to_array_paren_inverted_divergence` verifier test to the C++-correct values and add a `lo fu` fixture that emits such a param header. (F2) once the downstream type/cast cleanup actions land, confirm the forced-output `SUB(reg,0)` is eliminated and `condconst2`'s `Immediate Conditional #8` passes again; flip the `condconst2_typed_sig_but_leaks_sub_unlike_oracle` verifier test to assert NO `SUB`.

## LOSS-155: w10-implied-vars — the explicit/implied INLINING is now realized (the merge wiring fires + the recovered return-value storage is left un-tied for the single-def transient shape), but the un-tie decision is a STRUCTURAL stand-in for `ScopeLocal::inScope` and `ActionMarkImplied::checkImpliedCover` returns `true` unconditionally.

(D1) `mark_output_storage_addr_tied` decides whether the recovered return-value storage stays addr-tied (-> explicit) by a kuna structural heuristic — left UN-tied (eligible to be marked IMPLIED and inlined) only when the function has a LOCKED non-void output AND exactly one written Varnode lives at the storage address AND that Varnode is defined by a lone `CPUI_COPY` whose source is a "pure transient" (not addr-tied, not spacebase, not stack-sourced). This is a narrower-than-`inScope` approximation: it ties MORE than the real `rangetree.inRange` query would, so it never inlines MORE than the C++ oracle (the safe direction for over-inlining). It is an IR-shape + recovered-output test, with NO function-name/address special-casing (verified by grep).

(D2) `check_implied_cover` (the port of `ActionMarkImplied::checkImpliedCover`) is a `return true` stub — the LOAD-crossing-STORE / call-crossing-cover / `Merge::inflateTest` HighVariable-intersection checks are not ported (they need the HighVariable cover graph). With the inlining now LIVE (D1 un-ties storage, `ActionMarkImplied` walks it), this residual is the ONLY thing besides the un-tie guard that could keep a cover-conflicting return value explicit. The over-inlining risk is bounded by the D1 guard: a single-def lone-COPY-from-unique transient has no aliasing/cover conflict, so the conditions under which D1 un-ties are exactly the conditions under which `checkImpliedCover` would also return `true`. A multi-write storage (boolless ACC, condconst_copy) or a call-/store-crossing value (global_cross) fails the D1 `written.len()==1`/pure-transient guard and stays explicit — measured on the corpus and pinned by adversarial tests. [accepted under an ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES branch — the merge wiring (`mergeOpcode(CPUI_COPY)` + `markInternalCopies`) is a faithful transcription of the C++ one-liners, the inlining moves the corpus TOWARD the oracle (redundant trim-COPY `// rax` locals removed, matching the oracle's single-named-local return), execution-failures flat, no datatest regression — C++ oracle 207/207 + 675/675 PARITY OK untouched]
- date: 2026-06-14
- kind: (D1) kuna divergence (structural `inScope` stand-in, refines LOSS-137's heuristic); (D2) deferral (HighVariable cover-graph not ported — folded into LOSS-138's "HighVariable type/cover surface")
- cpp-anchor: (D1) decompiler/cpp/funcdata_varnode.cc:993-997 (`ScopeLocal::inScope` -> `fl = Varnode::mapped | Varnode::addrtied`), database.hh:599-600 (`Scope::inScope = rangetree.inRange(addr,size)`). (D2) decompiler/cpp/coreaction.cc:3479-3517 (`ActionMarkImplied::checkImpliedCover`: the LOAD/STORE cover `contain(storeop,2)`+`isPossibleAlias`, the call-crossing `contain(callop,2)`, the `getMerge().inflateTest(defvn,vn->getHigh())` intersection). The realized merge one-liners: coreaction.hh:393 (`mergeOpcode(CPUI_COPY)`), :1034 (`markInternalCopies`).
- rust-anchor: (D1) rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/coreaction_cleanup.rs (`mark_output_storage_addr_tied` — the `output_locked && written.len() == 1` + `is_pure_transient` lone-COPY guard). (D2) rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/coreaction_cleanup.rs (`check_implied_cover` -> `true`; `base_explicit`'s addrtied SUBPIECE/PIECE conservative `return -1` arms + the multipleInteraction/processMultiplier term-duplication promotion left as the conservative no-op — both pre-existing from rport/w10-structure-printbody). The realized wiring: `ActionMergeCopy::apply` -> `Merge::merge_opcode(CPUI_COPY)`, `ActionCopyMarker::apply` -> `Merge::mark_internal_copies` (rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/merge.rs:1194, :2091) over the `MergeContext for Funcdata` bridge.
- surface: a recovered single-def transient return value is now inlined (the redundant trim-COPY `// rax` local + `v1 = v2;` self-copy is merged away: `v2 = ZEXT(a0); v1 = v2; return v1;` -> `v1 = ZEXT(a0); return v1;`), matching the C++ oracle's single-named-local return direction (the condconst datatest's `Conditional Constant #10/#11` `v1 = x;`/`v1 = 0x14;` shape). What is NOT covered: (a) a LOCKED-output function whose return value is a single-def transient that genuinely crosses an aliasing LOAD/STORE or a call would be inlined by D1+D2 where the C++ `checkImpliedCover` keeps it explicit — not observed on the corpus (D1's pure-transient/lone-COPY guard excludes the call-crossing `global_cross` case via its unlocked output, and excludes constants-crossing-calls in the corpus); (b) the term-duplication `processMultiplier` promotion of >1-descendant implieds and the addrtied SUBPIECE/PIECE `contains`/`isPartialRoot` refinements in `base_explicit` remain the conservative explicit default.
- restoration criteria: replace `mark_output_storage_addr_tied` with the real `ScopeLocal::coalesceSymbols`/`syncVarnodesWithSymbol` `inScope` (`rangetree.inRange`) sync once the W4 ScopeLocal range tree lands; port `checkImpliedCover` over the HighVariable cover graph (`Cover::contain`, `isPossibleAlias`, `Merge::inflateTest`) once the cover surface is exposed across the merge seam, then add a fixture: a locked-output single-def transient return that crosses an aliasing STORE (must STAY explicit under the real cover test, where D1+D2 today would inline it). Port `base_explicit`'s `multipleInteraction`/`processMultiplier` and the addrtied SUBPIECE/PIECE `contains` arms. Verified by the w10-implied-vars verifier (round 1): 4 adversarial tests in rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w10_implied_vars_adversarial.rs (trim-COPY inlined on condconst_conn — fails on base, passes on branch; multi-write condconst_copy stays explicit / not over-inlined; boolless ACC byte-parity unregressed; inlining data-driven, no boolless-token leak).

(D1) `is_mapped` over-skips relative to `getSymbolEntry` only when a Varnode has a SymbolEntry but is not `mapped`; conservative (can only MISS a propagation, never fabricate) and unreachable today (chain dormant). (D2) the CALLIND size guard rejects a type-locked param wider than the arg Varnode where C++ accepts it — conservative (never types where C++ would not), latent (CALLIND callee-param typing needs the LOSS-153 recovered-callee-proto path). (D3) unlocked-category cleanup, fake-input parameter synthesis, the unaliased-marking that removes INDIRECTs, and the return-storage unmap are absent — the alias `[0]==0` placeholder gate is computed from the same `sortAlias` result either way, so the ported placeholder is unaffected.

(D2) split `call_input_type_local` into the CALL arm (keep `size <= argsize`) and the CALLIND arm (drop it) to match typeop.cc:709 vs 766 exactly. (D3) port the remaining tail (clearUnlockedCategory both calls, clearCategory(fake_input), fakeInputSymbols, markUnaliased, checkUnaliasedReturn) once the `Symbol::category` + alias-block bookkeeping lands (the open half of LOSS-145). So (i) the `xunknown1 $$undef00000002 [4]` array LOSS-156 said the env-on path produces is NOT observed on the base either (the RuleLoad/StoreVarnode spacebase conversion that would feed `gatherVarnodes` a sized hint never fires in this pipeline), hence there is no regression for `clearUnlockedCategory` to fix; (ii) condconst datatest #10 (`v1 = x;`) PASSES on both base and branch regardless of the env var (it is satisfied via the register-recovery path, not a typed stack local), so this item neither created nor restored that pass. The full Rust datatest tally is byte-identical base==branch (47/456 assertions, 28 execution-failure files); the C++ oracle is untouched (207/207 + 675/675 PARITY OK). [accepted under an ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES branch — the two ports are faithful and harmless (net-neutral, no regression), and `clearUnlockedCategory(-1)` is legitimate always-on upstream behavior the pipeline needs eventually; the claimed improvement is simply dormant/unobservable until the upstream spacebase-conversion seam lands]

## LOSS-160: end-to-end verify tests depend on a freshly-built release decomp_test_dbg
- date: 2026-06-15
- kind: test-harness fragility (Rust test-only)
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_w10_*.rs (rust_print_c_for -> rust_oracle picks target/release/decomp_test_dbg)
- surface: the W10 end-to-end print-C verifier tests invoke the pre-built rust/target/release/decomp_test_dbg; `cargo test` builds DEBUG test binaries but does NOT rebuild that release binary, so after a source change the test runs a STALE binary and can spuriously fail (observed: 2 emptyblock-orform tests failed against a stale bin, passed after `cargo build --release -p kuna-console -p kuna-harness`).
- why: the e2e print-C tests need a real lifted/decompiled binary, which only the release engine binary produces; cargo's debug/release split makes the dependency implicit.
- workaround / harness requirement: rebuild the release bins before running these tests (the orchestrator does this before every integrated measurement). Restoration: make rust_oracle build-if-stale (cargo build --release) or gate the test on a freshness check; low-risk polish.

The cache machinery is fully realized and unit-tested, but its only live caller (`propagateTypeEdge` -> `resolve_union_in_flow`) hits the miss path on every call because (i) nothing in the live pipeline writes the cache (`setUnionField`/`setAddressBasedUnionField` have no live producer — the `ScoreUnionFields` driver and `ActionSetCasts::resolveUnion` are W4/W6 seams), and (ii) `find_union_resolve` is reached by no live code. ## LOSS-173: w10-displayformat — readVarnode's "Missing p-code sequence number" error is downgraded from IfaceParseError to IfaceExecutionError, so the console renders it under "Execution error: " instead of "Command parsing error: " (faithful otherwise; error-only path, no parity impact)
- date: 2026-06-15
- kind: (C) error-class divergence, cosmetic. The success path of `force varnode <varnode> <format>` and every other error throw in the path keep their correct C++ class; only the one constant-without-seqnum parse error is misclassified.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/ifacedecomp.cc:1480 (`IfaceDecompData::readVarnode`: `throw IfaceParseError("Missing p-code sequence number")` when a constant Varnode's reading-op seqnum is absent); decompiler/cpp/ifacedecomp.cc:3624-3629 (`execute()` catch frames: `catch(IfaceParseError&) -> "Command parsing error: "`, `catch(IfaceExecutionError&) -> "Execution error: "` — distinct, observable prefixes).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-console/src/ifacedecomp.rs:419 (`read_varnode` returns a plain `String` "Missing p-code sequence number" for the constant arm, losing the IfaceParseError class); rust/crates/kuna-console/src/ifacedecomp.rs:2154 (`IfcForceFormat::execute` maps the whole `read_varnode` result through `IfaceError::execution`, stamping it Execution). The console error renderer distinguishes the kinds (interface.rs:90-97 IfaceErrorKind::Parse vs Execution; is_parse()/is_execution() drive the prefix), so the misclassification IS observable as a different printed prefix.
- surface: only on a malformed interactive `force varnode <const-varnode-without-seqnum> <fmt>`. The constant arm of readVarnode requires a p-code seqnum; if the user omits it, C++ prints "Command parsing error: Missing p-code sequence number" and the Rust port prints "Execution error: Missing p-code sequence number". No datatest, no B5 oracle assertion, and no success-path output is affected (the whole-suite parity is intact: C++ 675/675 PARITY OK, kuna-console lib 183 passed, cargo test --workspace 0 failed). The functional behavior (the command fails and produces no forced format) is identical; only the diagnostic prefix differs.
- why: the seam was introduced because `read_varnode` was given a `Result<_, String>` signature (a single error channel) rather than a typed IfaceError, and the single call site uniformly wraps it as `IfaceError::execution`. C++ distinguishes the class at the throw site inside readVarnode. The fix is to either return a typed `IfaceError` from `read_varnode` (parse for the seqnum-missing arm, execution for "Requested varnode does not exist") or to special-case the seqnum-missing message at the call site — the former is the faithful transcription.
- restoration criteria: change `read_varnode` to return `Result<Option<VarnodeId>, IfaceError>`, emit `IfaceError::parse("Missing p-code sequence number")` for the constant-no-seqnum arm (matching ifacedecomp.cc:1480) and `IfaceError::execution` for the loc-scan/not-exist arms, and drop the `.map_err(IfaceError::execution)` blanket at ifacedecomp.rs:2154; then assert (e.g. in the console-family e2e) that the rendered prefix is "Command parsing error: " for the seqnum-missing case. Recorded by the w10-displayformat verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-174: w10-inline-body — the `inline_recursion` cycle set is a per-flow *owned clone* (`forwardRecursion` deep-copies the BTreeSet) instead of C++'s *shared pointer* into `inline_base`, so a grandchild function inlined inside one branch is NOT seen as already-inlined by a sibling branch of the same parent (cross-branch dedup lost). Not corpus-observable.
- date: 2026-06-15
- kind: (C) data-structure-identity divergence, faithful-partial. The membership logic (insert/contains/erase) and the corpus-observable behavior (recursion break, "Could not inline here", easy/hard model re-insert) are byte-faithful; only the *sharing* of the set across the nested-flow chain differs.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/flow.hh:103-104 (`set<Address> *inline_recursion;` — a POINTER; `set<Address> inline_base;` — the owned storage); flow.cc:1048 (`forwardRecursion`: `inline_recursion = op2.inline_recursion;` copies the POINTER, so the nested flow's `inline_recursion` aliases the PARENT's `inline_base` — mutations are mutually visible); flow.cc:1250-1255 (`inlineSubFunction`: top level does `inline_recursion = &inline_base`, then `inline_recursion->insert(data.getAddress())`); flow.cc:1267,1272 (the easy/hard-model erase/insert of `fd->getAddress()` through the shared pointer); flow.cc:54-78 (the clone-ctor `inline_base = op2->inline_base; inline_recursion = &inline_base` — same aliasing intent).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/flow.rs (`FlowInfo::inline_recursion: BTreeSet<Address>` — OWNED, not a shared handle; `forward_recursion` does `self.inline_recursion = op2.inline_recursion.clone()` — a DEEP COPY; `inline_sub_function` inserts/contains/removes on the owned set; `inline_flow` forwards the clone into the nested `inlineflow` before `generate_ops`). Because the set is cloned, an insertion the NESTED flow makes into its copy is not reflected back into the PARENT's set. The parent's own post-`inline_flow` `insert(fd)` (the hard-model re-insert, flow.rs `inline_sub_function` `res==1` arm) masks the divergence for the *direct* callee, but a transitively-inlined grandchild G (inserted only in the nested copy) is invisible to a sibling parent call that also targets G.
- surface: ZERO on the inline corpus. inline.xml exercises a leaf EZ inline (add50->add100), a one-level hard inline (compare->twohard) and a self-recursion break (collatz<->collatz1); none has the "parent inlines A and B where A's body already inlined grandchild G and B also calls G" shape that would diverge. The verifier's end-to-end tests (verify_w10_inline_body.rs) confirm the recursion break terminates and the inner call survives, matching the C++ B5 oracle exactly for these cases. The divergence would surface only on a deeper inline graph: C++ would print one "Could not inline here" (G already on the shared stack) where the Rust would inline G a second time. The C++ oracle (675/675 PARITY OK) is byte-untouched; this is a Rust-internal model choice.
- why: the shared-pointer-into-`inline_base` idiom (one set object aliased by every flow in the inlining chain) has no zero-cost Rust analogue without `Rc<RefCell<BTreeSet>>` threaded through every nested `FlowInfo`; the porter chose an owned clone forwarded at `forward_recursion` time, which reproduces the cycle-break for the single-chain (depth-first) case the corpus needs. Threading a shared handle is a larger refactor of the `FlowInfo` ownership model with no consumer that would exercise the difference yet.
- restoration criteria: when an inline corpus (or a real binary) with a diamond inline graph (sibling parent calls converging on a shared grandchild) lands, replace the owned `BTreeSet` + clone with a shared `Rc<RefCell<BTreeSet<Address>>>` (or pass `&mut` the parent's set down the `inline_flow` call) so `forward_recursion` aliases rather than copies, matching flow.hh:103; pin it with a depth-2 diamond test asserting the single "Could not inline here" the C++ shared model produces. Recorded by the w10-inline-body verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-175: w10-inline-body — the payload-injection arms of `injectPcode` (`injectUserOp` for CALLOTHER, `injectSubFunction` for a registered call-fixup inject-id) are W4-deferred `continue` no-ops; only the whole-body in-lining arm (`inlineSubFunction`) is wired. A CALLOTHER user-op or a fixup-applied CALL renders un-injected (as the original call), matching pre-inline-wave behavior.
- date: 2026-06-15
- kind: (A) subsystem-absent deferral, fail-closed. The in-lining arm (the load-bearing path for inline.xml/inlinetarget.xml) is fully ported; the two payload arms decline cleanly (leave the op in place) because the `PcodeInjectLibrary` payload + `doInjection` p-code-template weaving is not yet ported.

## LOSS-177: w10-strings — `HeapSequence` truncates the 64-bit PTRADD element-size multiplier to `int4` before comparing it to `ptr_add_mult` in `find_base_pointer` (constseq.rs:478-479) and `find_initial_stores` (:589), whereas the C++ compares the FULL-WIDTH offset. A crafted PTRADD whose `getIn(2)` constant has bits above bit 31 set but whose low 32 bits equal `ptrAddMult` is ACCEPTED by the Rust back-walk (it steps past the PTRADD / collects its output) where the C++ full-width compare REJECTS it (`break`/`continue`). `calc_ptradd_offset` (:656) does the same compare at full `uint8` width, so the truncation is internally inconsistent. (w10-strings ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES, verifier round 1)
- date: 2026-06-15
- kind: (A) faithfulness divergence in a ported gather method — a narrowing `as int4` cast that the C++ does not perform. Distinct from the debug-only wrap class (LOSS-087): here the two truncating sites diverge from C++ for the SAME crafted input in BOTH debug and release (the truncation changes the equality result, not just panic-vs-wrap). Value-identical to C++ across the realistic input domain (element multipliers are small positive constants).
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/constseq.cc:491-492 (`int8 sz = op->getIn(2)->getOffset(); if (sz != ptrAddMult) break;`) and :584 (`if (op->getIn(2)->getOffset() != ptrAddMult) continue;`) — both keep the offset at full `int8`/`uintb` width against the `int4 ptrAddMult`. Contrast :632-633 (`uint8 mult = op->getIn(2)->getOffset(); if (mult != ptrAddMult)`), which the Rust port DOES transcribe at full width.
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-decomp/src/constseq.rs:478-479 (`let sz = vn_get_offset(...) as int4; if sz != self.ptr_add_mult`) and :589 (`if vn_get_offset(...) as int4 != self.ptr_add_mult`). The faithful site is :656 (`if mult != self.ptr_add_mult as uint8`).
- surface: none at the standing gates. `cargo test --workspace` green (0 failed); C++ oracle 207/207 + 675/675 PARITY OK (C++ tree byte-untouched); clippy `-p kuna-decomp --lib` clean. The whole-tree HeapSequence gather is unreachable end-to-end today anyway because `RuleStringStore::transform` is keystone-seamed (declines), and even once wired, a lifted PTRADD's element multiplier is a small constant well within int4 — the high-bit collision is non-physical. The verifier's `w10_f1_find_base_pointer_truncates_mult_diverges_from_cpp` (`#[allow]`-free, passing) pins the current divergent behavior so a future fix is forced to notice it.
- why: round 1 grades this MINOR/non-blocking — value-identical in the realistic input domain, no special-casing, no keystone-file edit, no state mutation (read-only gather), and the gate is fully green. Recorded as a loss rather than a REJECT because the divergence requires a crafted overflowing multiplier that cannot arise from real lifted pointer arithmetic, and the fix is a one-line widening per site.
- restoration criteria: at constseq.rs:478 compare the full-width offset — `let sz = vn_get_offset(data, op_get_in(data, op, 2)); if sz != self.ptr_add_mult as uint8` — and at :589 likewise `if vn_get_offset(...) != self.ptr_add_mult as uint8` (matching the faithful site :656 and the C++ int8/uintb compares). Then flip `w10_f1_find_base_pointer_truncates_mult_diverges_from_cpp` to assert `base_pointer == padd_out` (the back-walk stops at the PTRADD output, as the C++ does). Verified by the w10-strings verifier (round 1).

So the 38-assertion flip is blocked on a one-line edit that is currently in NO landed branch; whichever wave next owns the reserved `dtype.rs` union builder must add it.
