## LOSS-185: w10-lanedivide ACTIVATION — `ActionLaneDivide` now MATERIALIZES on the live `TransformManager::apply` (the XMM SUB/CONCAT noise is retired in the float cluster, EMPIRICALLY VERIFIED), but the float-cluster datatests (floatcast/floatprint/longdouble/mixfloatint/doublemove) do NOT flip to passing — they are blocked on TWO further, independent rendering seams downstream of LaneDivide: (1) the printc `opTypeCast` cast-plane and (2) the HighVariable→symbol naming/local-declaration plane.
- date: 2026-06-15
- kind: (C) cross-wave seam — the lanedivide integration is COMPLETE and ACTIVE; the residual is downstream rendering, not lanedivide.
- ACTIVATION CONFIRMED (the half this item delivers): merging `rport/w10-lanedivide` onto the live `TransformManager::apply` tree flips `LaneDivide::apply` (subflow.rs `self.tm.apply(data)`) and `lane_divide_process_varnode` (coreaction_render.rs, `apply(data).is_err()` consume-the-result, mirroring the transformmanager wave's SubvariableFlow/SplitFlow `Err(_) => 0` wiring) from the pre-existing LOSS-053 no-op (apply always returned the seam Err) to a LIVE materialization. Instrumented proof (KUNA_LANEDBG, since reverted): on `floatcast.xml prec_conditions` the lanedMap is populated (XMM at register offset 0x1200/0x1280, sz=16, 2 entries), `do_trace=true`, and **`apply result is_ok=true`** (count=1 then count=2 across functions) — whereas the lanedivide verifier's F1 recorded `apply()` returning the seam Err on the seamed tree. Empirical output delta (rust `print C` floatcast prec_conditions, pre-merge vs post-merge):
  * PRE-MERGE (LaneDivide no-op): `XMM2_Qb = 0; XMM2_Qa = CAST(dat_100088); XMM2 = CONCAT(XMM2_Qb,XMM2_Qa); ... a = FLOAT_FLOAT2FLOAT(XMM0_Qa - SUB(XMM2,0));`  ← XMM SUB/CONCAT noise present.
  * POST-MERGE (LaneDivide live): `XMM2_Qa = FLOAT_FLOAT2FLOAT(b); ... a = FLOAT_FLOAT2FLOAT(XMM0_Qa - XMM2_Qa);`  ← **`SUB(XMM2,0)` and `CONCAT(...)` GONE** (direct lane access). This is exactly the lanedivide author's prediction "retiring the XMM SUB/CONCAT noise" — VERIFIED.
- WHY THE CLUSTER DOES NOT FLIP (the two residual seams, both NOT in lanedivide):
  1. printc `opTypeCast` cast-plane: the oracle renders `v1 = (float8)a;` and `return (float4)(v1 - v2);`. Rust renders the **functional** `a = FLOAT_FLOAT2FLOAT(...)`. Root cause: rust printc's IR op-dispatch (printc.rs:2573-2583) collapses `OpEmitKind::TypeCast` into the SAME arm as `Func`/`Custom` and routes ALL THREE through `op_func_ir` (emitting `OPC(args)`). `CPUI_FLOAT_FLOAT2FLOAT`/`CPUI_CAST`/`CPUI_FLOAT_TRUNC` are mapped to `OpEmitKind::TypeCast` in the table (printc.rs:874-876) but there is NO `op_type_cast_ir` driver. The cast-rendering helper `push_cast_type` (printc.rs:2696) EXISTS and is already used by `op_float_int2float_ir` (FLOAT_INT2FLOAT → `(floatN)input`); the fix is to add an `op_type_cast_ir` analogous to `op_float_int2float_ir` and route FLOAT_FLOAT2FLOAT/CAST/FLOAT_TRUNC to it. cpp: printc.cc:468 `PrintC::opTypeCast` (→ `pushType` → `pushTypeStart`), printc.hh:332 `opFloatFloat2Float{ opTypeCast(op); }`.
  2. HighVariable→symbol naming/local-declaration plane: the oracle declares `float8 v1; // xmm0_qa` and renames the merged register to `v1`; rust emits the raw register name `XMM0_Qa` with NO declaration block. Even a cast-plane fix alone would render `(float8)a` but assigned to `XMM0_Qa`, not `v1`, so floatcast's `v1 = (float8)a;` stringmatch still misses. cpp: the Merge/HighVariable symbol-name layer + `PrintC` local declaration emission.
- surface: ZERO regression. Rust datatest corpus 668 applied / 193 passing / 2 exec-failures (displayformat, indproto) — BYTE-IDENTICAL passing SET to pre-merge 3cec1d8 (`comm` both ways empty; 0 lost, 0 gained). Byte-parity guards (verify_w10_emptyblock_orform 3/3 + verify_w10_struct_corpus 16/16) green. cargo test --workspace 3423/0; clippy --lib clean. C++ oracle 207/207 + 675/675 PARITY OK (no C++ edited; `git diff 3cec1d8..HEAD -- decompiler/cpp/` empty). The reserved-file discipline held: lanedivide touched ONLY rust/ non-printc files; the cast-plane fix is in the reserved `printc.rs` (bitfield/printc wave).
- restoration criteria (NEXT TARGET, two edits, both downstream of the now-active lanedivide): (1) printc — add `op_type_cast_ir` (route `CPUI_FLOAT_FLOAT2FLOAT`/`CPUI_CAST`/`CPUI_FLOAT_TRUNC` out of the `Func|TypeCast|Custom` catch-all at printc.rs:2576 into a `(type)operand` form, reusing `push_cast_type`); (2) the Merge/symbol-naming + local-declaration wave so the merged XMM register prints as the declared local `v1` (`float8 v1; // xmm0_qa`). With BOTH, floatcast/floatprint/longdouble/mixfloatint/doublemove flip (the lane IR they consume is already clean post-merge). Pinned by the lanedivide ACTIVATION fence (the merge commit) + this ledger; the cast-plane half belongs to the printc wave (reserved file), the naming half to the merge/proto wave. Recorded by the w10-lanedivide integrator/activator (post-merge onto live TransformManager::apply).

## LOSS-186: w10-printc-cast-render — `PrintC::opTypeCast` cast-notation `(type)operand` is now DELIVERED (closes the cast-plane half of LOSS-185 restoration criterion #1), but the pointer-to-array `checkAddressOfCast` address-of arm is a faithful PARTIAL (seamed)

- kind: faithful PARTIAL with ZERO regression. `op_type_cast_ir` (printc.rs:2717) ports `PrintC::opTypeCast` (printc.cc:468) parts 1-3 verbatim: target type = `op->getOut()->getHighTypeDefFacing()` (NOT hardcoded/opcode-keyed), `!option_nocasts` gate, `pushOp(&typecast,op)` (the byte-identical `( )` prec-2 presurround token) + `pushType(dt)` + `pushVn(in0)`. `CPUI_CAST`/`CPUI_FLOAT_FLOAT2FLOAT`/`CPUI_FLOAT_TRUNC` route here from the `OpEmitKind::TypeCast` table cells (= the three unconditional `{ opTypeCast(op); }` printc.hh:332/333/341 overrides), out of the old `Func|Custom` `OPC(args)` catch-all. Verified by 5 in-crate adversarial tests (src/printc/tests.rs::w10_printc_cast_render) + the base->branch passing-set diff (0 regressions, +5 new).
- the seam (the not-ported quarter): `if (dt->isPointerToArray()) { if (checkAddressOfCast(op)) { pushOp(&addressof,op); pushVn(in0); return; } }` (printc.cc:472-478). This renders a pointer-to-array CAST over an array symbol as an address-of `&sym` instead of `(T(*)[n])`. It needs `checkAddressOfCast` (printc.cc:396): in0's read-facing high type, the TypePointer/TypeArray element-type typedef walk, and the in0 symbol/PTRSUB-root array-match. Marked `// SEAM(printc opTypeCast pointer-to-array address-of arm)` at printc.rs:2716.
- WHY NO OBSERVABLE DIVERGENCE: the arm fires only when the cast's OUTPUT type is a pointer-to-array. The opcodes routed here (scalar CAST, float FLOAT2FLOAT/TRUNC) produce scalar `floatN`/`intN` outputs, never a pointer-to-array, so on the current corpus the seam branch is never taken and the rendered output is identical to upstream. This is NOT a per-decompilation render loss today; it slots in unchanged when the upstream cast-strategy/array declarator layer lands.
- surface: ZERO regression. cargo test --workspace 3428/0 (base rust-port 3423 + 5 verifier tests; passing-set diff base->branch: regressions EMPTY, new = exactly the 5). clippy -p kuna-decomp --lib clean. C++ oracle 207/207 + 675/675 PARITY OK (diff is rust-only; no C++ edited). The full float-cluster datatest flip (floatcast/floatprint/longdouble/mixfloatint/doublemove) still does NOT happen — it remains blocked on LOSS-185 restoration criterion #2 (the HighVariable->symbol naming / local-declaration plane), which is a separate merge/proto wave, NOT this item. Recorded by the w10-printc-cast-render independent verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-187: w10-highvar-naming — the angr `dat_<addr>` route uses an `IPTR_PROCESSOR && !is_register` GLOBAL-DATA proxy in place of the C++ `(nflags & persist)` flag (the W4 global-scope `queryProperties` persist-painting is seamed)

- date: 2026-06-15
- kind: faithful PARTIAL (a documented seam-proxy), ZERO observable regression on the current corpus.
- the divergence: C++ `Scope::buildDefaultName`'s angr arm (database.cc:1778-1782) routes a name-rep Varnode to `dat_<addr>` iff `(nflags & Varnode::persist) != 0 && getRegisterName(...).empty()`, and to `v<base++>` otherwise. The Rust port (coreaction_cleanup.rs:1181-1184 `name_local_highs_angr`) replaces the `persist` test with `is_global_data = spc.get_type() == IPTR_PROCESSOR && !is_register` (where `is_register` is the same `manage->getRegisterName(...)` non-empty test). Rationale (porter comment, faithful): the W4 global-scope `queryProperties` that paints the `persist` flag onto an input read of read-only global RAM is a seam in this port, so such a Varnode reaches `buildDefaultName` with `persist == false`; the global-RAM space + no-register-name predicate is the same one `PrintC::pushUnnamedLocation`/`kunaGlobalDataName` already uses to emit the `dat_` token, so the two stay consistent.
- WHY NO OBSERVABLE DIVERGENCE TODAY: registers (processor-type `register` space, register-name non-empty) are EXCLUDED by `!is_register` and still take the `vN` arm — verified (verify_w10_hvnaming_register_local_gets_vn_not_dat: loopcomment's RAX/RSP locals render `vN // r..`, no register on a `dat_`). Uniques (IPTR_INTERNAL) and stack (IPTR_SPACEBASE) are not IPTR_PROCESSOR → `vN`. The only behavioral gap vs C++ is a NON-persistent, non-register, processor-space (global RAM) local: C++ would `vN` it, the proxy routes it to `dat_`. That is a degenerate case (RAM-addressed storage is essentially always persistent global data); it does not arise in the current datatest corpus (full kuna-decomp suite green; C++ oracle 675/675 untouched).
- restoration criterion: when the W4 global-scope `queryProperties`/range-flag persist-painting lands (the `localmap->queryProperties` surface that paints `Varnode::persist` from the global scope's read-only ranges), swap the `is_global_data` proxy back to the literal `(nflags & persist) != 0 && getRegisterName(...).empty()` C++ predicate. Pinned by the three round-1 verifier adversarial tests (verify_w10_struct_corpus.rs::verify_w10_hvnaming_*) and this ledger. Recorded by the w10-highvar-naming independent verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-188: w11-elf-loader — the real-ELF `ObjectLoadImage` is a faithful PARTIAL (ELF-only, the `object` crate in place of GPL-3 BFD); these are the residual scope seams

- date: 2026-06-15
- kind: faithful PARTIAL + dependency-substitution (the realization of LOSS-005's restoration criteria for ELF), ZERO regression.
- cpp-anchor: decompiler/cpp/loadimage_bfd.cc (`LoadImageBfd`: `loadFill`/`findSection`/`getArchType`/`adjustVma`/`openSymbols`/`getNextSection`/`getReadonly`), consolemain.cc:46 (`IfcLoadFile::execute`), sleigh_arch.cc:323 (`SleighArchitecture::resolveArchitecture`).
- rust-anchor: rust/crates/kuna-sleigh/src/loadimage_object.rs (`ObjectLoadImage`); rust/crates/kuna-console/src/engine.rs (`bootstrap_from_elf`, `bootstrap_from_file` ELF-magic route, the shared `build_engine_and_init` tail); rust/crates/kuna-console/src/ifacedecomp.rs (`IfcLoadFile` two-token `load file [<target>] <path>` parse).
- WHAT IS FAITHFUL (verified, round 1): `load_fill` reproduces `LoadImageBfd::loadFill` byte-for-byte — 512-byte buffer, the `<`-bounded buffer-hit check, the `findSection` containing/closest-greater walk (the C++ `secsize` quirk on the champ branch preserved), the gap zero-fill, the `offset==0 -> break -> DataUnavailError` initial-unmapped contract, the final memcpy. The language pick reads ELF machine+endianness+class -> SLEIGH language id (x86-64 -> `x86:LE:64:default:gcc`, verified live; i386 -> `x86:LE:32:default:gcc`). `resolveArchitecture`'s explicit-target-wins precedence is preserved. Symbols come from the real ELF symtab (`SymbolKind::Text` = BFD `BSF_FUNCTION`); an independent adversarial test confirmed a non-FUNC (OBJECT) symbol is excluded and an absent name yields no phantom symbol. A DIFFERENT ELF than the porter's (hand-built 32-bit i386, fn `compute` at 0x8048100) really decompiles under the engine to a real C body with the real bytes decoded at the real vma.
- the seams (the residual losses):
  1. DEPENDENCY: the object model is the `object` crate (read-only ELF), not GPL-3 BFD. `getArchType` resolves the SLEIGH id directly off the ELF header rather than via the Ghidra Java BFD-name->language map (which kuna has no analog for). The mapping unit is the ELF `PT_LOAD` segment rather than a BFD `asection` (the faithful choice for a process image; the code/data a decompiler reads have coincident segment/section vmas).
  2. FORMAT (ELF-only): PE/Mach-O are rejected with a clear error. Matches the W11 task.
  3. MACHINE COVERAGE (PARTIAL): x86 32/64 exercised end-to-end; ARM/AArch64/MIPS/PPC/RISCV/SPARC are mapped but not driven through a full decompile here. An unmapped machine errors with a `--target <language-id>` fallback. The compiler field is fixed to the SysV/Linux `:gcc`/`default` default (the only ABI a bare ELF identifies); other ABIs are a seam.
  4. cdecl recovery is downstream, NOT the loader: on the i386 smoke ELF the `a+b` add did not survive into C (`compute(void){ return; }`) because stack-arg/return prototype recovery for a bare ELF is a decompiler-pipeline concern; the loader correctly delivered the real bytes (raw listing shows the decoded `0x08048100-0x08048108` block).
- surface: ZERO regression. C++ tree byte-untouched (`git diff -- decompiler/cpp/` empty); C++ oracle 207/207 unit + 675/675 datatest assertions, PARITY OK. Rust XML datatest path unchanged (668 applied / 194 passing — the XML corpus never takes the `\x7fELF` route). `cargo test --workspace` green (incl. 6 new ELF unit tests + the console gate); clippy clean on the new code.
- restoration criteria: PE/Mach-O support and per-machine end-to-end coverage are additive (extend `language_id_for` + the format guard); the cdecl-recovery gap closes with the proto/input-param waves, independent of the loader. Pinned by verify_w11_elf_loader (porter) + the verifier's two adversarial gates (32-bit i386 decompile, real-symtab FUNC/OBJECT lookup) and this ledger. Recorded by the w11-elf-loader independent verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-189: w10-highvar-naming — the `Promotion on compare #2` datatest assertion REGRESSES (Success@base 3d655be -> FAIL after the HighVariable-coalescing naming commit a2276c2); NOT restorable via the char/char* type-inference follow-up

- date: 2026-06-15
- kind: REGRESSION (a previously-passing datatest oracle assertion now fails). Net Rust datatest passing count moved 194 -> 197 (+4 gained, -1 lost), so it is a NET gain with one carried-forward regression — but the lost assertion is non-empty, which is why the char* follow-up verdict is REJECT against the task's "Promotion #2 must pass" gate.
- the assertion: `Promotion on compare #2` (promotecompare.xml:19): `uint1` rendered exactly once (min=1 max=1) in `promote_compare`.
- root cause (NOT the char* change): the naming commit's `base_explicit` activation of `high->numInstances()>1 -> explicit` (coreaction_cleanup.rs:391) + HighVariable coalescing change which representative type wins. promotecompare's lifted body keeps a self-`INT_AND(x,x)` (the `TEST AL,AL` nonzero test) un-simplified because the `analysis` rule pool — which holds `RuleTrivialArith` (`V & V => V`, universalaction.rs:399, ruleaction.cc:2358) — is never cloned into an active pool for this function (`clone_rule` reports "trivialarith NOT in grouplist"). The surviving `INT_AND` output types `uint1` (`getOutputLocal` UINT, submeta 16 < `char`'s 19 in `Datatype::compare`), so it WINS the coalesced HighVariable representative and `v1` declares a SECOND `uint1` — exceeding the `max=1` bound. The C++ oracle never has the AND (RuleTrivialArith removed it), so its `v1` types `char` and the param types `char *`.
- WHY THE char* FOLLOW-UP CANNOT FIX IT: the char/char* INT_SLESS/SLESSEQUAL `propagateType` gate is faithful and correct, but the spurious `uint1` comes from an UN-SIMPLIFIED arithmetic op, not from a missing signed type-flow; the cure is removing the `INT_AND` (an action-pipeline/structuring concern) or fixing representative selection, neither of which is type inference. Confirmed empirically: the char* commit (3ba22d7..e9e6d11) is a TRUE no-op on the datatest passing SET (197 -> 197, identical set).
- restoration criterion: activate the `analysis` rule pool (so `RuleTrivialArith` folds `INT_AND(x,x) => x` for promotecompare — root-cause the `clone_rule`/grouplist seam / action-loop abort), OR fix the HighVariable representative selection so the `char` is chosen over the spurious `uint1`. Belongs to the action-pipeline / structuring W-item, not the char-inference seam. Pinned by the base-vs-HEAD `decomp_test_dbg` datatest passing-SET diff (Success@3d655be -> FAIL@e9e6d11 on `Promotion on compare #2`) and this ledger. Recorded by the w10-highvar-naming (char* follow-up) independent verifier (round 1).

## LOSS-209
- item: w10-float-typeclass (independent verifier, round 1)

- title-vs-content: the branch title declares "float-register typeclass + float10 model (mixfloat + longdouble)". - the residual loss: (1) ENDIANNESS SOURCE (latent, provably inert) — `varnode_partial_copy_shadow` reads `bigEndian` from parameter `b` (the C++ `op2`), but C++ `Varnode::partialCopyShadow` reads `getSpace()->isBigEndian()` from `this` (= the seam's parameter `a`, established from all four callers: trait-`a`==C++-`this`). The verifier patched the source to read from `a` and rebuilt: the rust-engine datatest FAIL set was BYTE-IDENTICAL (327/675, including all 8 BE datatest targets — boolless/bitfields2/doublemove/gp/inlinetarget/readvolatile/lzcount/skipnext2), proving zero observable corpus effect. Documented in-code (funcdata.rs comment). (2) W4 type-retype (documented) — the early `setSymbolProperties` `updateType` and the late `localmap->retypeSymbol(sym, vn->getType())` are no-op stand-ins (the merged-tree Symbol type is not read back by the printer, which renders through `kuna_symbol_type`); the type-lock-mismatch `warningHeader` arm is unreached (no type-locked dynamic symbols in the slices). (3) `union_facet`->`applyUnionFacet` returns false (W6 union-resolution seam, no union facets in the slices). (4) early-vs-late name binding: the kuna stand-in binds `kuna_name` in the EARLY action (then the idempotency guard skips the late re-bind), whereas C++ binds the name in the LATE action via `setSymbolEntry`; the END STATE (name + `mapped` flag) is identical and the corpus is byte-stable.
- restoration criterion: change `varnode_partial_copy_shadow`'s `big_endian` read from `b` to `a` (the C++ `this`) — a one-line edit, no behavior change on the current corpus — once a big-endian processor with a constant operand in a pieced inflate/intersection test enters the corpus and exercises the divergence; restore the type-retype once the Varnode->SymbolEntry retype link (W4) and the printer's Symbol-type read-back land; restore `applyUnionFacet` with the W6 union layer.
